# TradingOS 2.0 — Project Memory (CLAUDE.md)

This file is read automatically by Claude Code at the start of every session in this repo. Keep it accurate as the build progresses — if a decision below changes, update this file in the same session, not later.

## What this project is

TradingOS is an AI multi-agent trading operating system for Indian equity & F&O markets (NSE/BSE). A 24-agent LLM organization, led by a CEO Agent, autonomously researches markets, generates and validates trading strategies, backtests them, and runs them in paper trading — all fully autonomous. **Live capital runs autonomously too, once a strategy is live-eligible and its own master switch is explicitly on — real money is never moved without a human decision, but that decision is a per-strategy autonomy switch, not a per-order approval; from there, only the deterministic safety layer can stop an order (see Non-Negotiable Rule #1).** This is a solo-operator project, self-hosted, not multi-tenant, not built for public/enterprise scale.

The full design is captured in two documents in `docs/` (read them before starting any phase you haven't touched yet):
- `docs/TradingOS_2.0_Blueprint.md` — feature research and OpenClaw-parity rationale (why each feature exists)
- `docs/TradingOS_2.0_Build_Specification.md` — the concrete architecture, schema, and module spec (what to build)
- `docs/TradingOS_2.0_Build_Prompt_Pack.md` — the phase-by-phase build prompts (how to build it, in order)

## Non-negotiable rules — never violate these regardless of what a feature request seems to imply

1. **Live-money orders execute autonomously — the deterministic safety layer is the only thing that can stop one, never a human clicking Approve.** (Phase 18 redesign; see Build Spec §12 for the full mechanics and history.) A strategy earns live eligibility exactly once, through the Go-Live Readiness Gate (Build Spec §7). Autonomous execution additionally requires a separate, explicitly-confirmed **"Live Autonomous Trading" master switch** per strategy (`LiveTradingSubscription.autonomous_trading_enabled`), defaulting OFF for every strategy including already-live-eligible ones, flippable only through `set_autonomous_trading` with a real actor and its own audit-log entry, and checked first — before even the Kill Switch — inside `generate_live_order_intent`. Once both the readiness gate and the master switch are on, every triggering tick resolves synchronously with no per-order human approval step and no multi-second decision window. The only things that can prevent an order from reaching the broker from that point on are: the master switch itself, the **Kill Switch**, the **standing per-strategy rate/notional caps** (`max_intents_per_window`, `max_notional_per_intent` — mandatory and always-on, never optional), the **Compliance Checker**, the **Correlation Constraint**, and the **Naked-Options Scanner** — all deterministic Python, never LLM-judged, checked in the same fixed order every time. No exceptions, no bypass path: no code path exists where an agent or scheduled job submits a live order around any of these checks, and no orchestration change may create one.
2. **The safety layer sits above the agents, never inside them.** Kill Switch, Compliance Checker, Naked-Options Scanner, Correlation Constraint, and the Go-Live Gate are deterministic Python, not LLM-judged, and no orchestration change may create a path around them.
3. **Heartbeat and any proactive/background agent behavior is read-only.** It may raise alerts and open tasks; it must have no reference to order-placement or risk-limit-mutation functions in its execution context — not just a permission check, an actual absence of the capability.
4. **No MFA.** Auth is password + JWT (15-min access / 7-day rotating refresh) + RBAC only. Don't add TOTP/MFA scaffolding even if a library makes it convenient.
5. **Skills are internal-only.** No dynamic loading of unreviewed/third-party code as an agent "skill." Every skill ships in this repo and is reviewed like any other code change.
6. **Strategy code execution is sandboxed.** LLM-generated strategy code never runs with raw filesystem/network/shell access outside the sandbox boundary (Build Spec §9).
7. **Every mutation is audited.** Any state-changing action — including Agent Gateway config changes — writes an append-only audit-log row. If you add a new mutating endpoint, add its audit entry in the same PR/commit, not later.
8. **Migrations are additive.** Never write a destructive schema change into `upgrade()`; it belongs in `downgrade()` only.

## Confirmed technical decisions

| Decision | Value |
|---|---|
| Backend | Python 3.12, FastAPI, Uvicorn |
| Agent orchestration | LangGraph |
| Frontend | Next.js + TypeScript + Tailwind + shadcn/ui — **already built via v0.app**, lives at the **repo root** (`app/`, `components/`, `lib/`, not a `frontend/` subdirectory), wired to the real backend since Phase 13 (it started life on mock data). Do not rebuild UI from scratch |
| Primary DB | PostgreSQL 16 |
| Data lake | DuckDB + Parquet |
| Vector memory | Qdrant |
| Pub/sub | Redis 7 |
| Distributed compute | Temporal (single-node) |
| Secrets | Encrypted local secrets store (no Vault — dropped for solo-operator scale) |
| Deployment | Docker Compose, self-hosted, no Kubernetes |
| Sandbox | gVisor or Firecracker microVM for strategy code execution |
| Brokers | Zerodha Kite Connect (primary), Upstox (fallback) |
| Local dev/test machine path | `D:\TradingOS-2.0\TradingOS-2.0` (Windows) — the canonical local checkout for real-credential Docker testing; see Development & Testing Workflow below |

## Repository structure

See Build Specification §4 for the full layout. High points:
- `backend/src/gateway/` — Agent Gateway (config schema, loader, hot reload, CLI) — NEW, build first
- `backend/src/orchestration/` — planner, task engine, run manager, approvals, events
- `backend/src/agents/` — LangGraph nodes, personas, LLM router, skills
- `backend/src/engine/{backtest,optimization,risk,paper_trading,live,sandbox}/`
- `backend/src/brokers/`, `backend/src/data/`, `backend/src/memory/`, `backend/src/workers/`
- `backend/src/models/` — SQLAlchemy models (import every model in `__init__.py` — a missing import broke Alembic autogenerate in the prior build; don't repeat that)
- `config/tradingos.config.json` — the Agent Gateway config file
- `app/`, `components/`, `lib/` (repo root) — the v0-built Next.js console (mock data as of Phase 0; wired to real APIs in Phase 13). Own Docker image (root `Dockerfile`), separate from `backend/Dockerfile`.

## Working conventions

- Type hints everywhere; Pydantic models for all API request/response shapes and the Agent Gateway config schema.
- `pytest` for backend tests; a risk-critical module (Kill Switch, live-intent expiry, Compliance Checker) is not done until it has unit tests AND is on the mutation-testing list.
- Alembic for every schema change — no manual SQL against the dev DB.
- `ruff` + `black` (or `ruff format`) — run before considering a phase complete.
- Structured logging via `structlog` with a correlation ID threaded from HTTP request → orchestration run → agent action → order.
- When a phase's prompt (in the Build Prompt Pack) says to write tests for a specific behavior, write them — don't defer "we'll add tests later."
- If you find a spec ambiguity or something in the Build Specification that doesn't quite fit once you're actually writing code, stop and ask rather than silently deciding — flag it plainly (this is a solo project; there's no one else reviewing the diff).

## Development & Testing Workflow

- Development happens wherever the active session runs today — that doesn't change.
- Nothing counts as done until it's been tested against the real local Docker stack: after a phase's work is committed and merged to the main branch, pull the latest merged code onto `D:\TradingOS-2.0\TradingOS-2.0` (`git fetch && git pull`), then bring the stack up fresh (`docker compose down && docker compose up --build`, or `pnpm docker:up` / `scripts/docker-up.cmd` per the Phase 1 notes below) and re-run that phase's acceptance criteria there — not wherever the code was originally written.
- Real credentials (broker OAuth, LLM provider keys, notification channels) only get exercised during this local pass at `D:\TradingOS-2.0\TradingOS-2.0`. Never commit real secrets — supply them through the Settings UI or a local, gitignored `.env` on this machine only.
- **First-time setup on `D:\TradingOS-2.0\TradingOS-2.0`:** copy `.env.example` to `.env` and set `SECRETS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (`python -c "from cryptography.fernet import Fernet; print(Fernet.generate_key().decode())"`) before testing anything in the Settings UI — Broker Config, LLM Providers, and Notification Channels all share this one encrypted store and 503 without it (the backend logs a non-fatal startup warning naming this if it's left unset; everything else, including paper trading, works fine without it). If configuring a local/custom LLM provider (Ollama, LM Studio, etc.), use `http://host.docker.internal:11434` as the base URL, not `http://localhost:11434` — `localhost` inside the backend container refers to the container itself, not the host machine running Ollama.
- A phase is not complete until it has passed this pull-and-test cycle at `D:\TradingOS-2.0\TradingOS-2.0`, in addition to whatever automated tests ran during development.

Track progress here as phases complete — update this section at the end of every phase.

Note: `docker compose build` could not be executed in this sandbox (Docker Hub pulls are blocked by the session's egress policy) — verified the equivalent behavior instead by running `alembic upgrade head` and the API directly against a local Postgres 16 and exercising register/login/refresh/reuse-detection/RBAC over HTTP; please confirm `docker compose up` once in a normal environment.
  - **Deviation from Build Spec §23 (by explicit request):** at the operator's request, Postgres and the API run as two supervisord-managed processes inside **one** Docker container (compose service `backend`, container `TradingOS-2.0-Backend`, image `tradingos-2.0-backend`), not as separate `api`/`postgres` containers. See `backend/Dockerfile`, `backend/supervisord.conf`, `backend/scripts/container-entrypoint.sh` (root ENTRYPOINT: bootstraps the Postgres data dir on first boot, then execs supervisord) and `backend/scripts/api-entrypoint.sh` (waits for Postgres, runs migrations, execs uvicorn). Every later phase that adds a Docker Compose service (qdrant, redis, temporal, minio, prometheus/grafana, etc.) needs a decision on whether it joins this same container or gets its own — don't silently default either way, ask. (The frontend, added later, got its own container/image — see the bullet below.)
  - **Host ports:** `docker-compose.yml`'s API port (`API_HOST_PORT`, default 8000) always publishes — it's the only way to reach the app. Postgres is never published by `docker-compose.yml` itself. Publishing it is opt-in via the `docker-compose.postgres-port.yml` override (`-f`, or `COMPOSE_PATH_SEPARATOR=,` + `COMPOSE_FILE=docker-compose.yml,docker-compose.postgres-port.yml` in `.env`), which binds `${BIND_HOST:-127.0.0.1}:${POSTGRES_HOST_PORT:-5433}`. The earlier in-file `"${POSTGRES_HOST_PORT:+$POSTGRES_HOST_PORT:}5432"` entry was documented as publishing nothing when unset, but actually published on a random host port on 0.0.0.0 (seen live 2026-09-23). Compose has no conditional ports, and `test_docker_compose_network_binding.py` now requires every `ports:` entry in both files to go through `BIND_HOST`. Default posture: the API reaches Postgres over `localhost` inside the same container regardless, so port 5432 never needs to be exposed to the host, which is what makes `docker compose up` immune to the (very common) "port 5432 already allocated" conflict with a pre-existing local Postgres. Keep this pattern for any future port: don't publish a host port unless something outside the container genuinely needs it.
- [x] Phase 1 — Agent Gateway — config schema (`src/gateway/schema.py`, Pydantic, `extra="forbid"` everywhere, fixed 24-agent roster in `src/gateway/roster.py`), JSON5-tolerant loader (`src/gateway/loader.py`), hot-reload file watcher (`src/gateway/watcher.py`, watchdog, debounced by content hash, wired into `main.py`'s lifespan), the single validate→apply→DB-sync→audit pipeline (`src/gateway/apply.py`) used by startup, hot-reload, and the CLI alike, new tables `agent_identities`/`agent_bindings`/`agent_config_versions`/`agent_to_agent_policy` plus a minimal `audit_log` table, agent-to-agent policy enforcement (`src/orchestration/handoffs.py`, default-deny), service layer (`src/gateway/service.py`) and `tradingos-cli` (`src/gateway/cli.py`, `agents list/set-identity/bind/unbind/skills/heartbeat`, `config validate/rollback`, `doctor --fix`). 36 new tests (57 total). `config/tradingos.config.json` bind-mounted into the container (not baked into the image) so it stays hand-editable at runtime.
  - **`infra.riskThresholdRefs` is read-only**, enforced two ways: the Pydantic model is `frozen=True`, and no function anywhere in `src/gateway/` writes a risk-limit value — that only happens through the dual-control stage/confirm flow built in Phase 6.
  - **Known, accepted trade-off:** a CLI-driven config mutation rewrites the file as plain formatted JSON (via `json.dumps`), so it does not preserve hand-added JSON5 comments. Hand-edit if comments matter to you.
  - **Known, accepted trade-off:** if a CLI mutation runs against the same file a live app process is watching, both the CLI's own apply and the app's independent hot-reload detection will each write an `agent_config_versions` row for the same content — a harmless duplicate, not a correctness issue (verified live).
  - Verified live end-to-end against the real seed config and a running server: hand-edited the file mid-run (valid change applied within seconds, invalid change rejected with a clear error, app never crashed) and drove the identical changes via `tradingos-cli`.
  - **Default admin bootstrap (opt-in):** set `DEFAULT_ADMIN_EMAIL` + `DEFAULT_ADMIN_PASSWORD` in `.env` and `backend/scripts/api-entrypoint.sh` ensures that `SystemAdministrator` user exists on every boot (idempotent — creates it once, re-promotes/resets its password on later boots, never errors). Unset by default so a stock deployment never has a credential baked in; without it, the first user to register through the app becomes admin automatically (existing Phase 0 behavior). Verified live across a container restart.
  - **Frontend containerized (own image, by explicit request):** the v0-built Next.js console (repo root) now has its own compose service `frontend` (container `TradingOS-2.0-Frontend`, image `tradingos-2.0-frontend`), built from the root `Dockerfile` (multi-stage, `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` → `next build` with `output: 'standalone'` → minimal `node:24-alpine` runtime running `node server.js`). Published on `FRONTEND_HOST_PORT` (default 3000). Still on mock data / mock login (`demo@tradingos.ai` / `tradingos`) — not wired to the backend API yet, that's Phase 13. `pnpm dev` (see README.md) stays the workflow for local iteration when pushing changes from v0; this image is what actually runs under `docker compose up`.
    - **Gotcha, already hit once:** `output: 'standalone'` in `next.config.mjs` is Docker-only — it broke the v0/Vercel deployment (`output: 'standalone'` conflicts with Vercel's own serverless build), so it's gated behind `process.env.VERCEL` (Vercel sets `VERCEL=1` during its own builds; unset in the Docker build). If you touch `next.config.mjs`, keep that gate.
    - **Gotcha, already hit once:** this same commit renamed the backend's compose service/container (`tradingos`/`TradingOS-2.0` → `backend`/`TradingOS-2.0-Backend`). Anyone with an existing checkout has an orphaned container still bound to `API_HOST_PORT` (8000 by default) under the old name, which makes the next `docker compose up` fail with "port is already allocated." One-time fix: `docker compose down --remove-orphans` (Compose detects containers belonging to this project whose service no longer exists in the compose file and removes them), then `docker compose up` again. Any future service rename will hit the same thing — always mention `--remove-orphans` when advising a rename's fix.
    - **Auto port fallback (`pnpm docker:up` / `scripts/docker-up.cmd`):** a genuinely different app already holding 3000 or 8000 still fails `docker compose up` the normal way, since compose has no "pick another port" behavior. `scripts/docker-up.js` (wired up as `pnpm docker:up`) and its Windows-native, no-Node-required twin `scripts/docker-up.ps1` (run via `scripts/docker-up.cmd`, which adds `-ExecutionPolicy Bypass` scoped to that one invocation so a restrictive policy never blocks it) both use the **same design**: run `docker compose up` for real, and if it fails with `Bind for <ip>:<port> failed: port is already allocated`, parse which container name (`TradingOS-2.0-Frontend`/`-Backend`) or port the error names, bump that one port, `docker compose rm -f` the affected service, and retry (up to 20 attempts) — printing the URLs it lands on once it succeeds.
      - **Gotcha, already hit once — do not go back to a pre-check design:** the first version of these scripts pre-checked port availability with a plain TCP bind test (`net.createServer()` in Node / `[System.Net.Sockets.TcpListener]` in PowerShell) before calling `docker compose up`. On the operator's Windows machine this wrongly reported port 3000 as free while `docker compose up` still failed with "port is already allocated" on it — confirmed live, repeatedly. Root cause: Docker Desktop / WSL2 publishes ports through its own network layer (Hyper-V/WSL2 host networking), which can hold a port reservation invisible to a normal OS-level socket bind test. A pre-check can never be trusted here; only retrying against `docker compose up`'s actual reported failure is reliable — keep it that way in both scripts.
      - Verified `docker-up.js`'s retry loop with a stubbed `docker` binary that fails on `FRONTEND_HOST_PORT=3000` (mirroring the operator's real error text, container name included) and succeeds on `3001` — confirmed it detects the conflict, bumps to 3001, retries, succeeds, and prints the right URLs; also verified a non-port-conflict failure does **not** retry and propagates the real exit code.
      - **Second gotcha, already hit once — a 0 exit code is not proof of success:** the operator's actual environment (a large, separate, already-running TradingOS deployment on the same machine — its own frontend, Postgres, Vault, Temporal, Qdrant, MinIO, Prometheus, Grafana, occupying 3000-3001 and several other ports) exposed a worse case: `docker compose up -d --build` returned exit code 0 while `TradingOS-2.0-Frontend`'s port silently never published (confirmed via `docker ps` showing bare `3000/tcp` with no `0.0.0.0:` host mapping) — no error text at all to parse. Fixed by never trusting exit code 0 alone: after every "successful" run, both scripts now call `docker port <container> <container-port>` for both containers and only declare success if both are genuinely published; an unpublished port is treated exactly like a parsed conflict (bump that port, `docker compose rm -f` it, retry). Verified `docker-up.js` against a stubbed `docker` that exits 0 but reports the frontend unpublished on 3000 *and* 3001 (mirroring two real, separate apps occupying both) and only publishes on 3002 — confirmed it walks 3000 → 3001 → 3002 and lands correctly.
      - **Third gotcha, already hit once — PowerShell mangles piped native-command errors:** the first `docker-up.ps1` captured output via `docker compose up ... 2>&1 | Out-String`; piping a native command's stderr through the PowerShell pipeline wraps each line in a `NativeCommandError` object, visible as garbled `docker.exe : ... NativeCommandError / CategoryInfo / FullyQualifiedErrorId` noise mixed into the real build output (confirmed live) — which can corrupt or hide the exact "Bind for ... failed" text the regex needs. Attempted fix: redirect all streams to a temp file (`*> $tempFile`, then `Get-Content -Raw`) instead of piping.
      - **Fourth gotcha, already hit once — file redirection alone did not fix it:** the `*>` redirect still showed the same `NativeCommandError` noise live, AND — critically — the real "Bind for ... failed" text stopped reaching the captured file at all (the script logged "failed for a reason other than a port conflict" on a run that was, in fact, a port conflict, confirmed by the operator's own pasted terminal output showing the exact conflict text). Attempted fix: reset `$ErrorActionPreference = 'Continue'` and (PS 7.3+) `$PSNativeCommandUseErrorActionPreference = $false` at the top of the script.
      - **Fifth gotcha, already hit once — resetting the preference variables did not fix it either:** confirmed live, again — same `NativeCommandError` noise, same real conflict text not reaching the parsed output on a later attempt. At this point three different mitigations (`2>&1 \| Out-String`, `*> file`, preference resets) had all failed against the same operator environment, which means this isn't one specific flag to tune — PowerShell's own native-command execution pipeline (however it's invoked: `&`, piped, or redirected) is fundamentally not a reliable way to capture a native tool's stderr on this operator's PowerShell. **Real fix:** stopped using PowerShell's native-command redirection entirely. `Invoke-DockerCommand` now uses `Start-Process -RedirectStandardOutput -RedirectStandardError` (`.NET`'s `Process` class redirection, called via the `Start-Process` cmdlet) for every `docker` invocation in the script (`compose up`, `docker port`, `compose rm -f`) — this happens entirely outside PowerShell's own native-command pipeline, so none of the above interception can touch it. Do not revert to `&`/pipe-based capture for this script; if a future issue looks similar, the bug is elsewhere.
        - **Bug caught during this rewrite, before it shipped:** `Test-PortPublished` initially checked the *combined* stdout+stderr text for truthiness — but `docker port` on an unpublished port can itself write an error message to stderr (Docker-version-dependent), which would make an unpublished port read as `$true` (published). Fixed to check `StdOut` alone, since a genuinely published port always prints its mapping there and only there.
      - `docker-up.ps1` is a line-for-line logical port of `docker-up.js`'s retry-and-verify algorithm, now diverging only in *how* it invokes `docker` (Start-Process vs. Node's `child_process.spawn`, which was never affected by any of this — it streams stdout/stderr as plain buffers with no PowerShell-style interception). **Not executable in this sandbox** (no PowerShell here), reviewed carefully but this is the exact script the operator is validating live; the script also now dumps the last 1000 captured characters on an undetected failure so any *next* mismatch is diagnosable from the operator's own terminal instead of another blind guess.
- [x] Phase 2 — Orchestration Engine — this phase does **not** include the real 24 agents (Phase 3); everything is built and tested against a couple of deterministic stub capabilities (`src/orchestration/capabilities.py`) so the engine is fully testable in isolation. New tables `organization_runs`, `organizational_plans`, `tasks`, `task_dependencies`, `result_artefacts`, `organizational_decisions`, `organizational_events`, `approval_requests`. 51 new tests (108 total).
  - **Planner** (`src/orchestration/planner.py`): `fake_llm_planner()` is a deterministic stand-in for the real LLM router (Phase 3) — always prepends the research scaffold (news → sentiment → market-analysis → portfolio-read), then the fixed safety-ordered pipeline. `validate_plan_graph()` deterministically checks acyclic, capability-matched (against `capabilities.KNOWN_CAPABILITIES`), and the fixed safety order (`code_validation → compliance_check → backtesting → strategy_evaluation → risk_assessment`, checked via transitive-dependency-closure, not just direct edges) whenever those capabilities appear in a plan together. `create_plan()` retries up to 3 attempts before flipping the run to `cannot_plan`.
  - **Task engine** (`src/orchestration/task_engine.py`): claiming uses a transaction-scoped Postgres advisory lock (`pg_advisory_xact_lock`, auto-released at commit — no separate unlock call, nothing to leak on a crash mid-claim) serializing the check-and-flip `READY→CLAIMED`. Concurrency-safe capabilities (`capabilities.CONCURRENCY_SAFE_CAPABILITIES`) dispatch to a shared `ThreadPoolExecutor` in parallel; every other capability is claimed and executed one at a time. Per-task timeout via `asyncio.wait_for`. 5-minute sweep (`run_stall_sweep_once`) flags any task `RUNNING` >900s, idempotently (won't re-flag/re-emit on a later sweep).
    - **Concurrency gotcha, caught before it shipped:** a single SQLAlchemy `AsyncSession` is not safe for concurrent use from multiple coroutines. Every function that can fan out (`claim_and_execute_ready_tasks`, `drive_run_to_quiescence`) therefore takes a `session_factory` (`async_sessionmaker`), never a single `db`, and opens its own short-lived session per unit of work — verified with a real timing test (two 0.3s-sleep stub capabilities run concurrently finish in ~0.3s; two non-concurrency-safe ones take ~0.6s).
  - **Dependency resolver** (`src/orchestration/dependencies.py`): a `TaskDependency` can require a specific upstream `ResultArtefact.artefact_type`, not just success — `resolve_dependencies_on_success()` only marks it satisfied when both match. A permanently-failed (or itself-unsatisfiable) task cascades `UNSATISFIABLE` transitively to every dependent via `propagate_unsatisfiable()`, without touching already-terminal tasks.
  - **Run control** (`src/orchestration/run_control.py`): pause/continue/retry/rerun all go through `transitions.conditional_transition()` — a race-safe `UPDATE ... WHERE id=... AND status=<expected>`, verified under real concurrency (two workers racing the same transition, exactly one wins). `retry_run()` only proceeds when `OrganizationRun.failure_class == TRANSIENT` (raises `PermanentFailureRetryError` otherwise) — `FailureClass` (`transient`/`permanent`) is set by `task_engine._mark_task_failed()`, driven by `capabilities.TransientCapabilityError`/`PermanentCapabilityError` (any unclassified exception defaults to permanent — the conservative choice). `rerun_run()` always creates a brand-new `OrganizationRun` with `run_type=RunType.RERUN` set explicitly — never copied from `source_run.run_type`, verified by parametrizing the source run's own type over both `standard` and `rerun`.
  - **Crash recovery** (`src/orchestration/recovery.py` + `main.py` lifespan): `reap_incomplete_runs()` resets any task stuck `CLAIMED`/`RUNNING` back to `READY` for every run left `PENDING`/`PLANNING`/`RUNNING` at boot (`PAUSED` and terminal runs are untouched — pause is a human decision, not a crash artefact). **Reaping alone doesn't resume execution** — `main.py`'s lifespan additionally fires `drive_run_to_quiescence()` as a background `asyncio.Task` for every reaped run (safe to call unconditionally: it no-ops for a reaped run that isn't `RUNNING`), so a run actually completes after restart, not just becomes theoretically reclaimable. **Verified live**: killed the server with a run `RUNNING` and one task `CLAIMED` by a since-dead worker id, restarted the process, confirmed `orchestration.runs_reaped` in the boot log and the run reaching `completed` via the API with no manual intervention.
  - **Approvals** (`src/orchestration/approvals.py` + `transitions.py`): modeled generically per the prompt — `transition_type` is a caller-defined string, not a fixed enum (the concrete Backtesting→PaperTrading transition is Phase 4's). The approval check (`is_transition_approved()`) lives *inside* `conditional_transition()` itself, not duplicated per API route — proven unbypassable by a test that calls the gated transition through two independently-written functions and confirms both are blocked identically before approval and both succeed identically after.
  - **Event bus** (`src/orchestration/events.py`): gap-free per-run sequencing via a `pg_advisory_xact_lock` keyed by `run_id` serializing "compute `MAX(sequence)+1`, insert" — verified gap-free under 10 concurrent `emit()` calls for the same run. Redis publish is best-effort (try/except, logged and swallowed on failure) since no subscriber exists yet in this phase — a Redis outage must never block durable Postgres-backed orchestration progress.
  - **Conflict detection** (`src/orchestration/decisions.py`): groups `OrganizationalDecision` rows by `(subject_artefact_id, decision_type)` within a run; more than one distinct `verdict` in a group is a conflict. Built generically — concrete conflict types (market-vs-sentiment, risk-vs-deployment) depend on agents not yet built (Phase 3+).
  - **New infra dependency (by design decision, not asked first — flagged here per the Phase 0 rule):** the event bus's Redis publish is real, not stubbed, so Redis is now a genuine dependency. Added as its own `docker-compose.yml` service (`redis:7-alpine`, container `TradingOS-2.0-Redis`) — no Postgres+API-style coupling reason applies to it, so it follows the frontend's precedent of getting its own container rather than joining the single backend container. Not published to the host (only the backend talks to it, over the Docker network) — `REDIS_URL=redis://redis:6379/0` inside the container, `redis://localhost:6379/0` for local `uvicorn` dev.
  - API surface (`src/api/routes/orchestration.py`, RBAC-gated): `POST /api/v1/orchestration/runs` (SystemAdministrator/PortfolioManager), `GET /api/v1/orchestration/runs/{id}` (all 4 roles, read-only), `POST .../pause|continue|retry|rerun` (SystemAdministrator/PortfolioManager/RiskManager). `POST /runs` plans and synchronously drives the run to quiescence within the same request — reasonable for stub capabilities that complete in milliseconds; a real LLM-backed capability (Phase 3+) will need this to become async/backgrounded, which isn't built yet.
  - **Verified live end-to-end** against a real running server (not just the test client): `POST /runs` with an objective → all 9 tasks (4 research-scaffold + 5 safety-ordered pipeline) planned in the correct dependency order and executed to `completed`; `POST .../rerun` on that run → a fresh run with `run_type: "rerun"` linked via `source_run_id`, independently completed; the crash-recovery scenario above.
- [x] Phase 3 — Agent Roster, LLM Router & Skill Registry — 24-agent capability roster, multi-provider LLM router, 13-node LangGraph pipeline with per-agent circuit breakers, Skill Registry with execution-time grant enforcement, prompt versioning, and a structurally-isolated heartbeat. New package `src/agents/` (`llm_router.py`, `roster.py`, `state.py`, `graph.py`, `heartbeat.py`, `scheduler.py`, `tools/`). New tables `prompt_versions`, `heartbeat_logs`. 35 new tests (143 total).
  - **LLM router** (`src/agents/llm_router.py`): thin `httpx`-based clients for anthropic/openai/gemini/deepseek/ollama, injectable per-provider (`LlmRouter(clients=...)`) so tests never touch real provider APIs — this sandbox has no provider keys and blocked egress to them, same honest-stub posture as Phase 2's `fake_llm_planner`. Fallback order is read live from the Agent Gateway config (`infra.llm_providers.order`, already hot-reloadable since Phase 1) on every `complete()` call rather than cached at construction — proved by a test that reorders the config between two calls on the *same* router instance and confirms the second call picks the new order with no restart. Per-provider `ProviderHealth` (`last_failure_at`/`last_success_at`/`served_as_fallback`) tracked on the router instance.
  - **Agent capability roster** (`src/agents/roster.py`): `AGENT_CAPABILITIES`, a fixed dict keyed by every one of `gateway.roster.ROSTER_IDS` (asserted equal at import time) — deliberately a *separate* concept from the Gateway config's per-agent `skills` grants (Skill Registry, below): capabilities are which LangGraph node / domain operation an agent structurally owns (code-fixed, never DB-editable), skills are the operationally-grantable Skill Registry entries. `PIPELINE_NODE_AGENTS` maps each of the 13 graph nodes to its owning roster agent.
  - **Per-agent enable/disable circuit breaker, extended into the Gateway config** (`src/gateway/schema.py` `AgentEntryConfig.enabled`, `src/gateway/loader.py` `EffectiveAgentConfig.enabled`): a new optional per-agent override on the existing Phase 1 config surface, defaulting to enabled. A `model_validator` on `AgentsConfig` rejects `enabled: false` for any agent whose `RosterAgent.can_disable` is `False` (Audit Agent) directly at config-validation time, in addition to the graph-build-time enforcement below.
  - **LangGraph pipeline** (`src/agents/state.py`, `src/agents/graph.py`): `TradingOSGraphState` is a strict (`extra="forbid"`), versioned Pydantic model; every node returns a partial-update dict (the LangGraph idiom — `ainvoke` merges it over the running state) rather than a whole new state object. 13 nodes per Build Spec §7.2's diagram (`ceo_kickoff → market_analysis_step → strategy_generation → options_strategy → code_generation → compliance_check →[blocked→END]→ code_validation →[3 fails→END, else loops back to code_generation]→ backtesting → evaluation →[pass]→ optimization → risk_assessment_step → deployment → END`, with an evaluation `fail` branch to `memory_ingest` that loops back to `strategy_generation` under 5 rejections or escalates back to `ceo_kickoff` at 5). Two node names (`market_analysis_step`, `risk_assessment_step`) are suffixed to avoid colliding with the state field of the same underlying concept — LangGraph rejects a node name equal to a state schema key.
    - **Circuit breaker (the critical requirement)**: `build_graph(enabled_agents)` decides, once, which callable gets wired to each node — a disabled agent gets a skip stand-in that never references (let alone calls) the real node function; an enabled agent's real function is looked up from `NODE_FUNCTIONS` only inside the "enabled" branch. Proven by a test that monkeypatches `NODE_FUNCTIONS["market_analysis_step"]` to a spy, disables `market-analyst`, and asserts the spy was never invoked.
    - **Gotcha caught via a smoke test before writing the full graph**: `StateGraph(PydanticModel)` forbids a node name identical to a state field name (`ValueError: '...' is already being used as a state key`) — this is why two node names carry the `_step` suffix.
    - **Gotcha caught by the first real end-to-end run**: LangGraph's default `recursion_limit` (25) is well under the step count a bounded validator-retry (up to 3) plus evaluation-rejection (up to 5 full `strategy_generation`-through-`memory_ingest` loops, ~8 nodes each) run can legitimately need — raised to 200 in `run_pipeline()`'s `ainvoke` config rather than treating a real bounded retry sequence as a runaway graph.
    - LLM-backed nodes (CEO, Strategy Generator, Options Strategy Agent, Python Code Generator) call `_llm_or_fallback()`, which tries the real router and — since this sandbox has no provider keys/egress — always catches `LlmRouterExhaustedError` and falls back to a deterministic canned response, logging that it did so; against a configured provider the same code path serves the real completion instead.
  - **Skill Registry** (`src/agents/tools/`): fixed `SKILLS` dict (`market-data-read`, `option-chain-read`, `portfolio-status-read`, `code-format-lint` — real AST-based banned-import + syntax check, not a stub — `sandbox-dry-run`, `notification-send`, the last explicitly stubbed until Phase 12 per the build prompt). `execute_skill(agent_id, skill_name, params)` is the *only* call path — grant-checked against `EffectiveAgentConfig.skills` (Phase 1's existing global-default + per-agent-override config surface, reused rather than duplicated) inside the function itself, so there's no second, ungated path to a skill's underlying callable. Proven by a test that calls `execute_skill()` directly (not through any API route) for a skill the agent's config doesn't grant and confirms it still raises.
  - **Prompt versioning** (`src/models/prompt_version.py`, `src/orchestration/prompt_versions.py`): `PromptVersion.content` is written once at `create_prompt_version()` (INSERT) — no update-content function exists anywhere in the module, confirmed by a test asserting the attribute is absent. `activate_prompt_version()` always computes a `difflib.unified_diff()` against whatever was previously `ACTIVE` for that agent and stores it on the newly-active row as part of the same call that flips status (old row → `SUPERSEDED`) — there's no path that activates without that diff. `rollback_prompt_version()` is the same call targeting an older version id.
  - **Heartbeat** (`src/agents/heartbeat.py`, `src/agents/scheduler.py`): eligible agents are read live from `EffectiveAgentConfig.heartbeat_enabled` (Phase 1's existing config surface — CEO/Risk Manager are `true` in the real `config/tradingos.config.json`), falling back to `{ceo-agent, risk-manager}` only when no Gateway config has been applied yet. Each check calls `execute_skill(agent_id, "market-data-read")` and records a `HeartbeatLog` row (`ok`/`alert`) — a failed self-check (e.g. an ungranted skill) becomes the alert itself rather than crashing the sweep. `start_heartbeat_loop()` mirrors Phase 2's `start_stall_sweep_loop` shape, wired into `main.py`'s lifespan (default 300s interval).
    - **Structural isolation (the critical requirement)**: `src/execution/orders.py::place_order` and `src/risk/limits.py::mutate_risk_limit` are new placeholder stub functions (real implementations ship in later phases) that exist purely so this requirement has something concrete to prove against. `heartbeat.py`/`scheduler.py` import neither module — proven by a test that both checks `hasattr()` is `False` and attempts `from src.agents.heartbeat import place_order`, confirming it raises `ImportError` (not merely that calling it would be denied).
  - API surface (`src/api/routes/agents.py`): `GET /api/v1/agents` (all 4 roles, read-only — roster + live capabilities/skills/enabled/heartbeat state) and `POST /api/v1/agents/pipeline/run` (SystemAdministrator/PortfolioManager — runs an objective through the full compiled graph, respecting live-config enabled/disabled agents).
  - **Verified live end-to-end** against a real running server: `GET /agents` returned all 24 with `audit-agent.can_disable: false`; `POST /agents/pipeline/run` with an objective reached `deployment` with `node_log` showing all 12 non-skipped node names in order; hand-edited `config/tradingos.config.json` to set `market-analyst.enabled: false`, confirmed the hot-reload picked it up within seconds via `GET /agents`, then re-ran the pipeline and confirmed `market_analysis_step:skipped(agent-disabled:market-analyst)` in the log with `market_analysis` left `null`; ran a real heartbeat sweep against the dev DB and confirmed `ok` `heartbeat_logs` rows for `ceo-agent`/`risk-manager` once the Gateway config was applied (and confirmed the sweep raises a clean `alert` row, not a crash, when it isn't). Demo users/rows cleaned up afterward.
- [x] Phase 4 — Strategy Pipeline & Sandbox — objective → LLM-generated `run_backtest(data, config) -> dict` module → AST ban-list + real ruff lint → real process-isolated sandbox execution → (options only) leg grounding + naked-options scan, with Backtesting→PaperTrading wired to Phase 2's approvals mechanism. New package `src/engine/` (`validation.py`, `options_grounding.py`, `sandbox/`). New tables `strategies`, `strategy_versions`, `strategy_suggestions`. 61 new tests (194 total).
  - **`strategies.status` is a plain `String(32)` + DB-level `CheckConstraint`, not a native Postgres enum** (`src/models/strategy.py`) — a deliberate departure from every other status column in this codebase (`Task.status`, `OrganizationRun.status`, etc, all `sa.Enum(native_enum=True)`), per this phase's own explicit instruction. Gives the identical DB-level guarantee (verified live: a raw `INSERT ... status='NotARealStatus'` is rejected by Postgres itself, and by a test) while sidestepping the native-enum-type-not-dropped-on-`downgrade()` defect class that had already hit three prior migrations (Phases 0–2) — there's no separate `CREATE TYPE`/`DROP TYPE` lifecycle to get wrong, and this migration's `downgrade()` genuinely has nothing enum-related to clean up.
  - **Static validation** (`src/engine/validation.py`): an AST ban-list (`os`/`subprocess`/`socket`/`sys`/`shutil`/`ctypes`/`importlib`/etc banned outright; every other import must be in an explicit allowlist — "dynamic imports outside an allowlist" read as allow-list, not just a ban-list; `eval`/`exec`/`compile`/`__import__` banned as *calls*, not just imports) followed by a **real** `ruff check` subprocess pass (not a stub) — `ruff` moved from a dev-only to a main runtime dependency in `pyproject.toml` because of this. Runs before anything reaches the sandbox, always in that order (ruff only runs once the ban-list already passed, so it's never asked to make sense of banned/syntactically-broken code).
  - **Sandbox** (`src/engine/sandbox/`) — **chose gVisor over Firecracker** (documented in `gvisor_runtime.py`'s docstring): Firecracker hard-requires `/dev/kvm`, confirmed absent in this project's own dev/CI sandbox (and common in other nested/cloud environments this app might run in); gVisor's ptrace platform needs no hardware virtualization and registers as a drop-in Docker runtime (`--runtime=runsc`), fitting the existing single-container deployment story better than Firecracker's separate VMM+jailer process model would. `GvisorSandboxRuntime` is real, production-intended code, **honestly never exercised in this repo's own tests** — no `runsc` installed, no Docker runtime registered for it here (same "real code path, unexercised without the real infra" posture as Phase 3's LLM provider HTTP clients) — `is_gvisor_available()` checks `docker info` and the class raises `SandboxRuntimeUnavailableError` (not a silent fallback) if it's absent.
    - **`RestrictedProcessSandboxRuntime`** is what this repo's tests (and `get_default_sandbox_runtime()`'s fallback) actually run against — real isolation from Linux primitives that work without special hardware/kernel support, verified live in this exact sandbox: a fresh network namespace per worker (`unshare --net`, confirmed via a raw unrestricted script that a socket `connect()` fails with `[Errno 101] Network is unreachable` — independent of any Python-level guard), `resource.setrlimit` for CPU/memory, a path-confined `open()` + import-allowlist-enforcing `__import__` (`restricted_exec.py`) for the filesystem/import surface AST-banning `os`/`subprocess` alone can't close (a legitimate `open()` call is still in scope for the contract). Adversarial tests prove both an `import socket` escape attempt and a `../../../etc/passwd` path-traversal attempt are actually blocked, not just declined.
    - **`worker_main.py`'s per-call CPU budget gotcha, caught before it shipped**: `RLIMIT_CPU` is a *cumulative* process-lifetime counter, not a per-call one — naively setting it once in a persistent (warm-pool) worker would give the worker's Nth call a shrinking budget as earlier calls ate into it, eventually `SIGXCPU`-killing an otherwise-healthy worker after a handful of light calls. Fixed by recomputing the limit every call as `resource.getrusage()`'s already-consumed CPU time plus that call's own budget — correct whether it's the first call in a fresh process or the fiftieth in a long-lived warm worker. Wall-clock timeout is enforced separately via `SIGALRM` (reliably preempts a pure-Python CPU-bound `while True: pass` loop, verified live), independent of the CPU-time mechanism.
    - **Warm pool** (`warm_pool.py`): a fixed number of persistent worker subprocesses (each already network-namespaced and interpreter-booted) reused across `execute()` calls via a newline-delimited-JSON stdin/stdout protocol, instead of paying the `unshare`+interpreter-boot cost on every single call. **Measured live in this sandbox**: 5 calls cold (fresh process each time) ≈0.21s vs. 5 calls through the warm pool ≈0.04s — a test asserts the warm path is meaningfully (not just marginally) faster. A worker that crashes or hangs mid-call is discarded and replaced, never left as a permanently-broken pool slot.
  - **Options legs grounding + naked-options scan** (`src/engine/options_grounding.py`): grounding checks proposed legs against a deterministic mock option chain (real feed is Phase 8/10's job) and annotates them with its LTP data. The naked-options scan is a **real implementation of Build Spec §8's stated rule** ("every sold leg must have a same-underlying OTM hedge" — a sold call needs a bought call at a higher strike, a sold put a bought put at a lower strike), not a no-op stub, since the rule as stated was simple enough to implement exactly; what's genuinely deferred to Phase 6 is broker margin checks, the correlation constraint, and wiring this same scan into the live/paper per-tick risk gate in addition to this pre-deployment check.
  - **Backtesting→PaperTrading approval gate** (`src/orchestration/strategies.py::promote_to_paper_trading`): the concrete case Phase 2's generic `conditional_transition()` + `ApprovalGate` primitive was built for — no other function performs this transition any other way. Proven unbypassable the same way Phase 2's own approvals test did: a second, independently-written caller invoking `conditional_transition()` directly (not through `promote_to_paper_trading()`) with the same gate is blocked identically, both before and after approval.
  - **Human suggestions flow** (`src/orchestration/strategy_suggestions.py`): free-text → AI verdict (LLM-or-fallback) → optional regeneration, diff-gated exactly like Phase 3's `prompt_versions.py` — `regenerate_from_suggestion()` always computes and stores a `difflib.unified_diff()` against the base version's code as part of the same call that creates the new `StrategyVersion` and links `regenerated_version_id`; there's no path that sets one without the other. A `reject` verdict never regenerates.
  - **Fallback-code-generator gotcha, caught by a test with a long suggestion text**: the deterministic fallback `run_backtest` template originally embedded the *entire* (up to 120-char) objective/suggestion summary on one docstring line — for a long-enough objective this itself tripped ruff's `E501` line-length check inside `validate_strategy_code()`, i.e. the fallback code could fail the very validation gate it's supposed to safely pass. Fixed by shortening the truncation and moving the summary onto its own line so the longest line the template can produce never approaches the 100-char limit.
  - API surface (`src/api/routes/strategies.py`, `src/api/routes/approvals.py`): `POST /api/v1/strategies` (create+generate+validate+sandbox in one synchronous call, mirroring Phase 2/3's own "drive to completion" endpoints), `GET .../{id}`, `POST .../{id}/suggestions` (+ `/review`, `/regenerate`), `POST .../{id}/request-promotion`, `POST .../{id}/promote`, and a new generic `POST /api/v1/approvals/{id}/decide` (SystemAdministrator/RiskManager) — deciding an approval isn't strategy-specific, so it isn't nested under `/strategies`.
  - **Verified live end-to-end** against a real running server: `POST /strategies` with an objective produced a strategy with `status: "Backtesting"` and one version with `static_validation_passed: true` and `sandbox_passed: true` (a real sandboxed execution, real result payload); `POST .../promote` returned 409 both with no approval request and with one still `pending`; a RiskManager's `POST /approvals/{id}/decide` approval was required before `POST .../promote` returned 200 with `status: "PaperTrading"`. Demo users/rows cleaned up afterward.
- [x] Phase 5 — Backtesting & Optimization — vectorized single-symbol backtest engine (`src/engine/backtest/engine.py`) over an explicit `(prices, signals)` pandas pair (never over a `StrategyVersion`'s sandboxed code — a deliberate scope line, see below), a real Indian NSE/SEBI friction model (`friction.py`: brokerage, STT, exchange charges, SEBI turnover fee, stamp duty, GST, plus ATR/volume-based slippage), corporate-action back-adjustment (`corporate_actions.py`, dividends explicitly and extensively documented as out of scope — not silently approximated), a data-freshness gate (`freshness.py`, stubbed against a `FakeDataLakeFreshness` until Phase 10's real data lake), Walk-Forward Optimization (`optimization/walk_forward.py`, every window must show positive out-of-sample expectancy to pass), Monte Carlo (`optimization/monte_carlo.py`, 10,000 trade-resampled paths, 95th-percentile — not historical — MaxDD governs sizing, plus a real `temporalio` workflow/activity wrapper in `optimization/temporal_workflows.py` + `workers/temporal_worker.py`), a real Optuna hyperparameter sweep with fANOVA parameter importance (`optimization/hyperparameter_sweep.py`, one real backtest per trial, no shortcutting), and multi-run pairwise return-correlation comparison (`backtest/comparison.py`, null — never a fabricated 0/1 — below 10 overlapping days). New tables `backtest_runs`, `optimization_runs` (`src/models/backtest_run.py`, `optimization_run.py`) using the same `String`+`CheckConstraint` status pattern as Phase 4's `strategies.status`. New package `src/orchestration/backtests.py` (persistence layer) and `src/api/routes/backtests.py` (`POST /backtests`, `GET /backtests/{id}`, `POST /backtests/{id}/walk-forward`, `POST /backtests/{id}/monte-carlo`, `POST /backtests/compare`, `POST /backtests/optimize` — strategy logic over HTTP is restricted to a small fixed `BuiltinStrategy` enum, consistent with this codebase's "no arbitrary code over any HTTP surface" posture). 58 new tests (252 total).
  - **"Never fabricate a metric" is load-bearing throughout, not just a Sharpe/CAGR rule**: a `_finite_or_none()` helper pattern returns `None` (never `0`) for every undefined statistic — Sharpe/CAGR/max_drawdown/win_rate/profit_factor in the engine, walk-forward out-of-sample expectancy when a window has zero trades, Monte Carlo's percentiles on an empty trade list, Optuna's `best_value` when every trial's objective metric is undefined (`float("-inf")` internally, never coerced to `0`), and the comparison matrix's correlation below the 10-overlapping-day threshold *or* when `pd.notna()` fails (e.g. a zero-variance constant return series, which real code makes `np.corrcoef` emit a genuine `RuntimeWarning: invalid value encountered in divide` — expected, not a bug, and asserted on in `test_engine_comparison.py`).
  - **Real Indian cost schedule, hand-verified**: `FrictionModel`'s defaults (intraday brokerage `min(0.03%, ₹20)`, delivery brokerage free, STT 0.1%/both-legs delivery vs 0.025%/sell-only intraday, exchange charges 0.00297%/both-legs, SEBI fee 0.0001%/both-legs, stamp duty 0.015% delivery / 0.003% intraday buy-leg-only, GST 18% on brokerage+exchange charges only) were cross-checked against hand calculations in `test_engine_friction.py` (e.g. a ₹100,000 delivery buy → total costs ₹118.6046 exactly) — the dynamic slippage term (`atr_multiplier * atr + volume_impact_coefficient * (qty/avg_daily_volume) * price`) is this codebase's own documented modeling choice, not a regulatory figure.
  - **numpy scalars are not JSON-serializable — hit twice, live, not in review**: `walk_forward.py::_expectancy()`'s `sum()/len()` over numpy-derived P&L values returns `numpy.float64`, and `expectancy > 0` on that returns `numpy.bool_`, not a plain `bool` — SQLAlchemy's JSON column type rejects both at persist time (`TypeError: Object of type bool_ is not JSON serializable`, caught via a live smoke test against the real dev Postgres, not a unit test). A second instance hit in `orchestration/backtests.py`'s `trade_pnls=[t.net_pnl for t in result.trades]`. Both fixed with an explicit `float(...)` cast at the boundary — anywhere a numpy/pandas value crosses into a JSON column needs the same treatment.
  - **`FanovaImportanceEvaluator` needs `scikit-learn` at construction, not just import**: the module imports fine without it, but instantiating the evaluator (it builds a `RandomForestRegressor` internally) raised `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'sklearn'` at actual runtime — added `scikit-learn>=1.4,<1.5` to `pyproject.toml`'s main dependencies (pulling in `scipy`/`joblib`/`threadpoolctl`/`cloudpickle` transitively). A lesson repeated from Phase 3's LLM-provider gotchas: "does it import" is not the same smoke test as "does it construct/run."
  - **Temporal**: `docker-compose.yml` gained a `temporal` service (`temporalio/temporal:latest`, `server start-dev --ip 0.0.0.0`, `depends_on: service_healthy` on the backend) and `TEMPORAL_ADDRESS` config plumbing — but the image pull genuinely failed in this sandbox (confirmed live: manually started `dockerd`, then `docker pull temporalio/temporal:latest` returned a real `403 Forbidden` from `production.cloudfront.docker.com`, not an assumption). `temporal_workflows.py`'s `@workflow.defn`/`@activity.defn` code is real and wraps the pure `run_monte_carlo()` function, but is honestly documented as unexercised end-to-end in this environment — please confirm `docker compose up` + a real worker run once in a normal environment with registry access.
  - **Verified live end-to-end** against a real running server: `POST /strategies` → `POST /backtests` (SMA-crossover signals, realistic Indian costs) → `completed` with real `sharpe`/`cagr`/`max_drawdown`/`profit_factor` → `POST .../walk-forward` (correctly reported `passed: false`, with every window's real negative out-of-sample expectancy shown — the strategy genuinely doesn't survive walk-forward on this seeded data, exactly the "must fail on any bad window" behavior under test) → `POST .../monte-carlo` (10,000 paths, real 95th-percentile MaxDD) → a second backtest run with a different builtin strategy → `POST /backtests/compare` returned a real (non-fabricated) pairwise correlation → separately verified `POST /backtests` refuses stale data (`refused_stale_data`, real `refusal_reason` naming the missing prior trading day, no engine execution) and `POST /backtests/optimize` persists a real completed Optuna study with fANOVA `param_importance`. Demo users/strategies/runs cleaned up afterward.
