a self-clearing WebSocket Latency Guard (`latency_guard.py`, 100ms default, the deliberate opposite latching behavior); a Compliance Checker (`compliance.py`, SEBI-style position limits + NSE-style circuit-filter band, `RegulatoryDataProvider` Protocol + `ReferenceTableRegulatoryDataProvider` — same Protocol-swap posture as Phase 5's `DataLakeFreshnessCheck`, deterministic and independent of any LLM output); the naked-options scanner from Phase 4 (`src.engine.options_grounding.naked_options_scan`) now also wired into the live/paper per-tick gate, not just the pre-deployment check; a Correlation Constraint (`correlation_constraint.py`, 0.85 default vs. a `NiftyBenchmarkProvider` Protocol + seeded `FakeNiftyBenchmarkProvider`) wired into **both** `src.orchestration.strategies.promote_to_paper_trading` (pre-deployment) and the new per-tick order-intent gate — the exact same function at both call sites, closing the gap a prior build left open; Volatility-Adjusted Position Sizing (`position_sizing.py`, ATR-based risk-per-trade); a Broker Circuit Breaker (`circuit_breaker.py`, opens after 3 consecutive 5xx, 1-minute cooldown, stubbed alert fan-out hook, tested against a `FakeBrokerAdapter` and an injectable clock); a Go-Live Readiness Gate (`go_live_gate.py`, ALL of ≥30 trades / ≥21 calendar days / ≥10-day clean Shadow Mode streak / ≤20pp win-rate divergence, all configurable); and dual-control risk limits (`src/models/risk_limit.py`, `src/orchestration/risk_limits.py`, `src/api/routes/risk_limits.py` — stage → confirm-by-a-different-user → apply, self-confirmation rejected inside the orchestration function itself, not only by RBAC — now the **only** way a value backing `infra.riskThresholdRefs` actually changes). New package `src/orchestration/risk_gate.py` (`create_order_intent`, the single choke point: kill switch checked first, ahead of compliance/naked-options/correlation, so a tripped switch blocks intent *creation*, not just a later submission step). New tables `kill_switch_states`, `risk_limits`, `risk_limit_change_requests`. New API routes `src/api/routes/kill_switch.py` (status/check/reset), `src/api/routes/risk_limits.py` (dual control), `src/api/routes/risk.py` (order-intent demo endpoint + Go-Live readiness evaluation). 62 new tests (314 total).
  - **Deviation from the literal instruction path**: the phase description said `src/api/routers/risk_limits.py`; kept it at `src/api/routes/risk_limits.py` instead to match every other route module in this codebase (`src/api/routes/*.py`, all wired via `src/api/router.py`'s `include_router`) — treated as a wording slip, not a deliberate new convention, and not worth a second parallel `routers/` directory.
  - **The order-intent risk gate is genuinely new infrastructure, not yet consumed by a real trading engine**: Phase 7 (Paper Trading Engine) and Phase 8 (broker adapters) don't exist yet, so `src.orchestration.risk_gate.create_order_intent` has no real caller in this codebase beyond its own tests and the `POST /api/v1/risk/order-intents` demo endpoint. That endpoint is deliberately what those later phases will call for real — this phase proves the gate itself (kill switch → compliance → naked-options → correlation, in that order, never reordered) works end-to-end over HTTP ahead of the engine that will depend on it.
  - **Kill switch persistence over a module-level singleton, on purpose**: "two separate instances that never share state" is enforced by a DB row per `mode` behind a UNIQUE constraint, not by two Python class instances — this is an async, multi-worker FastAPI service, so a module-level mutable singleton would not actually be shared across requests/workers the way the safety guarantee needs; a DB row is real cross-request, cross-process shared state, and the "never self-clears" contract is verified live against a real recovery observation (drawdown back to 0% after being fully tripped), not just unit-tested against the pure evaluator.
  - **Correlation constraint's default benchmark is always active, not opt-in**: `promote_to_paper_trading` defaults `benchmark_provider` to a seeded `FakeNiftyBenchmarkProvider` internally when the caller passes none, rather than skipping the check whenever no provider is supplied — the check only genuinely no-ops when there is real backtest-return data to skip (a strategy with no completed Phase 5 backtest yet, which is exactly what Phase 4's own promotion tests exercise, so they're unaffected). This was a deliberate choice against defaulting to "off": Build Spec §8 calls out a prior build's gap here explicitly, so silently making the check opt-in again would reintroduce a milder version of the same defect.
  - **`ComplianceCheckResult`/naked-options/correlation-breach are business outcomes, not exceptions; malformed input is a `ValueError`**: `check_position_limit`/`check_circuit_filter_band` raise `ValueError` for a non-positive portfolio or reference price (a caller bug — those values come from the system's own state in real use, never from unvalidated external input at this layer) but return `blocked=True` with a human-readable reason for an actual regulatory breach. Keeping that line clean is what let `run_compliance_checks` in `src.orchestration.risk_gate` combine all four check families (compliance, naked-options, correlation, and eventually more) into one flat `reasons: list[str]` without ambiguity about which failures are "the gate correctly rejected an order" versus "the caller passed garbage."
  - **Verified live end-to-end** against a real running server, matching this phase's three explicit acceptance criteria: (1) tripped the live kill switch via a real drawdown-breach observation, confirmed a subsequent order-intent request returned `423 Locked`, confirmed a simulated *full equity recovery* did **not** self-clear it (still `423` afterward), confirmed the separate paper-mode kill switch was completely unaffected and still accepted intents, then confirmed only an explicit `POST /kill-switch/live/reset` restored order-intent creation; (2) walked the Go-Live readiness gate through all four conditions one at a time — each one failing alone (`num_trades`, then `calendar_days_running`, then `clean_shadow_mode_streak_days`, then win-rate divergence) correctly reported `eligible: false` with the specific failing check named, and only the request with all four satisfied returned `eligible: true`; (3) staged a `max_drawdown_pct` change as one user, confirmed a same-user self-confirm attempt was rejected with `403`, confirmed a second, different user's confirm succeeded, applied it, and confirmed `GET /risk-limits` reflected the new effective value. Demo users and rows cleaned up afterward.
- [x] Phase 7 — Paper Trading Engine — the fully autonomous paper trading engine, Build Spec §11: **Layer 1** (`src/orchestration/paper_trading.py::run_daily_signal_generation`) re-runs the real Phase 5 engine (`run_vectorized_backtest`) once/day per active `PaperTradingSubscription` and records whichever BUY/SELL transition `src.engine.paper_trading.daily_signal.detect_todays_signal` reads off the *same* one-bar-forward position-lag convention the backtest engine itself uses internally — a shared `src/engine/backtest/signals.py` (extracted from a previously-private Phase 5 API duplicate) generates the deterministic `BuiltinStrategy` signal, never a second independently-written lag rule that could drift from Phase 5's. **Layer 2** (`process_tick`) is tick-driven: a universal stop-loss (`stop_loss.py`, one configurable % applied uniformly to any open position) is checked first on every tick regardless of signal state; re-entry falls out for free by re-reading the subscription's *latest* `DailySignal` row each tick rather than gating on a consumed-flag, since a stop-loss exit only flattens the position without touching signal state. Ticks flow through real Redis Streams (`paper:ticks:{symbol}`, `tick_feed.py`, `XADD`/`XRANGE` drain-since-cursor — not pub/sub, so a consumer that falls behind still sees every tick, and not `XREADGROUP` since there's no competing-consumer fan-out to justify) — Redis itself already existed in `docker-compose.yml` since Phase 2, just reused. Depth-walked fill simulation (`order_book.py::simulate_fill`) walks `OrderBookSnapshot` levels and returns an honest partial fill (`filled_quantity < requested_quantity`, `fully_filled=False`) whenever total depth is insufficient — never silently assumes a full fill. Position accounting is a single running average-cost-basis ledger per position (`position_ledger.py`, signed quantity, explicitly documented as a deliberate simplification vs. FIFO lot matching — it can't answer "which lot was this sale against" the way FIFO can). F&O margin (`margin.py`) is a percentage-of-notional approximation (long option = full premium, short option = premium + notional×pct, futures = notional×pct); `MarginEstimate.is_span_calculation` is always `False` — explicitly **not** a real SPAN calculation. Multi-leg execution (`multi_leg.py::execute_multi_leg_order`) fills each leg independently against its own book snapshot, in order, with no retry and no rollback of an already-filled leg if another leg only partially fills — mirrors real exchange behavior (no cross-leg atomicity without a specific combo order type) and is surfaced honestly via `MultiLegFillResult.fully_filled`/`any_filled`. Every entry/exit/re-entry goes through Phase 6's `src.orchestration.risk_gate.create_order_intent(mode="paper", ...)` as the **only** gate — itself fully automatic — with **no `ApprovalGate`/human-approval call anywhere in this module**; a tripped kill switch (`KillSwitchTrippedError`) or a compliance/correlation rejection (`OrderIntentRejectedError`) is caught, logged (`warning`/`info` respectively), and returns `None` rather than propagating, so neither crashes the autonomous per-tick drain loop for other subscriptions. The whole engine runs hands-off via a new `AsyncIOScheduler(timezone=IST)` (`paper_trading_scheduler.py`, started in `main.py`'s lifespan): a daily `CronTrigger(hour=8, minute=0, timezone=IST)` job (ahead of the 09:15 open) plus two `interval` jobs (mock tick publish every 3s, tick drain every 5s), all three gated by `is_market_open_ist()` (`market_hours.py`, weekday + 09:15–15:30 IST window — explicitly documented as not a full NSE holiday calendar, same honest-approximation posture as Phase 5's `previous_trading_day`) so they fire continuously but only *act* during real market hours. New tables `paper_trading_subscriptions`, `daily_signals`, `paper_positions`, `paper_fills`. New API routes `src/api/routes/paper_trading.py` (`POST /subscriptions`, `GET /subscriptions/{id}`, `GET /subscriptions/{id}/position`, `GET /subscriptions/{id}/fills`, `GET /subscriptions/{id}/signals`, `POST /daily-signal-run`, `POST /subscriptions/{id}/tick`). Honest-stub Protocols reused from the Phase 5/6 swap posture: `PriceDataProvider`/`FakeDailyPriceProvider`, `OrderBookProvider`/`MockOrderBookProvider`, `TickSource`/`MockTickSource` — all explicit Phase 8 (broker adapters) / Phase 10 (real data ingestion) stand-ins, swappable later with no call-site changes. 69 new tests (383 total).
  - **Gotcha — fake price provider's window discontinuity broke transition detection**: `FakeDailyPriceProvider.daily_bars()` originally regenerated a fresh random 90-day window from `as_of` backwards on *every call* (a new `np.random.default_rng(seed)` draw each time), so every day's lookback was an unrelated random series and no real BUY/SELL transition ever occurred even scanning 200+ days. Fixed by generating one continuous ~16-year series per symbol (`2020-01-01`–`2035-12-31`), cached per-instance, and slicing via `.loc[:as_of].tail(lookback_days)` — verified overlapping windows now agree with each other.
  - **Gotcha — full-notional entry sizing breached Phase 6's own compliance limit**: `process_tick`'s entry quantity was originally `int(initial_capital // tick_price)` (100% of capital), which always exceeded the default 10% SEBI-style position limit and was silently rejected by the risk gate on every live entry attempt (caught via a real `paper_trading.order_intent_rejected` log line). Fixed by adding a `position_size_pct` field (default 5.0) to `PaperTradingSubscription` and sizing entries off `initial_capital * (position_size_pct / 100.0)` instead — required regenerating the Alembic migration.
  - **Gotcha — tz-aware vs. tz-naive `Timestamp` comparison in the daily job**: `run_daily_signal_job` computed `as_of` as a tz-aware `pd.Timestamp.now(tz=IST).normalize()`, but `FakeDailyPriceProvider`'s index is tz-naive, so comparing them raised `TypeError: Cannot compare tz-naive and tz-aware datetime-like objects.` — caught by a scheduler test, fixed with `.tz_localize(None)`.
  - **Gotcha — Python's salted `hash()` broke "deterministic" per-symbol seeding across processes**: both `FakeDailyPriceProvider`'s default seed derivation and `MockTickSource`'s per-symbol RNG used `hash(symbol)`, which Python salts randomly per process by default (`PYTHONHASHSEED`) — so a "seeded, reproducible" series actually differed across separate process invocations, breaking a test that computed an expected signal date in one process and asserted it in another. Fixed by replacing `hash(symbol)` with `zlib.crc32(symbol.encode("utf-8"))` in both call sites; re-verified stable across three separate process runs.
  - **Verified live end-to-end** against two real running `uvicorn` processes (once during real open IST market hours, once during real closed IST market hours), matching this phase's acceptance criteria: enrolled a subscription, generated a real daily BUY signal off the live Phase 5 engine, drove a tick-driven entry with a realistic depth-walked partial-book fill, confirmed a no-signal tick was correctly a no-op, drove a tick through the universal stop-loss into a realistic exit fill with a correctly negative realized P&L, confirmed re-entry on a later tick with no new daily signal required (the acceptance-critical Layer 2 behavior), and retrieved the full fill history — all without a single human click. Separately confirmed the unattended scheduler: during real open market hours the daily/tick-publish/tick-drain jobs fired and acted on their own timer; during real closed market hours the same jobs still fired on schedule but correctly no-op'ed per the `is_market_open_ist()` gate. All demo data (subscriptions, positions, signals, fills, strategy/version, demo user) cleaned up afterward and confirmed removed.
- [x] Phase 8 — Broker Integrations — a single `BrokerAdapter` interface (`src/brokers/base.py`: place/modify/cancel order, order book, margin, positions, quote, option chain, expiries, plus a pure no-network `build_order_payload`) with two concrete, real httpx-calling implementations, Build Spec §13: `ZerodhaKiteAdapter` (`src/brokers/zerodha.py`, `has_sandbox=False` -- Kite Connect has no sandbox at all, and `get_option_chain`/`get_expiries` honestly raise `NotImplementedError` rather than fabricate an empty list, since building one requires the NFO instrument master Phase 10 hasn't built yet) and `UpstoxAdapter` (`src/brokers/upstox.py`, `has_sandbox=True` -- Upstox publishes a real Sandbox environment at a separate base URL, and `sandbox=`/`pointed_at_sandbox` on an instance is independent of the broker-level `has_sandbox` capability flag; option chain/expiries are fully implemented here, unlike Zerodha). `ResilientBrokerAdapter` (`src/brokers/resilient.py`) wraps any adapter's nine network-calling methods through Phase 6's existing `BrokerCircuitBreaker` (`src.engine.risk.circuit_breaker`, unmodified -- only adapted onto the richer Phase 8 surface), routing every `BrokerServerError` (5xx) through one breaker instance while leaving `build_order_payload` bypassing it entirely (a pure local method can't fail against a broker). Shadow Mode (`src/orchestration/shadow_mode.py::run_shadow_order_check`, new `shadow_mode_runs` table) honestly differentiates a genuine sandbox dry run (Upstox: a real network call against the sandbox base URL, `confidence="real_sandbox_dry_run"`, `broker_response` populated) from local-payload-construction-only (Zerodha: `place_order` is never called at all, `confidence="local_payload_only"`, `broker_response` always `NULL` by construction) -- both branches also write a matching `AuditLog` entry, and the two confidences are never merged into one boolean anywhere in the stored record, an API response, or a log line. An encrypted local secrets store (`src/security/secrets_store.py`, Build Spec §3/§20 -- deliberately not Vault) persists broker credentials as one Fernet-encrypted file (`cryptography.fernet`, key from `SECRETS_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, never stored alongside the ciphertext) with 0600 file permissions; `BrokerCredentials.__repr__` is redacted, and the write-only credentials API (`src/api/routes/broker_credentials.py`, SystemAdministrator-only writes/deletes) has no response schema that ever echoes a value back -- only a per-broker `configured: bool`. `src/brokers/tick_source.py::build_tick_source()` replaces Phase 7's permanent `MockTickSource` wiring in `main.py`'s lifespan: it polls real broker quotes via a circuit-breaker-wrapped adapter when credentials are configured, falling back to the Phase 7 mock feed otherwise (which is what this sandbox, with no live broker credentials or egress, always resolves to) -- `TickSource.next_tick` became `async` (was sync in Phase 7) so a real HTTP-polling implementation can await a call. New API routes `src/api/routes/broker_credentials.py` (`GET/POST/DELETE /broker-credentials{/broker}`) and `src/api/routes/shadow_mode.py` (`POST /shadow-mode/check`, `GET /shadow-mode/runs`, always builds a sandbox-pointed adapter for Upstox regardless of caller intent, so a dry run can never accidentally reach production). New table `shadow_mode_runs`. 52 new tests (435 total).
  - **Design choice -- Zerodha's missing option chain is an honest `NotImplementedError`, not an empty list**: Kite Connect has no dedicated option-chain endpoint; building one requires downloading and filtering the full NFO instruments CSV dump, which is Phase 10's instrument-master-sync territory. Returning `[]` would read as "no options exist for this underlying" instead of "not wired up yet" -- the same silently-wrong-shape failure mode this codebase has refused everywhere else (Phase 5's dividend/correlation-null handling, Phase 7's partial-fill honesty). Upstox's adapter implements both fully instead, since Upstox's v2 API genuinely has the endpoints.
  - **Design choice -- adapters build a fresh `httpx.AsyncClient` per call, with an injectable `transport`**: matches Phase 3's `src.agents.llm_router` client shape (`async with httpx.AsyncClient(...) as client`) rather than holding one shared client across a service's lifetime, which would need explicit close-on-shutdown wiring in `main.py`. The tradeoff (no connection-pool reuse across calls) is the same one `llm_router` already accepted; the payoff is that every adapter method is unit-testable by constructing the adapter with `transport=httpx.MockTransport(handler)` and nothing else.
  - **Gotcha -- `TickSource.next_tick` had to become `async`**: Phase 7's `TickSource` Protocol defined `next_tick` as a plain sync method (fine for `MockTickSource`'s in-memory random walk), but a real broker-quote poller must await an HTTP call. Fixed by making the Protocol, `MockTickSource.next_tick`, and `publish_ticks_once`'s call site all `async`/`await` -- a one-file, contained change since nothing else in the codebase called `next_tick` directly. Renamed `publish_mock_ticks_once`→`publish_ticks_once` and the scheduler's `run_mock_tick_publish_job`/`MOCK_TICK_PUBLISH_JOB_ID`→`run_tick_publish_job`/`TICK_PUBLISH_JOB_ID` at the same time, since as of this phase the job is no longer permanently mock-only.
  - **Gotcha -- `build_tick_source()` resolves once at process startup, not per-tick**: `main.py`'s lifespan calls it exactly once when wiring `start_paper_trading_scheduler`, so credentials written via the credentials API *after* the process has booted don't take effect until the next restart. Confirmed live (see below) -- this is a deliberate simplicity tradeoff for this phase, not a bug, and worth a note for Phase 9/12 if hot-reloading broker credentials becomes a requirement.
  - **Verified live** against a real running `uvicorn` process: (1) wrote Zerodha credentials over HTTP, confirmed the write response and the subsequent `GET /broker-credentials` status listing never contained the raw values anywhere in the response body, confirmed the on-disk ciphertext file was 0600 and did not contain the raw secret string as a substring, and confirmed deleting the credentials cleared the configured flag; (2) ran a real Shadow Mode check against Zerodha with no mock transport at all -- it returned instantly with `confidence="local_payload_only"`/`broker_response=null`, proving live that this path never attempts network I/O (this sandbox's outbound proxy blocks unlisted egress, so a real attempt would have failed loudly, not silently); (3) ran the same check against Upstox (real credentials configured, sandbox-pointed adapter) and it genuinely attempted a network call to `api-sandbox.upstox.com`, failing with `httpx.ProxyError: 403 Forbidden` from this sandbox's own egress policy -- the failure itself is the proof: Upstox's path really does try to leave the process, unlike Zerodha's; (4) restarted the server after configuring Zerodha credentials and confirmed `paper_trading.tick_source_using_broker_quotes` logged at startup (vs. no such log on the very first boot with zero credentials configured, which correctly fell back to `MockTickSource` and ran Phase 7's full daily-signal/tick/stop-loss cycle exactly as before with no regression); (5) directly invoked `build_tick_source().next_tick("INFY")` against the running configuration and observed the same real, sandbox-network-policy-blocked attempt rather than a fabricated tick. All demo credentials, shadow-mode-run rows, audit-log entries, and demo users cleaned up afterward and confirmed removed.
- [x] Phase 9 — Live Order Intent Pipeline & Sign-off Queue — the human-gated live trading flow, Build Spec §12: the single most important product decision in this rebuild, operationalized as full autonomy through paper trading (Phase 7) but a mandatory human click for anything that moves real money. `live_order_intents` (`src/models/live_order_intent.py`) is the exact §12.3 schema (id, strategy_id, symbol, side, quantity, intent_type, generated_at, expires_at, status, approved_by, approved_at, resulting_order_id, batch_authorization_id — deliberately no price column, see below) with `status` constrained to `pending_approval → approved → submitted/failed`, `pending_approval → rejected`, or `pending_approval/approved → expired`; no function anywhere except `_submit_intent_to_broker` may ever write `submitted`. `LiveExecutionPipeline` (`src/orchestration/live_trading.py`) generates intents using the *same* tick-driven signal logic Phase 7's paper engine uses — `generate_builtin_signals`/`detect_todays_signal`/`check_universal_stop_loss`/`apply_fill` are imported and called directly from `src.engine.paper_trading.position_ledger`, never reimplemented — but a triggering tick writes a `pending_approval` row instead of simulating a fill; a symbol's live quote is mode-agnostic, so `run_intent_generation_job` drains the *same* Redis Stream key (`paper:ticks:{symbol}`) Phase 7/8's tick-publish job already produces, with no separate live-specific tick-publishing job. A background expiry sweep (`expire_stale_intents`, `run_expiry_sweep_job` in the new `src/orchestration/live_trading_scheduler.py`) resolves any intent past its configured window (`LiveTradingSubscription.intent_expiry_seconds`, default 90s, `CheckConstraint 0<x<=300` enforcing the hard 5-minute platform-wide max) to `status=expired` server-side, on a 10s interval job — deliberately the one job in this phase *not* gated by `is_market_open_ist()`, since a stale intent must resolve safely even after the market closes and nobody is looking at the UI. A one-time-per-strategy human sign-off (`approve_strategy_for_live_eligibility` in `src/orchestration/strategies.py`, mirroring `promote_to_paper_trading`'s exact shape) requires the Phase 6 Go-Live Readiness Gate to pass fresh on every call *before* the generic `ApprovalGate`+`conditional_transition` check even runs — `StrategyStatus.PAPER_TRADING → LIVE_ELIGIBLE` — and `generate_live_order_intent` refuses to generate anything for a strategy not already in `LIVE_ELIGIBLE`. Approve/reject endpoints (`src/api/routes/live_trading.py`) let a human decide an individual intent; on approval, `_submit_intent_to_broker` re-quotes fresh via `adapter.get_quote()` (there's no price column to smuggle a stale one through — a real market order is priced at execution, not at planning) and re-runs Phase 6's `create_order_intent(mode="live", ...)` risk gate one more time before ever calling the broker adapter, then writes an `Order` row (status `submitted`/`failed`) and, only on a genuine submission, a `Trade` row (status always `pending_confirmation` or `failed`, **never** `filled` — this codebase has no execution-report polling/webhook ingestion yet, an explicit honesty line rather than a fabricated fill, documented as a Phase 11-class concern) plus an `AuditLog` entry; a rejected intent never reaches the broker adapter at all. Bounded batch pre-authorization (`LiveBatchAuthorization`, `create_batch_authorization`) lets a human pre-approve up to `max_intents` future intents for a strategy within `[window_start, window_end)` and a `max_notional_per_intent` cap in one logged action; `_find_eligible_batch_authorization` re-checks `intents_used < max_intents AND max_notional_per_intent >= notional` itself under `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on *every* call — it never trusts a caller-supplied `batch_authorization_id` — and only on a fresh, passing re-check does `generate_live_order_intent` auto-consume it (auto-approve + immediately submit through the same `_submit_intent_to_broker` path manual approval uses); an intent that would exceed either bound is rejected server-side even if a batch authorization row exists, proven by two dedicated tests that simulate a client trying to exceed each bound independently. The Kill Switch (Phase 6) stops intents from being *generated*, not just approved: `generate_live_order_intent` calls `assert_not_tripped(db, "live")` as its literal first action, before any subscription/strategy lookup — additive to, not a replacement for, the pre-existing submission-time check inside `_submit_intent_to_broker` (via `create_order_intent`), since a human can sit on a pending intent for up to 5 minutes during which the switch could trip; both paths are covered by dedicated tests, including one that trips the switch *between* generation and approval and confirms submission is still blocked. New tables `live_order_intents`, `live_batch_authorizations`, `live_trading_subscriptions` (per-strategy/symbol config: `sma_window`, `initial_capital`, `stop_loss_pct`, `position_size_pct`, `intent_expiry_seconds`, plus mutable `last_signal_type`/`last_signal_reference_price`/`last_signal_generated_at` columns holding Layer 1's daily-signal state directly — no separate `live_daily_signals` table, since only the latest signal ever matters for intent generation and every resulting intent already gets its own persisted, human-visible row), `orders`, `trades`, `live_positions`. New shared broker helper `build_configured_adapter()` (`src/brokers/factory.py`, extracted so both `src.brokers.tick_source.build_tick_source()` and the new live pipeline pick a configured broker adapter the same way) and a `get_live_broker_adapter()` FastAPI dependency wrapping it, specifically so tests can override it via `app.dependency_overrides` — the same seam Phase 8's `get_broker_credentials_store` established. 22 new tests across `tests/test_orchestration_live_trading.py`, `tests/test_orchestration_live_trading_scheduler.py`, `tests/test_live_trading_api.py` (457 total).
  - **Alembic circular-FK saga (`live_order_intents.resulting_order_id` ↔ `orders.live_order_intent_id`) — took three separate rounds to get right, the most reusable lesson from this phase**: round 1, the naive autogenerated migration created `live_order_intents` (with an inline FK to `orders`) before `orders` existed at all — invalid DDL, plus a SQLAlchemy `SAWarning: Cannot correctly sort tables; there are unresolvable cycles`. Fixed by manually splitting the FK into its own `op.create_foreign_key(...)` call placed *after* both tables exist in the migration script (with a matching `op.drop_constraint(...)` first in `downgrade()`). Round 2: the full pytest suite then failed with `sqlalchemy.exc.CircularDependencyError: Can't sort tables for DROP`, because `tests/conftest.py`'s `Base.metadata.drop_all()`/`create_all()` (ORM-metadata-level schema setup, independent of the hand-fixed Alembic script) hit the identical cycle. Fixed by adding `use_alter=True, name="fk_live_order_intents_resulting_order_id_orders"` directly onto the `ForeignKey()` definition in `src/models/live_order_intent.py`. Round 3 — the subtle one: after adding `use_alter=True` to the model and regenerating a clean single migration, `alembic upgrade head` reported success with **no errors at all**, but `SELECT conname FROM pg_constraint WHERE conname = 'fk_live_order_intents_resulting_order_id_orders'` came back **empty** — the constraint genuinely didn't exist. Root cause: Alembic's autogenerate, even with `use_alter=True` on the model, correctly *excludes* the FK from `live_order_intents`'s `CREATE TABLE` (avoiding the forward-reference error) but then never adds it back anywhere in the generated script — it's silently dropped, not deferred. Real fix: an explicit `op.create_foreign_key(...)` call by hand in `upgrade()` after `orders` is created (same as round 1's fix, but this time verified by directly querying `pg_constraint` after `alembic upgrade head`, not just trusting a clean exit code) — re-verified the full upgrade→downgrade→upgrade round-trip and `alembic check` reports "No new upgrade operations detected." **Takeaway for any future circular FK**: `use_alter=True` alone is necessary but not sufficient — always confirm the constraint actually landed with a raw `pg_constraint` query, never trust Alembic's silent success.
  - **Test bug caught by a real HTTP full-cycle test, not by review**: `tests/test_live_trading_api.py`'s mock broker transport hardcoded a mismatched symbol key (`"DEMOAPISTOCK"`) while the actual enrolled/queried symbol in the full-cycle test was `"DEMOSTOCK"` — the mock quote lookup silently returned an empty dict for the real symbol, producing `last_price=0.0` and failing Phase 6's compliance check with `ValueError: reference_price must be positive`. Fixed by making the handler symbol-agnostic (read the symbol out of the request's own query params and echo it back as the response key) instead of hardcoding a placeholder.
  - **Real production bug caught only by live verification, not by any test**: `GET /api/v1/live-trading/orders/{order_id}/trades` had no `register_policy` call at all, so every request — even from a role that should have access — returned `403 Forbidden` unconditionally. Only surfaced because the live-verification pass exercised the full approve→order→trades read cycle over real HTTP; the existing test suite happened not to hit that exact route. Fixed by adding the missing `register_policy("GET", ..., roles=list(Role))` alongside its sibling routes. Worth remembering: a missing RBAC policy registration is a silent-403 failure mode that unit tests using a single fixed test role can miss entirely if that route is never actually called in the suite.
  - **Verified live end-to-end** against a real running `uvicorn` process, matching every piece of this phase's acceptance criteria: confirmed the Go-Live Gate genuinely blocking live-eligibility sign-off with bad readiness metrics (422, naming the failing checks) and succeeding once all four Phase 6 conditions passed; watched a live order intent appear in the sign-off queue generated off a real tick against the live daily/Layer-2 pipeline; let one intent run past its configured window and confirmed the autonomous background sweep (not a client-side timer, not a manual trigger) resolved it to `expired` with zero orders ever created; attempted approval with no broker configured and got an honest `503` with the intent left unmutated (no partial state corruption on a failed dependency); configured real (sandbox-equivalent, fake but present) Zerodha credentials and attempted approval again — it genuinely tried a live network call to `api.kite.trade`, which failed only due to this sandbox's own egress policy (`403 Forbidden`, captured verbatim in the `AuditLog` row's `details` JSON) with zero `Order` rows created on that failure, proving the submission path is real network I/O, not a stub; and confirmed the kill switch stops intent *generation* itself, not just approval — tripped the live kill switch via a real drawdown breach, confirmed the live intent count was unchanged (3 before, 3 after) across a `generate-intent` call that correctly returned `423 Locked`, and confirmed via server logs that the scheduler's own background jobs (expiry sweep, market-hours-gated intent generation) had been firing unattended the entire session with no manual trigger anywhere in the demonstration. All demo rows (users, strategy + version, `LiveTradingSubscription`, all `LiveOrderIntent`/`LivePosition` rows, `ApprovalRequest`/`AuditLog` entries, broker credentials) cleaned up afterward and confirmed removed; kill switch reset to untripped.
- [x] Phase 10 — Market Data & Data Lake — the market data pipelines, Build Spec §14: a real DuckDB + Parquet data lake (`src/data/lake.py`) partitioned `year/month/symbol.parquet` for both `ohlcv_daily` and `ohlcv_intraday`, with idempotent partition writes (every write is a read-merge-dedup-write against the existing partition file, never a blind append — writing the same batch twice adds zero rows, and an overlapping batch with a corrected value replaces in place) and two DuckDB catalog views refreshed on a schedule via a short-lived `catalog.duckdb` connection (every other internal read goes straight at the Parquet files through an ephemeral in-memory DuckDB connection, so the nightly catalog refresh's brief write-lock never contends with a concurrent read). Six pipelines, **all genuinely scheduled** (`src/orchestration/market_data_scheduler.py`, APScheduler, mirroring Phase 7/9's shape) rather than manual/dormant — the explicit fix this phase's prompt called out by name: incremental daily OHLCV ingestion (evening cron, after NSE close, skipping NSE holidays), corporate actions ingestion, instrument master sync, intraday minute-bar ingestion (market-hours-gated interval job), nightly DuckDB catalog refresh, and nightly checksum+row-count-validated backup (`src/data/backup.py`) — plus NSE Bhavcopy/F&O bhavcopy as the one **on-demand fallback** path (`src/data/bhavcopy.py`), which genuinely attempts a real network fetch against NSE's public archive before honestly falling back to the deterministic synthetic provider on failure. The **symbol universe** driving every scheduled job is derived live from active `PaperTradingSubscription`/`LiveTradingSubscription` rows — no separate fabricated watchlist; a fresh install with nothing enrolled simply has nothing to ingest, and every job no-ops cleanly (no provenance row) rather than erroring on an empty list. A real NSE holiday calendar (`src/data/nse_calendar.py`) closes the gap Phase 5 (`previous_trading_day`) and Phase 7 (`is_market_open_ist`) both explicitly documented by name: fixed-date national holidays (Republic Day, Maharashtra Day, Independence Day, Gandhi Jayanti, Christmas) are generated in code across a fixed year range; movable/lunisolar holidays (Holi, Diwali, Eid, Guru Nanak Jayanti, ...) are deliberately **not** hardcoded for years without a verified NSE circular — fabricating exact dates would be the same class of dishonesty this codebase refuses everywhere else — and instead load from an operator-maintained `config/nse_holidays.json` (shipped seeded with real 2025 dates as a working example). New reference-data tables `instruments`, `corporate_actions`, `market_data_provenance` (one row per pipeline run: what ran, against what source, with what outcome — e.g. `bhavcopy_fallback_synthetic` vs `nse_bhavcopy_real`, the same honesty `source`/`confidence` distinction Phase 8's Shadow Mode already drew) and `dataset_freshness_records` (one row per symbol/data_type/data_date actually landed in the lake, written only *after* `src.data.lake.has_data_for` confirms the write is real and readable back — freshness state can never drift ahead of what the Parquet store actually has). **Item 7, the explicit "go back and remove the stub" instruction**: `src/api/routes/backtests.py` no longer constructs `FakeDataLakeFreshness` — it builds a real `DataLakeFreshnessSnapshot` from `src.data.freshness_snapshot.load_freshness_snapshot` (querying `dataset_freshness_records`) unioned with whatever dates the caller's own submitted `bars` cover, so a caller who supplies explicit bars (every prior phase's dev/test path) keeps working unchanged, while a symbol the real pipeline has actually ingested is now genuinely recognized as fresh even when the request's own bars don't happen to include the required date — proven live (see below) by a backtest that only passes the freshness gate because the real lake has the data, not because of what was submitted. `FakeDataLakeFreshness` itself is untouched and stays a legitimate test fixture, same as every other Fake-prefixed stand-in in this codebase; only production code stopped constructing one. Phase 7's `PriceDataProvider` Protocol — built explicitly for this moment ("Phase 10's real data lake becomes the provider later with no call-site changes") — now has a real implementation (`src/data/price_provider.py::DataLakePriceProvider`) wired into `main.py` for both the paper and live trading schedulers, falling back per-symbol to the same synthetic `FakeDailyPriceProvider` when the lake doesn't yet have enough ingested history for that symbol (the same graceful-degradation shape as Phase 8's `build_tick_source()`). Market Pulse (India VIX, sector-index and global-index day-change) is on-demand only, no persistence, computed straight from the same deterministic provider. New API routes `src/api/routes/market_data.py`: `GET /market-data/{pulse,freshness/{symbol},instruments,provenance}` (read, all roles) and `POST /market-data/{ingest/daily,ingest/intraday,ingest/corporate-actions,ingest/instrument-master,bhavcopy-fallback,catalog-refresh,backup}` (operator roles) — every manual trigger calls the exact same orchestration function the scheduler calls automatically, the same "demo trigger, never a required step" posture as Phase 7's `/daily-signal-run` and Phase 9's `/generate-intent`. 39 new tests across `tests/test_data_lake.py`, `tests/test_nse_calendar.py`, `tests/test_orchestration_market_data.py`, `tests/test_market_data_scheduler.py`, `tests/test_market_data_api.py`, `tests/test_price_provider.py` (496 total).
  - **Real production bug caught only by live verification, not by any test**: the holiday-skip early-return branch of `run_incremental_daily_ingestion` called `_record_provenance` (which only `db.add()`s + `flush()`es a row) and returned directly, without the `await db.commit()` every other code path in that module has. Over HTTP this was invisible — the endpoint still returned a well-formed `MarketDataProvenanceResponse` with a real-looking id, because the in-session identity map reflected the flushed-but-uncommitted row — but a fresh request against the same database found nothing: the row silently never persisted, rolled back the moment the request's session closed. Caught by manually re-querying Postgres directly after a live holiday-skip trigger and finding the table empty. **Fixed** by adding the missing `await db.commit()` before the early return. The existing unit test for this path (`test_incremental_daily_ingestion_skips_nse_holiday`) hadn't caught it either, for the identical reason live verification almost missed it: it only asserted on the in-memory object the same session had already flushed, never re-opened a fresh session to prove the write actually survived. **Strengthened the test** to re-query via a brand-new `db_session_factory()` session afterward — confirmed it fails against the pre-fix code and passes with the fix. **Lesson for every future orchestration function with an early-return branch**: every path that writes must commit before returning, and any test asserting "this row exists" must prove it from a session that never saw the write happen, not the one that wrote it.
  - **Design choice — Parquet round-trips DATE columns back as pandas Timestamps regardless of what Python type was written**: `write_daily_bars`' read-merge-write cycle initially compared freshly-computed `datetime.date` objects (new rows) against `pandas.Timestamp` objects (re-read from an existing partition file), which `sort_values`/comparison operators can't compare across a concat — `TypeError: Cannot compare Timestamp with datetime.date`. Fixed by always re-normalizing an existing partition's key column back to the same dtype as the incoming batch immediately after reading it, before any concat/dedup/sort.
  - **Design choice — an empty data lake must never break the catalog views**: a bare `read_parquet('glob/*.parquet')` against a directory with zero partition files raises `duckdb.IOException: No files found that match the pattern`, which would make `refresh_catalog_views` crash on a fresh install with nothing ingested yet. Fixed by checking for at least one matching file first and, when none exist, defining an empty-but-correctly-typed view (`SELECT NULL::DATE AS date, ... WHERE FALSE`) instead of ever calling `read_parquet` on an empty glob.
  - **Design choice — Postgres, not a live DuckDB query, backs the freshness gate**: `dataset_freshness_records` (fast, async-native, upsertable via the same `pg_insert(...).on_conflict_do_update(...)` pattern `src/gateway/apply.py` already established) is what `load_freshness_snapshot` queries, not a synchronous DuckDB call against `catalog.duckdb` inline in an async request handler — avoids both a blocking call inside the event loop and any file-lock contention with the nightly catalog-refresh job's own brief write access to that same file.
  - **Design choice — one real network attempt, not six**: every ingestion pipeline's underlying data *source* is the same deterministic `FakeMarketDataProvider` (daily/intraday bars, instrument master, corporate actions, Market Pulse) — an honest stand-in for a real market-data vendor integration this codebase doesn't have yet, same posture as Phase 7/8's Fake providers. Only the Bhavcopy fallback path (`src/data/bhavcopy.py`) genuinely calls out over `httpx` to NSE's public archive first, matching Phase 8's Upstox-Shadow-Mode-vs-Zerodha-local-payload precedent of picking one path to be authentically network-real rather than diluting six stubs into six half-real network calls.
  - **Verified live end-to-end** against a real running `uvicorn` process: confirmed all three APScheduler instances (market data, paper trading, live trading) started with their jobs registered from the boot log; manually triggered every pipeline over real HTTP (daily ingestion for a real trading day, the same call for a real fixed-date NSE holiday correctly skipping with zero rows and — after the bug fix above — a provenance row that genuinely survives a fresh query, intraday minute-bar ingestion, corporate actions, instrument master sync, Bhavcopy fallback genuinely attempting `archives.nseindia.com` and failing with a real `403 Forbidden` from this sandbox's own egress policy before honestly reporting `source: "bhavcopy_fallback_synthetic"`, catalog refresh reporting the correct row counts, and a nightly backup whose manifest validated clean) and confirmed real Parquet files, a `catalog.duckdb`, and a timestamped backup directory on disk; then proved the freshness-gate rewiring decisively — a backtest submitted with bars ending 2026-09-11 (deliberately not covering the required 2026-09-16 prior-trading-day) against a symbol the real pipeline HAD ingested came back `status: "completed"` (fresh only because of the real lake, since the submitted bars alone could never have satisfied it), while the identical request against a symbol that had never been ingested came back `status: "refused_stale_data"` with the correct reason. All demo rows (provenance, freshness records, instruments, corporate actions, backtest runs, strategy/version, demo user) and the real `data_lake`/`data_lake_backups` directories cleaned up afterward and confirmed removed.
- [x] Phase 11 — Audit & Observability — a hash-chained, DB-trigger-enforced append-only audit log (Build Spec §19), replacing Phase 1's plain `audit_log` table in place. `src/audit/chain.py` (pure, no SQLAlchemy dependency): `compute_entry_hash` (SHA-256 over `sequence|previous_hash|actor|action|entity_type|entity_id|canonical_json(details)|created_at.isoformat()`) and `verify_chain`, shared by both the live-DB reader and the WORM-archive reader so a divergence check is nothing more than running the identical verification twice and comparing. `src/audit/service.py::write_audit_entry` is the **only** function that may ever construct an `AuditLog` row (every prior phase's ad hoc `AuditLog(...)` call site — Phase 1's Agent Gateway apply/reject, Phase 8's Shadow Mode, Phase 9's live trading — migrated to call it instead) — serialized via a fixed-key `pg_advisory_xact_lock` (global, not per-entity: Build Spec §19 asks for one continuous chain, not a per-entity ledger), auto-picking up the bound structlog correlation ID unless one is passed explicitly. A hand-written Alembic migration (`ce1b6bee5a66_audit_log_hash_chain.py`) adds `sequence`/`previous_hash`/`hash`/`correlation_id` as nullable, backfills every pre-existing row (16 in dev) using the exact same `compute_entry_hash` production code uses, tightens to `NOT NULL`, then creates the append-only trigger — genuinely continuous from row one, not just from whenever the migration ran (verified live: sequence 1 is still `startup / agent_gateway.config_applied` from Phase 1).
  - **DB-level append-only trigger, verified from outside the app entirely**: `reject_audit_log_mutation()` (`RAISE EXCEPTION` on `TG_OP`) + `BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE` trigger — no code path, not even a raw `psql` `UPDATE`/`DELETE` as the app's own DB role, can alter or remove a row without first dropping the trigger. **Gotcha, caught by the test suite (not live verification)**: the trigger's `CREATE FUNCTION`/`CREATE TRIGGER` raw SQL only existed inside the Alembic migration, but `tests/conftest.py`'s `db_session_factory` fixture builds the test schema via `Base.metadata.create_all()`, which has no mechanism to run arbitrary DDL like that — so a fresh test DB silently had no trigger at all. Fixed by moving the two `CREATE ...` statements into two module-level string constants in `src/models/audit_log.py` (`CREATE_AUDIT_LOG_TRIGGER_FUNCTION_SQL`, `CREATE_AUDIT_LOG_TRIGGER_SQL`), attached via `sqlalchemy.event.listen(AuditLog.__table__, "after_create", DDL(...))` so `create_all()` installs the trigger too — and the Alembic migration now imports and executes these same two constants instead of duplicating the SQL, so the two paths can't drift apart. **Second gotcha, caught by the same test**: SQLAlchemy's `DDL` construct treats a bare `%` in its statement as a Python format placeholder when compiled against a table-bound `after_create` event (it substitutes `%(table)s`/`%(schema)s`/`%(fullname)s`) — the trigger function's `RAISE EXCEPTION '...: % is not permitted...', TG_OP` broke with `TypeError: %i format: a real number is required, not dict` the moment `create_all()` tried to fire it. Fixed by escaping to `%%` only in the `DDL(...)` call site (`event.listen`'s payload); the Alembic migration's `op.execute()` call, using the same unescaped constant, was never affected — it isn't bound to a table event and never goes through that substitution path. Both fixes are permanent, load-bearing infrastructure now, not test-only workarounds.
  - **WORM archive: a local append-only NDJSON file, not MinIO** (`src/audit/archive.py`), an explicit choice per Build Spec §5's own "MinIO optional at smallest scale, can start with local append-only file storage" wording and this codebase's repeated precedent (Phase 4's sandbox mount, Phase 10's data lake) of picking the locally-realizable option first. One continuous `audit_archive/audit_log.ndjson`, never daily-rotated (fragmenting the hash chain across files would turn one authoritative check into N per-file ones). After first creating the file, `_try_make_append_only()` attempts `chattr +a` (the ext4/xfs append-only attribute) — **confirmed genuinely working in this sandbox**, both in the test suite and live (`lsattr` showing the `a` flag set on a real running server) — falling back to a logged warning, never a crash, where the capability isn't granted; application-level discipline (the module never opens the file in any mode but `"a"`) is what holds the line day to day either way, the OS attribute is defense in depth. `run_archival_sweep` appends only rows past `last_archived_sequence` (idempotent re-runs archive nothing new). `verify_archive_chain_divergence` runs two independent checks — the archive's own internal chain validity, and every archived row's hash against the live DB's row for that sequence — either failing is a real, reported divergence naming the first broken/mismatched sequence; a genuine finding writes its own `audit.divergence_detected`/`audit.manual_verify_found_divergence` entry back into the chain (the one self-referential case in this codebase: tampering leaves a permanent, later, in-the-clear marker). `src/orchestration/audit_scheduler.py` runs both an archival sweep (every 300s) and a divergence check (every 3600s) unconditionally — unlike the market-data/paper/live-trading schedulers, audit activity isn't market-hours-gated.
  - **Structured logging + correlation ID, threaded HTTP request → orchestration run → agent action → order** (Build Spec §19's explicit acceptance line): `src/observability/logging.py::configure_logging()` wires `structlog` with `structlog.contextvars.merge_contextvars` as its first processor and `JSONRenderer` — every log line anywhere in the app automatically carries whatever correlation ID is currently bound. `src/observability/correlation.py::CorrelationIdMiddleware` (pure ASGI, not Starlette's `BaseHTTPMiddleware` — deliberately avoiding its historical `call_next`/task-boundary contextvar quirks) reads `X-Correlation-ID` if the caller supplied one, else generates one, binds it via `structlog.contextvars.bind_contextvars`, and echoes it back on the response header. Because Python `contextvars` propagate automatically through anything running synchronously in the same async call tree, **every prior phase's orchestration/agent/order code needed zero changes** to inherit this ID when triggered via HTTP — Phase 2's run creation, Phase 3's LangGraph node execution, and Phase 8/9's order placement all already run inside the same request. The only code that genuinely needed explicit wiring was autonomous, non-HTTP-triggered work: a small reusable `bind_job_correlation_id(job_name)`/`with_job_correlation_id(job_name, fn)` pair, applied at `add_job()` registration time (not inside each job body) across all 6 scheduler files (`paper_trading_scheduler.py`, `live_trading_scheduler.py`, `market_data_scheduler.py`, `audit_scheduler.py`, plus `task_engine.py`'s stall-sweep loop and `agents/scheduler.py`'s heartbeat loop) — every scheduled job tick now gets its own fresh, traceable ID (`job:<job_name>:<uuid>`), the autonomous-work equivalent of one HTTP request.
  - **Verified live, one request traced end-to-end**: `POST /api/v1/agents/pipeline/run` with a caller-supplied `X-Correlation-ID: trace-pipeline-demo-777` produced 18 structlog JSON log lines (every `llm_router.provider_failed` fallback attempt across 3 LLM-backed nodes, plus each node's `agents.graph.llm_unavailable_using_fallback`) all carrying that exact same `correlation_id` field, **and** the audit entry the middleware wrote for that same HTTP request carries the identical value in its `correlation_id` column — proving the HTTP→log and HTTP→audit links are the same ID, not two independently-generated ones.
  - **Blanket audit coverage via a retrofit middleware, not ~40 hand-edited routes**: `src/observability/audit_middleware.py::AuditLoggingMiddleware` (pure ASGI, added innermost so `CorrelationIdMiddleware` wraps it and its `write_audit_entry` call sees the same bound ID) fires for any non-GET/HEAD/OPTIONS request that reaches a real route and returns 2xx, writing a minimal (method+path action, best-effort entity id, status/path/query params) entry — deliberately generic, since it can't know a route's business semantics the way that route's own code does. Coexists with, doesn't replace, the richer domain-specific `write_audit_entry` calls already in Agent Gateway/Shadow Mode/live trading. **Gotcha**: pure ASGI middleware sits outside FastAPI's dependency-injection graph, so it can't use `app.dependency_overrides` for its DB session — reads `request.app.state.db_session_factory` instead, set to the real `AsyncSessionLocal` in `main.py` and overridden/restored per-test by `tests/conftest.py`'s `db_session_factory` fixture. `src/api/deps.py::get_current_user` now also sets `request.state.current_user`, purely so this middleware can extract a real actor email instead of always logging `"anonymous"`.
  - **Export/list/verify API** (`src/api/routes/audit.py`): `GET /audit/entries` and `GET /audit/export` (CSV/NDJSON, filterable by `entity_type`/`entity_id`/`actor`) role-restricted to exactly SystemAdministrator/ReadOnlyAuditor per the spec's literal wording — neither PortfolioManager nor RiskManager can read the trail, even though both can trigger the mutating actions that populate it. `POST /audit/verify` is narrower still (SystemAdministrator-only): it's more than a read, since a genuine divergence finding writes its own audit entry. **Verified live**: a PortfolioManager's `GET /audit/export` returned `403`; SystemAdministrator's CSV and NDJSON exports both returned well-formed output with the right `Content-Disposition`/content-type; a raw `psql UPDATE`/`DELETE` against `audit_log` both failed with the trigger's exact exception text; tampering with the on-disk archive (after clearing `chattr +a`, simulating the "attacker with root + CAP_LINUX_IMMUTABLE" scenario the module's own docstring names) made `POST /audit/verify` correctly report `diverged: true` with the exact first broken sequence, and wrote a real `audit.manual_verify_found_divergence` row back into the chain.
  - **Five Prometheus metrics, exactly the spec's list** (`src/observability/metrics.py`, `GET /metrics` deliberately unauthenticated — the conventional network-level-restricted posture, no RBAC role invented for a scraper): `tradingos_ws_latency_seconds` (histogram), `tradingos_agent_node_duration_seconds` (histogram, labeled `node`), `tradingos_order_dispatch_latency_seconds` (histogram, labeled `broker`) + `tradingos_order_dispatch_budget_breached_total` (counter), `tradingos_llm_token_usage_total` (counter, labeled `provider`/`token_type`), `tradingos_trading_holiday` (gauge, refreshed every 300s off the real `src.data.nse_calendar`). Each wired at the one real choke point its subsystem already funnels through: the agent graph's per-node wrapper closure (`src/agents/graph.py`), `ResilientBrokerAdapter.place_order` (`src/brokers/resilient.py`, also comparing elapsed time against a new `settings.order_dispatch_latency_budget_ms` — **500.0ms, this build's own documented choice**, since the spec names no number), and `LlmRouter.complete`'s success path.
  - **Real LLM token usage, never estimated**: `src/agents/llm_router.py` gained an `LlmCompletionPayload` dataclass and every provider client (Anthropic/OpenAI-compatible/Gemini/Ollama) now parses real `prompt_tokens`/`completion_tokens` from its own actual response shape, `None` — never a fabricated guess — when a provider doesn't report it, matching this codebase's established "never fabricate a metric" rule (same posture as Phase 5's null-correlation handling). A moderate, contained refactor: `LlmCompletionResult` gained the same two optional fields, one test fixture updated to match, zero other regressions.
  - **Real production gap, found and fixed this phase**: Phase 6's `WebSocketLatencyGuard` (`src/engine/risk/latency_guard.py`) was fully built and unit-tested but had **zero real call sites** anywhere in the tick pipeline until now (confirmed via grep — only its own test file referenced it). Wired a module-level instance into `paper_trading_scheduler.py`'s `run_tick_drain_job` tick loop: every drained tick is now genuinely timed, observed against `ws_latency_seconds`, and fed to the guard, which genuinely pauses (drops) further processing for that job tick when latency exceeds threshold — verified both by a new test (`test_tick_drain_job_observes_ws_latency_metric_and_feeds_the_latency_guard`) and live (`/metrics` showing real non-zero `tradingos_ws_latency_seconds_bucket` values after driving ticks through a running server).
  - **Grafana + Prometheus, config genuinely written but not verified end-to-end in this sandbox** (same "real config, honestly unexercised without reachable infra" posture as Phase 5's Temporal, Phase 8's `GvisorSandboxRuntime`): new top-level `observability/` directory — `observability/prometheus/prometheus.yml` (scrapes `backend:8000/metrics` every 15s) + `observability/prometheus/alert_rules.yml` (three rules: `MarketHoursBackendDown`, the spec's explicit "at least the market-hours-downtime alert rule" — approximates NSE's 09:15–15:30 IST/Mon-Fri window in UTC via PromQL's `hour()`/`day_of_week()` time functions, honestly documented as an approximation, not a real holiday-aware calendar; `OrderDispatchLatencyBudgetBreached`; `WebSocketLatencyElevated`), `observability/grafana/provisioning/{datasources,dashboards}/` (auto-provisions the Prometheus datasource and auto-loads `observability/grafana/dashboards/tradingos-overview.json` — 8 panels covering all five metrics plus backend-up). `docker-compose.yml` gained `prometheus`/`grafana` services (own containers, matching Redis/Temporal's precedent of not joining the single backend container) with host ports `PROMETHEUS_HOST_PORT`/`GRAFANA_HOST_PORT` (default 9090/3001 — not 3000, which the frontend already defaults to) published, since both are operator-facing UIs the same way the API/frontend are. `docker compose config --quiet` validated the full compose file cleanly; the actual `prom/prometheus`/`grafana/grafana` image pulls were not attempted (Docker Hub pulls are blocked in this sandbox, the same confirmed-live constraint as every prior phase's Docker service) — please confirm `docker compose up` once in a normal environment.
  - **Two real bugs surfaced by a user's actual `docker compose up -d --build` run on Windows/Docker Desktop, fixed post-merge**: (1) the `grafana` service's volumes bind-mounted `./observability/grafana/dashboards` at `/etc/grafana/provisioning/dashboards/json`, a path *nested inside* the separately-mounted, read-only `/etc/grafana/provisioning/dashboards` — Docker Desktop for Windows cannot create that nested mountpoint inside an already-`:ro` mount (`error mounting ...: read-only file system`), even though the identical config was never exercised against real Docker Hub pulls in this sandbox (`docker compose config --quiet` only validates config shape, not runtime mount behavior). Fixed by moving the dashboard JSON to live directly under `observability/grafana/provisioning/dashboards/json/` on the host — a single mount now covers both the `dashboards.yml` provisioning file and the JSON it references, no nesting. (2) `scripts/docker-up.js`/`docker-up.ps1`'s port-conflict retry logic matched `TradingOS-2\.0-Frontend` against the *entire* captured `docker compose up` output, not just the line naming the actual failing container — since `docker compose up`'s own output lists every service (including the frontend) transitioning through Creating/Starting/Started regardless of which one's port conflicted, a genuine Prometheus-port (9090) conflict was silently misattributed to the frontend and only `FRONTEND_HOST_PORT` ever got bumped, looping through several frontend ports while the real conflict went unaddressed. Fixed in both scripts by isolating the specific `port is already allocated` line before checking which container it names. Neither bug was caught by this repo's own test suite or `docker compose config` validation — both are runtime-only failure modes that only a real `docker compose up` against a real Docker daemon can surface, exactly the gap the "please confirm once in a normal environment" note above was flagging.
  - **Follow-up: the same misattribution fix wasn't the whole story** — re-running the fixed scripts against the same machine surfaced that the attribution logic was only ever wired to bump `API_HOST_PORT`/`FRONTEND_HOST_PORT`; a genuine Prometheus-port (9090) conflict was now correctly *identified* (no longer misattributed to the frontend) but the scripts had no `PROMETHEUS_HOST_PORT`/`GRAFANA_HOST_PORT` retry path at all, so they honestly aborted with "could not tell which service" instead of self-healing. Both `docker-up.js` and `docker-up.ps1` were refactored from two hardcoded backend/frontend variables to a table-driven list of all four published-port services (backend/frontend/prometheus/grafana), each with its own container name, container-internal port, env var, and compose service name — the conflict-attribution, not-actually-published, and success-reporting logic now all iterate that table generically, so any of the four ports self-heals the same way backend/frontend already did, and a fifth service would only need one new table entry. Verified end-to-end against a fake `docker` CLI shim (a local sandbox has no real Docker daemon reachable) simulating the user's exact observed sequence — a frontend port conflict, then a Prometheus port conflict, then success — for both scripts, plus separately verified the "exit 0 but port didn't actually publish" retry path and the "conflict on a port this script doesn't manage" honest-abort path, all passing in both the PowerShell and Node implementations.
  - **Every mutating action from every prior phase now audited, confirmed by the full regression suite**: adding the blanket middleware plus the `get_current_user`/`main.py`/`conftest.py` wiring introduced **zero regressions** — the full suite went from 496 (post-Phase-10 baseline) to 548 passing (52 new Phase 11 tests: pure hash-chain functions, the advisory-lock writer's gap-free sequencing under concurrency, the DB-level UPDATE/DELETE trigger rejection, `verify_db_chain`'s tamper detection, the WORM archive's append/sweep/divergence checks including a deliberately tampered copy, the export/list/verify API's role restrictions, the correlation middleware's header echo, the audit middleware firing on a real mutating request and staying silent on GETs/failures/anonymous vs. authenticated actors, metrics exposition format, and the order-dispatch-latency/budget-breach and WS-latency-guard wiring).
  - **Verified live end-to-end** against a real running `uvicorn` process, matching every piece of this phase's stated acceptance criteria: a raw `psql UPDATE`/`DELETE` against `audit_log` both failed at the DB level with the trigger's exact exception text; a deliberately tampered archive copy was correctly flagged by `POST /audit/verify` (`diverged: true`, exact sequence named) and wrote its own permanent divergence-marker audit entry; `GET /audit/export` correctly returned `403` for a PortfolioManager and well-formed CSV/NDJSON for a SystemAdministrator; `GET /metrics` served all five metrics unauthenticated; and one HTTP request (`POST /agents/pipeline/run` with a caller-supplied correlation ID) was traced end-to-end through 18 real structlog log lines across three different LangGraph nodes **and** its own audit-log row, all carrying the identical correlation ID — the literal acceptance-criteria sentence, demonstrated, not assumed. All demo users, the demo archive directory, and the tamper cleaned up afterward; the append-only audit rows those demo actions produced were, correctly, left in place (they can never be deleted) and confirmed still present after the demo users themselves were removed.
- [x] Phase 12 — Notifications & Omni-Channel — real inbound/outbound Telegram/Discord/Slack wiring (Build Spec §18), replacing the Phase 3 `notification-send` skill stub with genuine network I/O in both directions. Inbound: `src/api/routes/webhooks.py` (deliberately **not** RBAC-gated — signature verification IS the auth mechanism, one real scheme per channel matching that channel's actual protocol: Telegram's `X-Telegram-Bot-Api-Secret-Token` constant-time compare, Discord's Ed25519 signature over `timestamp+body` via `cryptography.hazmat`, Slack's HMAC-SHA256 over `v0:{timestamp}:{body}` with its 300s replay-tolerance window folded into the signature check itself), each additionally replay-guarded (`src/notifications/replay_guard.py`, a Redis `SET NX EX` per `(channel, message_id)` — proves not just "signed by the channel" but "this exact message hasn't been processed before") and rate-limited (fixed-window `INCR`/`EXPIRE` per `(channel, sender_id)`). A verified sender's message is deterministically classified (`src/notifications/classify.py`, a small fixed set of imperative lead verbs — not LLM-judged, reviewable, no ambiguity) into either a real persisted `OrganizationRun(source=RunSource.WEBHOOK)` via Phase 2's own `run_control.create_run` (not Phase 3's separate LangGraph demo endpoint — the two stay deliberately parallel systems in this codebase) or a synchronous CEO Agent reply via `LlmRouter.complete`, sent back over the same channel. Outbound: a single choke point `src/notifications/dispatch.py::notify(level, *, title, body, details)` fans out, best-effort per channel (never raises), to every channel subscribed to one of the **four fixed alert levels** (`kill-switch`, `sign-off`, `go-live`, `daily`) mirrored verbatim from the frontend's own closed, authoritative `app/settings/page.tsx` `ALERT_LEVELS` constant — not an open-ended list invented independently. Wired at the exact real event-transition points, not generic call sites: `check_drawdown` fires only on the genuine `False→True` kill-switch trip (never re-fires on an already-tripped subsequent check); `create_approval_request` fires for every sign-off item, covering both strategy promotion and live-eligibility requests; `generate_live_order_intent`'s `pending_approval` (non-batch) path fires its own sign-off alert per the spec's explicit "including live order intents from Phase 9" wording; `approve_strategy_for_live_eligibility` fires the moment the Go-Live Readiness Gate itself returns eligible, independent of whether the separate approval-transition check below it also happens to succeed. `daily` is the one level with no single triggering event — a new `src/orchestration/notification_scheduler.py` cron job (18:00 IST, unconditional like Phase 11's audit scheduler) summarizes tripped kill-switch modes, pending approvals, and pending live intents. New encrypted `NotificationChannelStore` (`src/notifications/channel_store.py`, same Fernet-encrypted-JSON-file pattern as Phase 8's `SecretsStore`, deliberately a separate file under the same encryption key rather than folded into it, since the two domains have genuinely different shapes) is **deny-by-default**: both `allowed_sender_ids` and `alert_levels` default to empty lists, an operator must explicitly opt a channel into receiving inbound messages or outbound alert levels. Real `httpx`-calling senders (`src/notifications/senders.py`, injectable `transport`, Phase 8's broker-adapter convention) for all three channels, genuinely exercised live against a local mock receiver in this sandbox (see below) since Discord/Slack take a caller-configured `webhook_url` (Telegram's URL is fixed to `api.telegram.org` and its real API was correspondingly left honestly unexercised, same "real code, no egress in this sandbox" posture as every other external-API module here). Separately: `LlmRouter` gained a `preferred_provider` param (prepended, deduplicated, ahead of the live Gateway fallback order — never provider-or-nothing, the full safety-net chain stays intact) and a genuine `stream_complete` async generator — real hand-parsed Anthropic SSE for that one provider (the "pick one to be authentically real" precedent from Phase 8/10), every other provider's ordinary complete response word-chunked into a simulated stream, with an `any_yielded` guard so a mid-stream failure after real content has reached the caller re-raises rather than silently splicing in a different provider. New in-app chat (`src/orchestration/chat.py`, `src/api/routes/chat.py`, `ChatSession`/`ChatMessage` models): real SSE streaming straight off `stream_complete`, a Redis abort flag checked between every yielded chunk (a partial reply is still persisted, marked `aborted=True`, never silently discarded), per-session model switch via `preferred_provider` (a private `Unset` sentinel distinguishes "leave model unchanged" from "clear to auto" since a bare `None` default can't express both), title-or-message-content search, pin, and markdown/JSON export. 85 new tests (`tests/test_notifications_*.py`, `tests/test_outbound_alerts_wiring.py` — the explicit "alerts actually fire for kill-switch/sign-off/go-live" test requirement — `tests/test_webhooks_api.py`, `tests/test_notification_channels_api.py`, `tests/test_agents_tools_notification.py`, 7 new `tests/test_agents_llm_router.py` cases, `tests/test_chat_api.py`, `tests/test_chat_orchestration_abort.py` — a dedicated hand-driven-generator test proving the real Redis abort-flag check stops the stream mid-chunk and persists only the pre-abort text, since the ASGI test client buffers whole SSE responses and can't practically pause a live request to call the abort endpoint in between chunks) — 624 total, zero regressions.
  - **Two real production routing bugs, caught only by running the new chat routes live, not by the test suite as originally written**: (1) `src/api/routes/chat.py`'s router was mounted with `prefix="/chat"`, but every `register_policy(...)` call (and the test file itself) addressed `/api/v1/chat/sessions/...` — since RBAC matches on FastAPI's exact `route.path` template (`src/core/rbac.py`), every chat request actually landed on the wrong route pattern entirely (`POST /chat/sessions` resolved against the `/chat/{session_id}` pattern with `session_id="sessions"`, a 405 Method Not Allowed, not even reaching RBAC). **Fixed** by changing the router's own prefix to `/chat/sessions` to match what its own policy table and every test always assumed — the sub-resource shape (`/chat/sessions`, `/chat/sessions/{id}`, `/chat/sessions/{id}/messages`) was the intended design throughout, just never actually wired. (2) Once routing was fixed, `GET /chat/sessions/{id}/messages` still 403'd for every role — its own `register_policy` entry was simply missing from the table (present for POST, absent for GET on the identical path) — a real "programming error, not an open door: it default-denies" case, exactly as `require_role`'s own docstring warns. **Fixed** by adding the missing `register_policy("GET", ...)` line. Neither bug was caught by `tests/test_chat_api.py` as first drafted, because that file was written against the intended API shape and only run for the first time during this phase's live-verification pass — a reminder that a test file exercising routes that don't yet correctly route will still fail loudly (it did — 9 of 11 tests failed on first run), just not with a signal that immediately says "the prefix is wrong."
  - **Redis test-isolation issues discovered and fixed, not by changing production code**: `src.core.redis_client.get_redis()` is a process-wide `@lru_cache`d singleton, which collides with pytest-asyncio's per-test event loops once a test file makes several real Redis calls in sequence (`RuntimeError: Event loop is closed`, order/volume-dependent, not reproducible on every Redis-touching test file) — fixed locally in `tests/test_webhooks_api.py` and `tests/test_chat_api.py` via an autouse fixture overriding `app.dependency_overrides[get_redis]` to the test's own already-event-loop-scoped `redis_client` fixture, not by modifying the shared `src/core/redis_client.py` module. Separately, the real, un-flushed, shared Redis instance in this sandbox (no test-specific Redis DB, unlike Postgres's `tradingos_test`) retained `notify:*` keys with fixed literal ids across repeated manual test-file runs within the same debugging session, causing false "replayed" failures on a test's supposed first call — fixed via a second autouse fixture in the affected files that deletes all `notify:*`-prefixed keys before each test (narrowly scoped, not a blunt `flushdb()`).
  - **Verified live end-to-end** against a real running `uvicorn` process, matching every piece of this phase's stated acceptance criteria: started a local mock HTTP receiver and configured the Slack channel's real `webhook_url` to point at it (Discord/Slack both take a caller-configured URL, unlike Telegram's fixed `api.telegram.org` endpoint, so this is genuine unmocked loopback network I/O through the real `httpx`-calling sender, not a transport injection); **tripped the live kill switch via a real drawdown-breach observation and received a real Slack webhook POST** (`[KILL-SWITCH] Kill switch tripped (live)...`) at the mock receiver within the same request; drove a full strategy through promotion (real sign-off alert delivered), live-eligibility request/approval (real sign-off alert), and the Go-Live Readiness Gate passing (real go-live alert delivered, independent confirmation that `status` genuinely transitioned to `LiveEligible`); enrolled the strategy in live trading, ran real daily signal generation against the live SMA-crossover engine to produce a genuine BUY signal, and **called the real `generate-intent` endpoint — the live order intent appearing produced a real outbound Slack notification** (`[SIGN-OFF] Live order intent pending approval: ALERTDEMO...`) delivered to the mock receiver, not just a UI badge — the literal acceptance-criteria sentence, demonstrated; separately verified the inbound webhook path end-to-end against the live server: a forged Telegram secret token was rejected `401`, a correctly-signed request from a sender not on the allowlist was rejected `403`, a verified sender's objective message (`"run a backtest for ALERTDEMO"`) returned `200` and produced a real, persisted `OrganizationRun(source="webhook")` that ran to completion, and replaying the identical `update_id` was rejected `401`; and confirmed the in-app chat's real SSE streaming, persistence, and markdown export all worked against the live server end to end. All demo rows (4 demo users, the strategy + version + subscription + live order intent + live position + approval requests, the webhook-spawned `OrganizationRun` and its plans/tasks/events, chat sessions/messages, both notification channel configs, and the local `secrets/notification_channels.enc` file) cleaned up afterward and confirmed removed; both kill switches confirmed untripped.

`GET /orders` (new read-only aggregator normalizing Phase 7's `PaperFill` and Phase 9's `Order`/`Trade` into one shape — no "latency" field fabricated, that KPI is a real Prometheus histogram, not a per-row column); `GET/PUT /gateway/config`, `POST /gateway/config/validate`, `GET /gateway/config/versions`, `POST /gateway/config/versions/{id}/rollback` (new thin HTTP front for the CLI's own `src.gateway.service`/`apply` functions — a PUT goes through the identical atomic-write-then-`apply_config_text` pipeline the CLI already uses, never a second mutation path); `GET /market-data/market-hours` (thin wrapper around the already-real `is_market_open_ist`, which had zero HTTP callers before this); `GET /risk-limits/changes` (lists `RiskLimitChangeRequest` rows so a dual-control change is visible to the *other* user who must confirm it — the stage/confirm/apply flow existed but had no way for a second user to discover a pending change). `RunResponse`, `StrategyVersionResponse`, `BacktestRunResponse`, `ApprovalRequestResponse`, `RiskLimitChangeResponse` each gained a few fields (timestamps, `code`, `daily_returns`, `trade_pnls`) that already existed on their ORM models but were never exposed — additive only, every existing `model_validate(orm_obj)` call site kept working unchanged. 5 new WebSocket channels (`src/api/ws_auth.py`, `src/api/routes/websockets.py`), each backed by a real existing mechanism, not a new event-logging system: `activity-feed`/`agent-logs` DB-poll the real hash-chained `audit_log` table (1.5s interval, 30-row backfill); `organization-events` is a real Redis `psubscribe` onto `organization-events:*` — `src.orchestration.events.emit()` has published there since Phase 2 but had zero WS consumer until now; `sign-off-queue` DB-polls pending `ApprovalRequest`+`LiveOrderIntent` rows every 2s, pushing `server_time` alongside each real `expires_at` so the frontend countdown is anchored to the server clock, never a client-started timer; `ticks` is `redis.xread(..., block=2000)` against Phase 7's real per-symbol tick stream. Auth is `?token=` query param (browsers can't set WS headers), a deliberate, narrow, documented exception to the codebase's otherwise-universal `require_role` HTTP convention.
  - **Frontend data layer**: `lib/api.ts` (fetch wrapper — JWT in `localStorage`, single-flight refresh-on-401 so concurrent 401s don't each fire their own refresh and race the backend's single-use refresh-token rotation, typed functions per domain built up incrementally as each page was wired) and `lib/ws.ts` (`useWebSocketChannel` hook, exponential-backoff reconnect, token re-read from storage on every reconnect attempt). `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL`/`NEXT_PUBLIC_WS_URL` added to `.env.example`, the root `Dockerfile` (as build-time `ARG`s — `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` vars are inlined into the client bundle at build time, not read at container start, so they must thread through as `ARG`→`ENV` before `pnpm build`, not just container `ENV`) and `docker-compose.yml`'s frontend `build.args`, defaulting to `http://localhost:8000`/`ws://localhost:8000` to match `API_HOST_PORT`'s own default.
  - **Auth**: `components/auth/auth-provider.tsx` rewritten for real `POST /auth/login` + `GET /auth/me` (login itself carries no role — a separate `/auth/me` call learns it), tokens persisted across refresh. a real "dispatch a new objective" form since the acceptance criteria requires creating a run from the UI); Strategies (real 7-stage lifecycle board, real generated `code` in the Code tab, the suggestion→AI-review→regenerate flow wired to the real endpoints, real Go-Live Readiness gate);
