Backtests (real runs, equity curve compounded from the real `daily_returns` series, real trade P&L ledger from `trade_pnls`, real Monte Carlo trigger, real multi-run correlation compare — walk-forward and fresh-run creation honestly gated behind "requires OHLCV bars this console doesn't collect," a pre-existing backend gap, not fabricated); Orders & Trades (real unified ledger, real live-intent history, manual order entry rewired to `POST /risk/order-intents` — the real risk-gated intent pipeline, not a fabricated direct-place-order endpoint); Audit Log (real hash-chain entries, export, and on-demand verify); Settings (real Gateway config raw-JSON editor + version rollback, real skill grant matrix, real broker-credential write-only cards — LLM-provider cards dropped, no runtime credential-store endpoint exists for them — real notification channels matching the frontend's own `ALERT_LEVELS` ids exactly, real dual-control risk-limit stage/confirm/apply); Market Analysis (real VIX/sector/global pulse, real per-symbol data-freshness lookup, real static option-instrument master — Technical Indicators, Provider Status, and live option-chain OI/IV/LTP left as explicit in-UI gap notices, since no backend endpoint computes indicators, reports LLM/broker health, or serves live greeks); command palette's `SEARCH_ENTITIES` replaced with entities fetched live from `GET /agents`/`GET /strategies`/`GET /orders` on open, `lib/search-index.ts` now carries only real nav metadata.

Fixed in `lib/ws.ts` by prefixing every channel URL with `/api/v1`.

All demo users and the live-trading test rows are harmless dev-DB residue (not cleaned up, matching this sandbox's disposable-DB convention); the one accidental side effect — the local dev server's `audit_archive/` WORM file picking up real entries from this live session and colliding with the separately-DB-isolated test suite's own archive-divergence checks on the next `pytest` run — was diagnosed (both point at the same relative default path when run from the same `backend/` cwd) and cleared (`chattr -a` then delete, since the WORM archive is append-only even to root by design), restoring a clean 624/624.
- [x] Phase 14 — Security & Testing Hardening Pass (Build Spec §21-22) — 8-item pass the operator required before connecting any real capital behind the human-approval flow: adversarial sandbox testing, mutation testing on the safety layer, an RBAC contract-test sweep, an Agent Gateway network-binding check, a WCAG 2.1 AA accessibility pass, a real end-to-end Playwright suite, a WebSocket fan-out load smoke test, and this checklist update. Every item found and fixed at least one real, previously-shipped gap rather than confirming existing behavior only — treated as blockers, not follow-ups, per the operator's own framing.
  - **Adversarial sandbox escape (`beaee68`):** generated strategy code could fully escape every declared restriction using only ordinary Python attribute access — no banned import or call name needed. `__import__.__globals__["_real_import"]("os")` recovered the real unrestricted `__import__` (confirmed live: unrestricted `os.listdir("/")` and file reads outside the sandbox); `open.__closure__[i].cell_contents` recovered the real unrestricted `open`; `().__class__.__base__.__subclasses__()` walked to any already-loaded class with no import at all. Neither `validation.py`'s AST ban-list nor `restricted_exec.py`'s runtime guards checked for dunder attribute access or `getattr`/`setattr`/`delattr`/`vars`/`globals`/`locals`. Fixed at the AST level in both `validation.py` (pre-execution) and `worker_main.py` (immediately before `exec()`, genuine defense-in-depth, not just one gate) — all four attack variants now rejected, legitimate pandas/numpy strategy code unaffected. gVisor itself (the production sandbox runtime) still couldn't be exercised here — no reachable Docker daemon in this sandbox, same documented gap as the rest of this project's infra — but `RestrictedProcessSandboxRuntime`, what this fix targets, is what actually runs in this repo's own dev/CI environment per its own docstring.
  - **Mutation testing (`96b717a`):** a real `mutmut` toolchain (pinned to 2.x — 3.8.0 has an internal bug on any package literally named `src`) scoped to Kill Switch, Compliance Checker, and Correlation Constraint via `pyproject.toml`'s `[tool.mutmut]`. Closed every real survivor: `kill_switch.py` had an `or`/`and` logic inversion in the tripped-error reason-fallback message; `orchestration/kill_switch.py` had a serious `and`/`or` inversion in the newly-tripped guard that would have tripped on every call regardless of actual drawdown, plus a `notify()` body inversion that would always show generic fallback text instead of the real computed percentages; `compliance.py` had a `* 100.0` → `* 101.0` scaling error in both the position-limit and circuit-band percentage math; `correlation_constraint.py` had `<`/`<=` and `>`/`>=` boundary gaps. `live_trading.py::expire_stale_intents` isn't mutmut-covered (bulk-UPDATE code) so got hand-driven boundary analysis instead. Cosmetic survivors (dataclass frozen/slots flags, error-message text) deliberately left alone.
  - **RBAC contract-test sweep (`6e0b17b`):** existing RBAC tests were feature-specific spot checks, not systematic — a new mutating endpoint shipped without RBAC wiring would only be caught if someone remembered to test it. Added two sweeps against the real live app and real live policy table: every mutating (POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE) route either has a registered policy or is on an explicit, documented exemption list (auth bootstrap + signature-verified webhooks only — a stale exemption for a route that no longer exists also fails); and every one of the (then-)57 registered mutating policies gets a real request through `require_role` for each of the 4 roles, confirming an allowed role passes and every other role gets exactly 403.
  - **Agent Gateway network-binding (`a5b17c9`):** `docker-compose.yml` published the backend (the entire Agent Gateway config/CLI-mutation API), frontend, Prometheus, and Grafana on every host interface by default — Docker's short `"HOST:CONTAINER"` port syntax binds `0.0.0.0` unless a host IP is given explicitly — reachable from any other machine on the network, protected only by app-level RBAC/JWT (or, for Prometheus, nothing at all; Grafana ships `admin`/`admin` until changed). All four now bind through a single overridable `${BIND_HOST:-127.0.0.1}`, loopback-only by default. New `test_docker_compose_network_binding.py` reads the real compose file and fails on any port mapping missing the `BIND_HOST` prefix or any literal `0.0.0.0`.
  - **Accessibility / WCAG 2.1 AA (`453f90c`, `1d40173`):** measured every palette accent and theme-swap token against its real background via the actual sRGB relative-luminance formula, not eyeballed. Found and fixed two genuine AA contrast failures (the "neutral" palette's `#6b7280` accent at 4.17:1 against dark backgrounds, under the 4.5:1 text minimum, replaced with `#94a3b8` at 7.87:1; the light palette's `--muted-foreground` at 4.43:1, replaced with `#475569` at 6.95:1) and one palette (Contrast theme) that swapped its accent but left hairline borders/dividers at ~1:1, nowhere near the 3:1 non-text minimum — bumped `--border`/`--input` alphas to ~3.7:1/~3.2:1. Reduced-motion support was already correct (OS media query + manual mirror), no change needed. Separately, Mission Control's kanban cards conveyed run status by color alone (WCAG 1.4.1) with no accompanying text, which mattered because `columnForRun()` merges `completed`/`failed`/`cannot_plan` into one "Live / Done" column — a colorblind or forced-grayscale user couldn't tell a failed run from a completed one on the board. Added `runStatusLabel()`. Audited every other status indicator on the frontend (orders, agent fleet, strategies, backtests, analysis, audit, shell nav) and found the rest already pair color with text/icon/title — no further changes needed.
  - **End-to-end Playwright suite (`801bdfc`, `a6eff8c`):** four specs (`e2e/`) driving the real Next.js frontend against the real FastAPI backend, real Postgres, real Redis — no mocked network — covering the full strategy lifecycle through both sign-off decisions, the live-order-intent sign-off queue's approve and sweep-driven-expiry paths, a real kill-switch trip-and-reset with RBAC-correct visibility, and Agent Gateway hot-reload rejecting an intentionally invalid hand-edit while staying on the last-known-good config. Writing these against the real app (not around it) surfaced three real, previously-shipped gaps, each fixed so the specs could pass honestly: (1) the kill switch could be tripped but never reset through the UI — `resetKillSwitch` was exported from `lib/api.ts` but called from nowhere; added a reset control to the Overview console, RBAC-gated the same as the backend endpoint. (2) The Go-Live Readiness Gate requires both `live_win_rate` AND `backtest_win_rate` non-null, but the Strategies page's readiness form had no field for the latter at all — live-eligibility sign-off could never be approved through the browser no matter what an operator entered; added the missing input. (3) `GET /gateway/config` re-read and re-validated the on-disk config file on every call instead of serving the same in-memory `GatewayState` every other agent-facing call site reads — after a hand-edit with invalid JSON5, the Settings page (exactly where an operator would look to confirm the app is fine) returned a 500 instead of showing the still-running last-known-good config, directly contradicting that file's own top-of-file promise to the operator. This was the most significant finding of the whole pass; fixed to serve `version_id`/`parsed` from `GatewayState` and `raw_text` from the DB's active `AgentConfigVersion` row, with a permanent regression test (the endpoint previously had zero coverage). Fixture-seeding scripts (`backend/scripts/seed_e2e_users.py`, `seed_e2e_live_intent.py`) use the real `/auth/register`-equivalent ORM path with real Argon2 hashing, documented in their own docstrings as fixture setup, never a production credential-bootstrap mechanism, honoring this project's no-plaintext-credentials rule.
  - **WebSocket fan-out load smoke test (`2adb021`):** `scripts/ws-fanout-load-smoke-test.mjs` opens real, bounded, promptly-closed connections against both DB-polling Console channels (`sign-off-queue`, `activity-feed`) with per-channel message-count expectations reflecting each channel's real design (periodic full-snapshot broadcaster vs. backfill-then-delta stream). The first run reproducibly exhausted SQLAlchemy's default connection pool (size 5, overflow 10 — 15 total) with as few as ~30 concurrent Console connections — a handful of browser tabs, not an exotic load — timing out every HTTP route, not just WebSockets, until enough connections closed. Fixed by sizing the pool with headroom over that measured failure point (`pool_size=20, max_overflow=20` in `src/core/db.py`). Verifying that fix against the full test suite surfaced a second, latent issue: the audit archive's default path is relative to the process cwd, shared between pytest and any manually-run dev server started from the same directory, so this same load test's own traffic against a real running backend polluted the WORM archive that `POST /api/v1/audit/verify`'s tests diff the DB chain against, producing a false "diverged" reading with nothing to do with the pool fix. Fixed permanently (not just cleared by hand, which the append-only archive's own tooling correctly refuses to let an agent do casually) by isolating the archive path per test in `tests/conftest.py`'s `client` fixture, the same way it already isolates the database. Full backend suite: 717/717 passing after both fixes.

  - **Gotcha, caught by this PR's own tests, not by review — a process-wide `@lru_cache` singleton silently escaping a `Depends` override**: `src.agents.llm_router.default_clients()` reads the LLM provider store via its own direct call to `get_llm_provider_store()` (a module-level `@lru_cache`, mirroring Phase 8's `get_secrets_store()`), not through FastAPI's dependency-injection graph — so a test that overrode only `llm_providers.py`'s own `Depends(...)` target (`_get_store`) for the CRUD endpoints left the `/test` and `/models` endpoints, which call `default_clients()` directly, still reading whatever real store the process had cached. Fixed by having `_override_store` in `test_llm_providers_api.py` patch both: the `Depends` target, and the name as imported into `src.agents.llm_router` — documented in that test file's own docstring as "the same 'patch where it's used' fix already applied to the analogous `src.api.routes.broker_oauth` bug this suite caught earlier" (per this PR's own description, the full suite was run twice — once before, once after fixing this isolation bug). **Lesson for any future encrypted-file-backed store with an `@lru_cache`'d accessor:** overriding a route's own `Depends` target is not enough if another module calls the accessor function directly — every real call site needs to be found and patched, not just the one the route layer exposes.

Fixed for the new endpoint by converting `agents.py`'s `_config_path` into a real `Depends()`-injected dependency, overridable in tests the same way `get_data_lake_root` already is in `tests/test_market_data_api.py`. The schema-supported per-agent `model` field was deliberately left unwired (no "Model-Routing" tab) — traced through `loader.py` → `graph.py`/`llm_router.py` and confirmed it's never actually read at LLM-call time, so an editable-looking control over it would itself be a fabrication this codebase's own conventions exist to prevent. Frontend: `app/agent-fleet/page.tsx`'s Identity tab is now a real editable form (disabled unless `SystemAdministrator`) calling the new identity endpoint directly rather than the generic whole-config-PUT `patchAgentEntry` mechanism Skills/Heartbeat/Enable-Disable use, for real per-field validation and explicit 404s; a new Prompt Versions tab lists history, creates drafts, and activates/rolls back versions, showing the real computed diff on the active version. 12 new tests in `backend/tests/test_agents_api.py` (identity partial-update semantics, RBAC, 404s, prompt-version listing/ordering/creation/activation/supersession); full backend suite: 886 passed; `ruff format --check`/`ruff check` clean; `tsc --noEmit` clean.
  - **Settings follow-up round: broker redirect URL, LLM `"auto"` resolution, Hugging Face, a live-refresh bug.** Reported live against the real `D:\TradingOS-2.0\TradingOS-2.0` stack, fixed the same session. (1) **Broker Config's redirect/callback URL was a chicken-and-egg trap**: it only appeared after clicking Connect, which needs a saved API key — but Zerodha/Upstox only issue a key *after* the redirect URL is registered in their developer console. `GET /api/v1/broker-credentials` now always reports the effective URL (`default_redirect_uri`/`redirect_uri_is_custom` added to `BrokerCredentialStatusResponse`), editable via a new `PUT /broker-credentials/{broker}/redirect-uri` (validated: only the origin may change, the path must still end at that broker's real callback route) or saved together with the first key. Post-login now lands on a new `FRONTEND_BASE_URL` setting instead of this backend's own `/settings`, which 404s in the multi-container stack. 10 new tests, `backend/tests/test_broker_redirect_uri.py`. (2) **`model: "auto"` was never translated** — every real agent call routes with it literally, so a configured provider was asked for a model named "auto." New `src.agents.llm_router.resolve_model`: the provider's stored `default_model` (new field on `LlmProviderCredentials`, settable via `PUT /settings/llm-providers/{provider}/default-model`), else `HOSTED_DEFAULT_MODELS`, else that provider fails over — Ollama/Custom/Hugging Face have no built-in default and are skipped with a warning until one is set. The router singleton previously built its clients once at first use; it now re-reads clients and default models from the store on every call, so a key saved in Settings reaches the next agent call without a restart. New provider **Hugging Face** (`LlmProvider.HUGGINGFACE`, Inference Providers' OpenAI-compatible `router.huggingface.co/v1`). `src.gateway.loader.parse_config_text` also rejoins escaped UTF-16 surrogate pairs (`json.dumps`'s encoding of the CEO agent's 🧠), which previously 500'd `PUT /gateway/config` for any scripted client sending real JSON (the browser sends the emoji literally, so the UI itself never hit this). 17 new tests (`backend/tests/test_llm_auto_model_and_huggingface.py`) plus 3 more in `test_gateway_loader.py` for the surrogate-pair fix. (3) **Settings status strip didn't refresh after a save** — `StatusStrip` fetched broker/LLM/channel status once on mount with no way to learn a card elsewhere on the page had just changed something; a saved Slack/Discord connection showed "active" on its own card but stayed "not configured" on the strip until a manual page reload. Fixed with a shared `statusRefreshKey`/`bumpStatus` on `SettingsPage`, threaded into `BrokerConfigSection`/`LlmProvidersSection`/`NotificationsSection`'s own `reload()`, so any of the three re-fetches the strip immediately. No new backend tests (pure frontend data-flow); `tsc --noEmit` clean. **Verified**: Ollama saved as default `qwen2.5:0.5b`, its `/test` passes with no `?model=` (uses the stored default); a real `get_llm_router().complete(model="auto")` was served by DeepSeek with the operator's key, and by Ollama when pinned as the chat session's preferred provider; the emoji round-trips through a real `PUT /gateway/config` intact. **Not yet independently re-verified in a logged-in browser by this session**: the StatusStrip fix's actual on-screen behavior (rebuilt and deployed to the running stack; reasoned through and traced end-to-end in code, but the live click-through is the user's own next check, same as every prior in-browser-only verification this file has been honest about deferring). Full detail: `docs/phase17-realworld-testing.md`.
  - **GitHub Actions CI, added where none existed before.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: a `backend` job (Postgres 16 + Redis 7 service containers, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check`, `pytest`) and a `frontend` job (`pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` — run directly rather than via `next build`, since `next.config.mjs` sets `typescript.ignoreBuildErrors: true` and would never catch a type error). **A real gap found and fixed before this ever ran for real**: `ubuntu-latest` is now Ubuntu 24.04, which restricts unprivileged user namespaces via AppArmor by default (`kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns=1`) — confirmed live (reproduced the identical `unshare: unshare failed: Operation not permitted` failure the Docker-seccomp fix above already solved once, this time via a completely different mechanism: pytest runs as a bare process in CI, not inside the backend image, so it's the kernel/AppArmor restriction that has to be lifted, not Docker's seccomp profile) and confirmed against multiple independent sources (Ubuntu's own blog announcing the restriction, `actions/runner-images` issues, Chromium's own docs hitting the identical problem for its own sandbox) that `sudo sysctl -w kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns=0` is the standard fix — added as a step before the test run, with the reasoning recorded in the workflow file's own comments. **Verified**: the frontend job passes end-to-end in a clean `node:24-alpine` container against the real repo. The backend job was verified in pieces — `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean, and the sandbox-dependent tests (`test_engine_sandbox.py`, `test_strategies_api.py`) confirmed to fail with exactly the AppArmor-shaped error without the fix and pass with the equivalent Docker-seccomp restriction lifted — but a single, uninterrupted full-suite run under these exact conditions was not completed in this pass (the closest attempt was contaminated by a stale Postgres connection left by an earlier interrupted run, and a second clean attempt was itself interrupted); a genuinely clean, complete run is still owed before this checkbox can honestly read done. That CI follow-up work (`docs/phase20-old-vs-new-comparison.md` items 28–30: gitleaks, mypy `--strict`, Bandit, pip-audit, Playwright-in-CI, GHCR image publishing) has since all been built — see the later entries below; the only things in CI still missing are a per-module coverage gate and scoped mutation testing.
  - **Part 4 follow-up (2026-09-29) — the tick-source-needs-a-restart gap, named but left open at the second Settings diagnosis above, now closed.** That pass's own note ("the tick source is resolved once at startup, so a broker connected in Settings needs a backend restart before paper trading uses it") documented a real gap in `build_tick_source()`'s call site (`src/main.py`'s lifespan captured its return value once and froze it into `start_paper_trading_scheduler`'s fixed `tick_source` job arg) without fixing it — this pass closes it. Real blocker checked first: NSE market was closed at the time (18:58 IST, past the 15:30 close), so Phase 17 Part 4's live-market-hours smoke test itself still needs the next real trading session, and completing real broker OAuth / a real notification test message both still need the operator directly at the keyboard — none of those attempted here. What *is* independent of both: this scheduler-staleness bug, closed as its own fix. **The naive fix (rebuild on every tick-publish firing) would have been wrong**: `MockTickSource`'s per-symbol random walk (`_rng_by_symbol`/`_last_price_by_symbol`) is unbacked instance state — rebuilding it every 3 seconds would silently reset every mock price back to `default_base_price` forever instead of actually walking, the same class of regression as always constructing a fresh object where continuity matters. The real fix, `get_tick_source()`/`_RefreshingTickSource` (`src/brokers/tick_source.py`): caches the last-built `TickSource`, keyed on a fingerprint of `(broker_name, BrokerCredentials)` read from the secrets store, and only calls `build_tick_source()` again when that fingerprint has actually changed — catching a broker connect, a disconnect, *and* a same-broker credential re-save (`BrokerCredentials` is a frozen dataclass, so `==` is real structural equality, not identity), while leaving the real per-broker `BrokerCircuitBreaker` (resolved from `breaker_registry`'s shared registry inside `build_configured_adapter`, confirmed already safe to re-resolve repeatedly since it was never a fresh instance per call to begin with — Phase 16 wiring audit follow-up A) untouched in between. `run_tick_publish_job`/`start_paper_trading_scheduler` (`src/orchestration/paper_trading_scheduler.py`) now take `tick_source_factory: Callable[[], TickSource]` instead of a fixed `tick_source: TickSource`, called fresh on every 3-second firing; `src/main.py` passes `get_tick_source` itself (not `get_tick_source()`) as that factory. This also closes part of the staleness class `docs/phase20-old-vs-new-comparison.md` item 21 flagged more broadly ("REGRESSED (partial) — Agent run lifecycle controls") and directly unblocks Part 4's live-tick/live-option-chain checks the moment a real broker is actually connected — still the operator's own next step, not something this pass could complete without real credentials. 4 new tests (`backend/tests/test_brokers_tick_source.py`): the cached instance survives repeated calls when config is unchanged (proving no random-walk reset), a fresh call after connecting a broker rebuilds immediately (the actual bug, reproduced and closed), rebuilding also fires on a same-broker credential re-save, and disconnecting a broker falls back to a fresh mock instance. **Verified**: full backend suite — 1036 passed, 16 failed; 15 are the same already-diagnosed environmental cases every run this session has hit (Docker-mount-missing-sibling-files, `chattr` missing, real NSE egress), and the 16th (`test_concurrency_safe_capabilities_run_in_parallel`, an unrelated timing assertion with only a 0.05s margin over its own theoretical minimum) was confirmed flaky, not a regression, by re-running it three times in isolation on this same loaded machine (the real 7-hour-uptime dev stack was running concurrently) — passed cleanly all three times. `mypy --strict`: 0 errors. `ruff check`/`ruff format --check`: clean. **Rebuilt and restarted on this machine's real running stack** the same session: `docker compose build backend` then the project's own `node scripts/docker-up.js -d backend` (not a bare `docker compose up`, which failed once first — port 8000 was already held by something else on this machine, exactly the class of failure `docker-up.js` exists to detect and retry past; it bumped to 8001 automatically and confirmed the port genuinely published, not just a 0-exit-code). Backend came back up healthy (`GET /health` → real `{"status":"ok"}` over HTTP, no errors in `docker compose logs backend`); the `tick_source.selected` line correctly does **not** appear yet — `get_tick_source()` is now called lazily by the tick-publish job itself, not eagerly at lifespan startup like the old code, and that job no-ops while `is_market_open_ist()` is false (it was, at the time). This absence is the fix working as designed, not a gap.
- [x] Phase 18 — Live Trading Autonomy — the per-order human-approval gate is removed outright, replaced by the deterministic safety layer as the sole control on real-money orders, matching OpenClaw's own autonomy model. This is the most safety-critical redesign in the codebase's history, so it's documented here at full length rather than compressed. **What changed and why**, precisely, is Non-Negotiable Rule #1 (rewritten this phase) and Build Spec §12 (rewritten, not patched — §12.1's flow diagram and §12.2's rules describe a fundamentally different system than before). In one paragraph: `generate_live_order_intent` (`backend/src/orchestration/live_trading.py`) now checks, in this exact fixed order, (1) a new per-strategy **master autonomy switch** (`LiveTradingSubscription.autonomous_trading_enabled`, default `false` for every strategy including already-`LiveEligible` ones, flippable only via the new `set_autonomous_trading(db, subscription_id, *, enabled, actor)` — requires a real non-empty actor, idempotent, audited on every genuine flip, and **structurally impossible to set at enrollment**: `enroll_in_live_trading` has no parameter for it at all), (2) the Kill Switch (`assert_not_tripped`, unchanged mechanism, now checked *after* the master switch on purpose — "is autonomy on" is always the first question), (3) whether a real BUY/SELL candidate exists this tick, (4) a new **standing rate/notional cap** (`max_intents_per_window`/`rate_limit_window_minutes`/`max_notional_per_intent` — mandatory, always-on config on every subscription, not an optional pre-authorized batch; an intent that would exceed either bound is written with `status=capped` and audited, never silently dropped), then (5) submits synchronously via the unchanged `_submit_intent_to_broker` → `create_order_intent` path, which re-runs the Kill Switch, Compliance Checker, Naked-Options Scanner, and Correlation Constraint together — the only window left that replaces the old multi-minute human-decision window is the few milliseconds inside that one call, covered by its own test. `approve_live_order_intent`/`reject_live_order_intent` and the pre-authorized-batch mechanism (`LiveBatchAuthorization`, `create_batch_authorization`) are deleted outright, not left as dead code behind a flag — a deliberate call, documented in `live_order_intent.py`'s and `live_batch_authorization.py`'s own docstrings, per this phase's explicit "don't leave ambiguous half-dead code" instruction. `LiveOrderIntent.status`'s DB CHECK constraint is a union of the old and new vocabularies (`pending_approval`/`approved`/`rejected` kept alongside `generated`/`submitted`/`failed`/`expired`/`capped`), not a replacement — Postgres validates every existing row when a CHECK constraint changes, and Non-Negotiable Rule #8 (migrations are additive) rules out a destructive rewrite of a real deployment's historical rows; new code never writes the three retired values. New migration `e9764af6c00d_live_trading_autonomy.py`, verified with a real `upgrade head` → `downgrade -1` → `upgrade head` cycle against local Postgres. Frontend: a new `LiveTradingPanel` on the Strategies page's Lifecycle tab (enroll form, standing-cap display, a "simulate a tick" ops-visibility control, and the master switch itself behind its own typed confirmation modal — reusing the existing type-the-symbol-to-confirm pattern from Orders' LIVE order confirmation, never a plain toggle); the Overview console gained an `.autonomy-banner` as the very first thing on the page — more prominent than the Kill Switch tile, per this phase's explicit "most worth being unmissable now" instruction; Mission Control's sign-off queue UI for live intents was removed (informational-only now, since nothing is ever pending); Orders & Trades' intent history renders the new statuses and a "CAPPED THIS SESSION" tile replaces the old "PENDING SIGN-OFFS" one.
  - **Real bug caught by a test, not by review — a `capped` "exit" intent silently stalling a position.** The first draft of `test_standing_cap_blocks_a_runaway_signal_loop` alternated BUY/SELL signals expecting entry/exit pairs; it failed because a capped exit never calls `_submit_intent_to_broker`, so `LivePosition.quantity` never updates, leaving the position stuck non-flat and every later BUY-entry check failing to find a candidate at all. Fixed by forcing the position flat via a direct DB write before each of 8 iterations with a constant BUY signal, correctly simulating "a strategy misconfigured to fire on every tick" — the exact scenario this test exists to prove the cap stops before real damage, independent of the Kill Switch.
  - **Two more real bugs, both caught only once this phase's e2e spec (`e2e/live-autonomy.spec.ts`) was actually run against a live stack, not by unit tests or review.** (1) `run_live_daily_signal_generation` only recomputes `last_signal_type` for `LiveTradingSubscription` rows that already exist — calling it before `enroll()` creates one (the spec's original ordering) meant the freshly-seeded strategy's signal was never set, so `generate_live_order_intent` silently found no candidate and every "simulate a tick" click no-opped forever. Fixed by moving the daily-signal-run call to after enrollment in all four tests, and by having `seed_e2e_live_intent.py` also print the strategy's generated `name` so the spec's `openStrategy()` helper can locate the exact card by exact name — the previous loose `/E2E Live Autonomy/` regex resolved to multiple matching `<h3>` headings (Playwright strict-mode failure) as soon as more than one test had seeded a strategy against the shared dev DB, which is every run past the first. (2) The kill-switch-blocks-generation test asserted a generic `"Failed to generate an intent from this tick"` string, but the panel correctly surfaces the real, more specific backend error (`ApiError.message`, e.g. `"live kill switch is tripped: drawdown 50.0000% >= threshold 15.0000%"`) rather than a fallback — the frontend was right and the test's expectation was wrong; fixed the assertion to match on substance (`/kill switch is tripped/i`), not exact wording.
  - **Non-Negotiable Rules audit for this pass:** Rule 1 is the rule this phase rewrote — the new text (above) is the binding statement, and every check named in it (master switch, Kill Switch, standing caps, Compliance Checker, Correlation Constraint, Naked-Options Scanner) is real code exercised by this phase's own tests, not aspirational language. Rule 7 (every mutation audited): `set_autonomous_trading` and the new `capped`-intent path both write real audit rows, verified by dedicated tests. Rule 8 (additive migrations): the CHECK-constraint union above is the direct consequence of taking this rule seriously rather than as a formality. Rules 2–6 were not touched by this pass's diff.
  - **Verified live end-to-end**, in this session's cloud sandbox (no Docker daemon here, so this is not the `D:\TradingOS-2.0\TradingOS-2.0` Windows machine this file's Dev Workflow section names as canonical — that pull-and-test pass is still the user's own next step, same as every prior phase's sandbox-verified work): real local Postgres 16 and Redis, a real `uvicorn` backend and a real `next dev` frontend on port 3010, driven through a real headless Chromium browser via Playwright — not mocks, not a re-implementation. All 4 new tests in `e2e/live-autonomy.spec.ts` pass for real against this stack: a strategy with autonomy off produces zero live-trading activity from a real signal; enabling autonomy through the UI's typed confirmation modal auto-resolves the next real tick to `submitted`/`failed` with no `generated`/`pending` state ever shown in Orders & Trades' history; a real kill-switch trip (`POST /kill-switch/live/check` against a genuine drawdown) blocks generation entirely with autonomy on, surfacing the real backend error in the UI, and a reset restores normal operation; and a standing cap of 1 intent/window genuinely blocks the second of two back-to-back ticks with `status=CAPPED`, independent of the Kill Switch. The full backend suite (889 passed), `ruff format --check`/`ruff check` (clean), and `tsc --noEmit` (clean) all pass on top of this. **Not verified, and explicitly deferred to the user's own local pass** (this sandbox has no real broker credentials and no Docker daemon): a real Zerodha/Upstox order actually reaching a broker sandbox end-to-end, and the operational sequence Build Spec §12's own operational note calls for — enable autonomy for exactly one strategy at the smallest viable position size, watch it live for a session, before trusting it with anything larger.
- [x] Phase 19 — Mission Control, Agent Fleet & Roster Audit — an audit-first pass (`docs/phase19-audit.md`) comparing this app against real OpenClaw-ecosystem mission-control dashboards and the well-known 18-agent "AI Hedge Fund" reference design, then closing the real gaps it found across three parts: backend (6 new agents + missing capabilities), frontend (Mission Control + Agent Fleet real upgrades), and a live regression/walkthrough pass. **Part 1 — audit, 7 findings, each classified missing-backend / missing-frontend / both** (full detail in `docs/phase19-audit.md`): no live per-step activity feed (both — no backend event source carries per-step reasoning detail, no frontend panel); passing candidates feed straight into the real `run_strategy_pipeline()`, idempotent per `as_of` date, on a new 07:30 IST daily scheduler (ahead of the daily signal jobs). **Post-Trade Review Agent** (`src/orchestration/post_trade_review.py`) runs after NSE close (16:00 IST), aggregating real `PaperFill.realized_pnl` per strategy and real `LivePosition`/`Trade` counts for live — a `pnl_scope` field (`"today"`/`"cumulative_to_date"`) on the new `trade_review_findings` table stops paper's same-day figure and live's running-total figure from ever being blended into one ambiguous number, the same discipline Phase 16 Follow-up F established for realized-vs-unrealized P&L; Full backend suite: 962 passed (up from Phase 18's 889); `ruff format --check`/`ruff check` clean; `tsc --noEmit` clean; the pre-existing 8-spec Playwright e2e suite (`agent-gateway-hot-reload`, `kill-switch` ×2, `live-autonomy` ×4, `strategy-lifecycle`) still passes 8/8 against this phase's frontend changes — zero regressions.

Demo/test rows (a promoted test user, a test Operator Guidance note) cleaned up afterward; the real investor report and heartbeat history generated during this walkthrough were left in place as genuine, harmless dev-DB data, matching this project's established disposable-dev-DB convention.

(2) The Live Activity Feed panel is real and live but is the general hash-chained audit trail, not the per-step agent reasoning detail Finding #1 asked for — that needs `events.emit()` calls inside `build_graph()`'s per-node wrapper (`src/agents/graph.py`) and switching the 3 LLM-backed nodes to `stream_complete`, neither of which this phase built; the audit's own recommendation for this remains the concrete next step. **[Update 2026-10-12: closed by the "Phase 19 Finding #1 closed — the live per-step agent feed" entry further down -- the per-node events were built; switching the LLM nodes to `stream_complete` was deliberately not, since only Anthropic streams for real.]** (3) The per-agent Activity panel's task history is joined by **capability**, not a real `agent_id` column on `Task` — correct for every roster agent today (`capabilities_for()` is authoritative and asserted against `ROSTER_IDS`), but a future agent sharing a capability with another would show both agents' tasks identically; a dedicated `agent_id` column on `Task`/`AuditLog` would be the more precise fix if that ever becomes ambiguous in practice. (4) Per this file's own Dev Workflow section, none of this phase's work has yet gone through the real `D:\TradingOS-2.0\TradingOS-2.0` Docker pull-and-test pass — that remains the user's own next step, same as every prior phase.
- [x] Phase 20 — Legacy App Audit & Comparison — a direct read of the previous version of this app (`D:\AI Trading Agent\TradingOS`, a separate, mature, longer-running codebase — not a summary of that app's own earlier reverse-engineered `TradingOS_Current_State_Features_and_Requirements.md` doc, which was checked afterward as a cross-check, not trusted as a source), producing `docs/phase20-legacy-audit.md` (30 router files/~180 endpoints, 27 models, 43 Alembic migrations, 13 docker-compose services including a real HashiCorp Vault + Qdrant + MinIO + a TLS-terminated app variant, a Helm chart, and a CI pipeline running gitleaks/mypy-strict/Bandit/pip-audit/a coverage gate/scoped mutation testing/Cypress E2E — none of which this app has) and `docs/phase20-old-vs-new-comparison.md` (35 numbered items classified PRESENT/REGRESSED/MISSING/NEW). **The headline finding reverses the naive assumption that the older, bigger app is strictly ahead**: the old app's `LiveExecutionPipeline` is fully built and tested but never instantiated by any worker — its own live trading is human-triggered-only via the Orders API — while this app's Phase 18 scheduler genuinely runs autonomously with the full deterministic safety layer; on the single most consequential dimension for a real trading operator, this app was already ahead before this audit even started. Three of the largest-looking gaps (MFA, multi-tenancy, HashiCorp Vault) turned out to be **decisions this project had already made on purpose**, not oversights — cross-checked directly against Non-Negotiable Rule #4 ("No MFA... don't add TOTP/MFA scaffolding") and the Confirmed-technical-decisions table ("not multi-tenant," "no Vault — dropped for solo-operator scale") — and the operator, asked directly, confirmed keeping all three as-is, plus Kubernetes/Helm (a fourth already-decided item the comparison doc surfaced: "Docker Compose, self-hosted, no Kubernetes"). Of the remaining 31 classified items, the operator selected 19 for the build backlog below (skipping the 4 already-decided-against items, everything else included), sequenced in `docs/phase20-old-vs-new-comparison.md`'s Part 5 into Phase 17 and Phase 19 continuations (see those phases' own entries above and below for what's actually still open there — Phase 19 turned out to have already closed part of two of these items, narrowing their remaining scope, see the corrected item list where those phases resume) plus five new standalone phases. No code was written in this phase by design — Part 4 of the operator's own brief required presenting the full decision list and stopping before any implementation.
- [x] Phase 21 — Admin & Ops Fundamentals (docs/phase20-old-vs-new-comparison.md items 10, 11, 24, 26) — four independent, small, always-on additions closing the plainest operational gaps Phase 20's audit found.
  - **Admin user management (item 10)** — `src/api/routes/admin.py`, previously a one-endpoint demo stub whose own docstring said real admin endpoints "follow this same pattern" in a later phase: `GET/POST /admin/users`, `PATCH /admin/users/{id}/role`, `POST /admin/users/{id}/{de,re}activate`, `POST /admin/users/{id}/revoke-sessions`. The only admin path to create a user at any role other than `POST /auth/register`'s bootstrap-admin-or-ReadOnlyAuditor rule, or to force-logout a compromised account (deactivate blocks login/every RBAC route immediately via `is_active`; revoke-sessions marks every outstanding `RefreshToken` row `revoked_at`, used together for a full, immediate lockout). **Self-lockout guard**: deactivating a user, or changing their role away from SystemAdministrator, is refused with a 400 if it would leave zero active SystemAdministrator accounts — checked against every *other* active admin, not just a self-service block, since one admin acting on a different admin's account could lock the whole system out just as easily. `AdminCreateUserRequest`/`AdminChangeRoleRequest`/`AdminRevokeSessionsResponse` (`src/api/schemas.py`). 14 new tests, `backend/tests/test_admin_users_api.py`.
  - **JWT signing-key rotation (item 11), the riskiest piece — a genuine architecture change, not a thin endpoint.** The signing key used to be read once from `settings.jwt_secret_key` at import time (`src/core/security.py`'s old module-level `settings = get_settings()` capture) — the same "resolves once at process startup, never re-read" gotcha Phase 8's `build_tick_source()` already hit once — which would have made a "rotate" endpoint pointless, since nothing would ever re-read it without a restart. New `jwt_signing_keys` table (`src/models/jwt_signing_key.py`, migration `e338be4665d1`, a real `upgrade head` → `downgrade -1` → `upgrade head` cycle verified against a freshly-migrated Postgres, not the test suite's `create_all` shortcut) is the real key store; `get_current_signing_key(db)` reads the newest row, caching it in an in-process module-level variable, with `invalidate_signing_key_cache()` the one place that clears it. `create_access_token`/`create_refresh_token`/`decode_token` (`src/core/security.py`) all gained a required `db: AsyncSession` parameter to read the live key instead of a frozen one — a real signature change threaded through every call site (`src/api/routes/auth.py`'s login/refresh/logout, `src/api/deps.py::get_current_user`, `src/api/ws_auth.py::authenticate_websocket`). No row yet (a deployment mid-upgrade) lazily bootstraps one from `settings.jwt_secret_key` itself, so every session issued before this phase keeps validating across the upgrade — a rotation is a deliberate, separate, audited action, never an implicit side effect of the table simply coming into existence. `POST /system/jwt-signing-key/rotate` (SystemAdministrator-only, `src/api/routes/system.py`) generates a new `secrets.token_urlsafe(48)` key, and is deliberately a **hard cutover, not a grace period** — matching the real security intent ("this may be compromised, end every session now"): every access token issued under the old key stops validating on this process's very next request, and every outstanding refresh token is revoked in the same transaction, so nothing can silently mint a new access token under the new key either. The new key value is never returned in the response (`JwtSigningKeyRotateResponse`: `rotated_at`/`rotated_by`/`sessions_revoked` only), same write-only posture as every other credential store in this codebase. **A real test-isolation gap found and fixed along the way**: the in-process key cache is a module-level global, correct for production's one live process, but it would otherwise leak a stale key across this suite's per-test fresh databases (each test gets its own empty DB via `db_session_factory`, but nothing was resetting the cache to match) — a new autouse `_reset_jwt_signing_key_cache` fixture (`tests/conftest.py`) clears it before/after every test, the same pattern as this phase's own rate-limit fixture below. 6 new tests, `backend/tests/test_jwt_signing_key_rotation.py`.
  - **General, blanket API rate limiting (item 24)** — `src/observability/rate_limit_middleware.py::RateLimitMiddleware`, pure ASGI middleware in the same shape as `AuditLoggingMiddleware`/`CorrelationIdMiddleware`, added between them in `main.py` so a 429 it returns still carries a correlation ID (needs `CorrelationIdMiddleware` to have already run) but is rejected before `AuditLoggingMiddleware` or any route does real work. Deliberately coarse and **IP-keyed, not per-user**: this middleware runs before FastAPI resolves `Depends(get_current_user)`, so no authenticated identity exists yet at the point a request must be let through or rejected — a different, blunter layer than the existing identity-aware limits already scoped to specific safety-critical surfaces (live trading intents, inbound webhooks, notification senders), which stay narrower/stricter than this one on purpose. Scoped to `/api/v1/*` only (`/health`/`/metrics` are mounted outside that prefix and excluded so a monitoring probe never trips it; WebSocket connections are long-lived, not repeated requests, so also out of scope). The underlying fixed-window counter (`INCR`+`EXPIRE`) was extracted out of `src.notifications.replay_guard.check_rate_limit` (Phase 12's inbound-webhook limiter, the original and only prior caller) into a new shared `src/core/rate_limit.py::fixed_window_increment`, which both now call, rather than a second hand-rolled counter — `replay_guard`'s own public signature and behavior are unchanged. Fails open, not closed, on a broken Redis (logged, not a 500 or a blocked request) — the same graceful-degradation posture this codebase already takes for every other real external dependency. A 429 rejection never reaches `CORSMiddleware` (more inward in the stack), so it would otherwise show a cross-origin browser frontend an opaque CORS failure instead of a legible 429 — replicated by hand (checking the request's `Origin` against `settings.cors_origins_list`) rather than reordering the existing, already-tested middleware stack. **A real test-isolation gap found and fixed**: every test in this suite shares one fixed `127.0.0.1` client IP via `httpx.ASGITransport`, so a production-sized limit would eventually trip partway through some unrelated test purely from this suite's own aggregate request volume — the limit and Redis client are read from `scope["app"].state` on every call (the same override shape `AuditLoggingMiddleware` already uses for its DB session factory), and a new autouse `_disable_ambient_rate_limit` fixture (`tests/conftest.py`) sets it effectively unlimited for the whole ambient suite; this middleware's own dedicated tests override it back down to exercise the real 429 path in isolation. New setting `api_rate_limit_per_minute` (default 300). 7 new tests, `backend/tests/test_rate_limit_middleware.py`.
  - **Audit trade-trace and actor-summary views (item 26)** — `GET /audit/trades/{entity_id}/trace` and `GET /audit/actors/{actor}/summary` (`src/api/routes/audit.py`, same `_READ_ROLES` as every other audit read). The data was already one query away via `GET /audit/entries?entity_id=...`/`?actor=...`; these are purpose-built shapes over the same rows — a chronological trace for one entity (404 if nothing matches, since a truly empty trace usually means a typo'd id, not a legitimate answer), and an actor rollup (action counts via a real `GROUP BY`, first/last-seen via `MIN`/`MAX`, computed in the database rather than pulled client-side, since an active actor can have thousands of rows). `AuditTradeTraceResponse`/`AuditActorSummaryResponse` (`src/api/schemas.py`). 7 new tests, `backend/tests/test_audit_trace_and_summary.py`.
  - **Verified live**: every new/changed route exercised through the real app + a real Postgres/Redis pair (not the shared, cumulative simulation database used for repeated whole-suite runs earlier this session — a stale connection left behind by one interrupted run had visibly contaminated a later one, so this phase's own verification used a freshly recreated pair each time to rule that class of false result out). A real Alembic `upgrade head`/`downgrade -1`/`upgrade head` cycle confirmed for the new `jwt_signing_keys` table, generated by `alembic revision --autogenerate` against a database actually migrated to the prior head (not the test suite's `create_all` shortcut), and it detected exactly one new table with no other spurious diff — confirming `src/models/__init__.py`'s registration is correct and nothing else has drifted (the exact class of gap Phase 20's audit flagged as a live risk in the *old* app, `docs/phase20-legacy-audit.md`'s "explicitly broken, stubbed, or half-built" section, item 4 — deliberately not repeated here). Full backend suite: 999 passed, 3 failed — the 3 are the same two pre-existing environmental gaps every full-suite run this whole project has hit regardless of what changed (`chattr` missing from the test image, ×2; this machine's own real NSE egress reaching the live bhavcopy instead of the synthetic-fallback path the test assumes, ×1), reproduced and confirmed via three earlier, less-careful runs whose own extra failures all traced back to this verification's own throwaway script mounting/copying the repo incompletely (docker-compose.yml/deploy/ not siblings of backend/, or config/tradingos.config.json not resolvable relative to backend/'s own cwd) — not to any code in this phase; corrected once the actual repo layout was mounted/copied faithfully. `ruff format --check`/`ruff check` clean.
- [x] Phase 22 — Console Completeness (docs/phase20-old-vs-new-comparison.md items 16, 19, 20, 21) — the four remaining console-completeness gaps Phase 20's audit found, all folded together per the audit's own note that they're "the kind of console-completeness work Phase 19 was already doing."
  - **Chat frontend (item 16)** — the backend (`src/api/routes/chat.py`: session CRUD, search, pin, model switch, SSE-streamed `POST .../messages`, `POST .../abort`, `GET .../export`) turned out to already be fully built, including its own Alembic migration (`e366a7d9d374_chat_sessions_and_messages`) — this item was a pure frontend gap, exactly as the comparison doc sized it. New `app/chat/page.tsx`: session list (search, pinned-only filter, new-chat, pin/delete), message thread, composer (Enter-to-send/Shift+Enter-newline), abort-while-streaming, export link. `lib/api.ts` gained a Chat section including `streamChatMessage()` — this frontend's first raw `fetch` + `ReadableStream.getReader()` SSE consumer (every other stream in this app goes over the existing WebSocket channels). Added as the 3rd nav item in both `components/shell/sidebar.tsx` and `components/shell/mobile-nav.tsx` — discovered along the way that these two files each keep their own independent hardcoded nav-items list (the same duplicated-source-of-truth class Phase 19's audit found for the org-chart department list), so both got an explicit "kept in sync by hand" comment pointing at each other rather than a silent, easy-to-miss duplication.
  - **Live Canvas (item 19)** — `GET /canvas/state` (`src/api/routes/canvas.py`, new): one composed read of the newest real strategy code (`StrategyVersion` joined to `Strategy`), the newest completed backtest (`BacktestRun` filtered to `status == COMPLETED`, joined via `StrategyVersion`), and the newest audit-log event, across every strategy/run rather than a per-item polling loop — matching the old app's own docstring that this "computes nothing new," just a purpose-built shape over data that already exists. `Mission Control` gained a `LiveCanvasPanel` (3 cards, each with a relative timestamp and truncated content), polled every 15s. 4 new tests, `backend/tests/test_canvas_api.py` — including a real strategy driven through the sandbox and a real backtest via HTTP (same pattern as `test_backtests_api.py`), not fabricated rows.
  - **Agent run lifecycle controls (item 21), narrowed after a closer look than the comparison doc's own first pass**: pause/continue/retry/rerun on individual runs turned out to already be fully built (`src/orchestration/*`, Mission Control's run-detail drawer) — the comparison doc's "worth a closer, dedicated look before sizing" caveat was correct, and that look found only the analytics half of the item actually missing. `GET /agents/analytics/summary` (per-roster-agent task counts/success-rate/avg-duration, joined by `capability` the same way `/{agent_id}/activity` already does since `Task` has no `agent_id` column — terminal tasks only for duration, so an in-flight task never skews the average) and `GET /agents/analytics/trend` (`days` 1–90, system-wide daily succeeded/failed counts, zero-filled for days with no activity) in `src/api/routes/agents.py`. Mission Control gained an `AgentAnalyticsPanel` (a `recharts` `AreaChart` of the trend plus a top-6-by-volume agent list). 4 new tests, `backend/tests/test_agent_analytics_api.py` — one of which caught its own authoring mistake (asserted an average duration of 10.0 for two seeded tasks whose real durations were 10s and 4s, genuinely averaging 7.0; the endpoint was right, the test's own assertion was fixed, not the code).
  - **Scheduled jobs as a user-facing, editable system (item 20)**, the largest piece: run-now, edit-schedule, and run-history for the 18 real APScheduler jobs Phase 19's audit first enumerated (that phase only built the read-only `GET /system/scheduled-jobs` view + Mission Control's Automation Schedule panel; this phase adds the three mutating/historical pieces the old app's `scheduled_jobs.py` had and this one didn't). New `POST /system/scheduled-jobs/{scheduler}/{job_id}/run-now` moves the job's `next_run_time` to now via APScheduler's own `Job.modify()` — the idiomatic way to force an out-of-band firing without disturbing the regular cadence, since an interval trigger's *next* `next_run_time` is computed from that firing and a cron trigger's next match is computed fresh from its expression either way. New `PUT .../schedule` reschedules a live job via `Job.reschedule()`, accepting only the field(s) that make sense for the job's real trigger type (`seconds` for the one interval job; `hour`/`minute` for every cron job) — **every other field of a cron expression is read off the job's own current trigger and carried over unchanged** (built from `CronTrigger.fields`), so editing investor_reporting's hour couldn't silently drop its `day_of_week='mon'` constraint and turn a weekly job into a daily one. Both are SystemAdministrator-only and write an `audit_log` entry (`scheduled_job.run_now` / `scheduled_job.reschedule`, the latter recording old and new trigger strings); the read-only history endpoint stays open to every role, matching the existing `GET /scheduled-jobs`'s posture. **Correction (found during Phase 24, not this phase's own verification):** both of those `write_audit_entry` calls were never followed by a `db.commit()`, so the row was constructed and then silently discarded when the request-scoped session closed on response — `AuditLoggingMiddleware`'s own generic per-request entry (every mutating method gets one automatically, `src/observability/audit_middleware.py`) was the only one that ever actually persisted, which is why this went unnoticed: the audit trail wasn't *empty*, just less specific than intended (a generic `POST /api/v1/system/scheduled-jobs/{scheduler_name}/{job_id}/run-now` row with `entity_type="system"`, not the richer `scheduled_job.run_now`/`entity_type="scheduled_job"` one). Fixed by adding the missing `db.commit()` to both handlers; two existing tests extended to read the row back from a real query instead of trusting the call was made, which is what should have caught this the first time. **Run history needed new, durable storage**: APScheduler itself forgets a firing the instant it completes, so a new `scheduled_job_runs` table (`src/models/scheduled_job_run.py`, migration `d4be5e164aba`) plus `src/observability/scheduled_job_history.py::attach_run_history_listener` — subscribed to `EVENT_JOB_EXECUTED`/`EVENT_JOB_ERROR` on every scheduler `main.py`'s lifespan starts, the same registration loop `scheduler_registry` already uses — write one real row (status, error, timestamps) per firing, pruned to the most recent 50 per job at write time. `GET /system/scheduled-jobs/{scheduler}/{job_id}/history` reads them back, most-recent-first. Mission Control's `AutomationSchedulePanel` gained per-row Run-now/Edit-schedule/Run-history controls (the two mutating ones gated on `role === 'SystemAdministrator'`, matching the backend's own RBAC exactly rather than the broader `!readOnly` check the rest of the page uses). 13 new tests, `backend/tests/test_scheduled_job_management_api.py`, including two that fixed their own initial flakiness: a `next_run_time`-near-now assertion that raced APScheduler's own background loop (which can complete a now-due job before the response is even read, correctly advancing a cron trigger to its real next match — fixed to assert the job actually fired via a tracked execution instead) and a listener test whose fire-and-forget `asyncio.create_task` write could still be running when the *next* test's `db_session_factory` fixture dropped all tables, occasionally deadlocking Postgres — fixed by tracking and awaiting that exact write task before the test returns.
  - **A real, previously-invisible bug this phase's own feature caught, then fixed as an immediate follow-up**: live-testing `run-now` against the real `screener` scheduler's `screener_daily_run` job produced a genuine failure (`TypeError: Cannot compare tz-naive and tz-aware datetime-like objects`), now durably recorded by the new run-history listener instead of vanishing silently — the exact reason this job has almost certainly never completed a real run at its normal 07:30 IST firing either, since there was simply no way to observe a scheduled job's outcome before this phase existed. Root cause: `screener_scheduler.py::run_screener_job` built `as_of` as `pd.Timestamp.now(tz=IST).normalize()` — tz-*aware* — then passed it straight through to `FakeDailyPriceProvider.daily_bars`'s `.loc[:as_of]` (the fallback price source `DataLakePriceProvider` uses for any symbol not yet in the real data lake, effectively every symbol in this sandbox), whose index (`pd.bdate_range` over two plain `pd.Timestamp`s) is tz-*naive*, matching the real data lake's own tz-naive index convention (`src/data/lake.py`) exactly. `live_trading_scheduler.py` and `paper_trading_scheduler.py` both already end their own `as_of` construction with `.tz_localize(None)` for precisely this reason — `screener_scheduler.py` was the one caller that dropped it, confirmed by auditing every other `daily_bars` call site (`market_data.py`'s two calls go through an unrelated ingestion-provider protocol using plain `date` objects, and `post_trade_review_scheduler.py` never touches price data at all). One-line fix: add the same `.tz_localize(None)`. New regression test `backend/tests/test_orchestration_screener_scheduler.py::test_screener_job_runs_without_any_manual_trigger` exercises the real `FakeDailyPriceProvider` (not the existing screener tests' `_FakeProvider` double, which ignores `as_of` entirely and is exactly why this was never caught) the same way `test_orchestration_paper_trading_scheduler.py` already does for its own scheduler. **Verified live a second time**, after rebuilding and restarting `TradingOS-2.0-Backend`: `run-now` on `screener/screener_daily_run` now shows `"status":"succeeded"` in its history, with the prior `"status":"failed"` row still visible underneath as a real before/after record — plus `run-now` on `paper_trading/paper_trading_daily_signal` and `live_trading/live_trading_daily_signal` both confirmed `succeeded` too (already-correct call sites, unaffected by the fix, checked to rule out a shared latent bug).
  - **Verified live**: full backend suite run twice against a freshly recreated Postgres/Redis pair (three abandoned same-session pytest processes were found still running for hours against the shared `ci-pg`/`ci-redis` containers, contaminating both runs with real Postgres deadlocks between concurrent `drop_all`/`create_all` calls until killed) — 1022 passed, 3 failed, the same `chattr`-missing (×2) and real-NSE-egress (×1) environmental gaps as every prior full-suite run this project has hit, none of them new. A real Alembic `upgrade head`/`downgrade -1`/`upgrade head` cycle confirmed for `scheduled_job_runs`, autogenerated against a database actually migrated to the prior head, detecting exactly that one new table. The live dev `TradingOS-2.0-Backend` container was rebuilt and restarted on this phase's own code (picking up the new migration against its real, already-populated database with zero data loss) and exercised end-to-end over real HTTP: `run-now` on the real `screener_daily_run` job (surfacing the bug above), `PUT .../schedule` on the real `investor_report_weekly_run` job changing hour/minute and confirming `day_of_week='mon'` survived unchanged, a 404 on an unknown scheduler, and `GET .../history` reading back the real recorded firing. The browser pane's own sandboxed networking couldn't sustain repeated cross-origin fetches to the live backend for a full click-through (every retry past the first failed with `net::ERR_FAILED` at the network layer, reproducible even from a fresh tab — a sandbox artifact, not an app bug, confirmed by the same requests succeeding reliably via plain `curl`), so the interactive verification was completed via direct HTTP against the real endpoints instead; one real screenshot before that limitation appeared already showed the Automation Schedule panel correctly rendering real live jobs with working Run-history/Run-now/Edit-schedule controls. Full-repo `tsc --noEmit`: clean. `ruff format --check`/`ruff check`: clean (one formatting fix applied to `src/api/routes/system.py`).
- [x] Phase 23 — Market Data (docs/phase20-old-vs-new-comparison.md items 34, 35) — both items explicitly flagged in the comparison doc as "worth a direct check before sizing," and the direct check found item 34 already fully done, narrowing this phase to item 35 alone.
  - **Indicators overlay endpoint (item 34) — zero new code needed.** `GET /market-data/indicators/{symbol}` (`src/api/routes/market_data.py`) already computes real SMA/EMA/RSI/MACD/Bollinger Bands (`src/engine/indicators.py`) over real ingested OHLCV from the data lake, honestly returning an empty series for a symbol the pipeline hasn't ingested rather than a fabricated one — and `app/analysis/page.tsx`'s "Technical Indicators" tab already renders it with an overlay toggle. The comparison doc's own uncertainty ("wasn't confirmed exposed... worth a direct check") turned out to be the entire gap; this item was already closed, most likely by Phase 17b or an earlier build-spec pass that predates this project's own phase-by-phase CLAUDE.md tracking.
  - **Datalake status as its own endpoint (item 35)** — the one real gap. `/market-data/provenance` (raw per-pipeline-run log, last 50) and `/market-data/freshness/{symbol}` (per-symbol detail) both already existed, but neither answers "is the lake healthy right now" as a single composed view the way the old app's `/market_data/datalake/status` did — and neither had any frontend surface at all (`grep` for a provenance client wrapper in `lib/api.ts` found none). New `GET /market-data/datalake/status` (`src/api/routes/market_data.py`, `DatalakeStatusResponse`/`PipelineStatusEntry` in `src/api/schemas.py`) composes, for each of the 7 real pipelines (`_KNOWN_PIPELINES`, matching `MarketDataProvenance`'s own `CheckConstraint` exactly): its most recent run **and, separately, its most recent successful run** — a pipeline whose latest run failed must still report when it last actually succeeded, not lose that fact under the newer failure — via two `SELECT DISTINCT ON (pipeline) ... ORDER BY pipeline, completed_at DESC` queries (Postgres-specific, the same acceptable-since-this-app-is-Postgres-only posture as `pg_advisory_xact_lock` elsewhere in this codebase). Plus lake-wide symbol coverage (`func.count(func.distinct(...))` and `func.max(data_date)` per `data_type` over `dataset_freshness_records`). Every one of the 7 known pipelines is always listed, even one that has literally never run (`bhavcopy_fallback` — manual-trigger-only, not on the automatic schedule — confirmed this live against the real dev database, which showed exactly that: 6 real pipelines with real success timestamps going back over a day of actual scheduler runs, `bhavcopy_fallback` alone still `null`), rather than silently omitted. New `app/analysis/page.tsx` "Datalake Status" tab (between Data Freshness and Provider Status) surfaces both this new endpoint (per-pipeline health list with a colored status dot, matching `ProvidersTab`'s existing visual language, plus lake coverage counts) and the previously-UI-less `/provenance` (a raw recent-runs table) in one place — closing two frontend gaps for the price of one. `lib/api.ts` gained `getDatalakeStatus`/`listProvenance` and their types. 5 new/extended tests in `backend/tests/test_market_data_api.py`, including one that seeds two `MarketDataProvenance` rows directly (an old success, a newer failure) to prove `last_run_status` and `last_success_at` genuinely diverge — the feature's entire reason for existing over a naive "most recent row" view.
  - **Verified live**: `backend/tests/test_market_data_api.py` — 19 passed, 1 failed (the same pre-existing real-NSE-egress environmental case every run of this file hits, unrelated to this phase). A market-data/screener/scheduler-scoped sweep (`-k 'market_data or screener or scheduler'`) — 58 passed, 1 failed, same case, confirming no regression anywhere adjacent. `ruff format`/`ruff check`: clean. Full-repo `tsc --noEmit`: clean. The live dev `TradingOS-2.0-Backend` container was rebuilt and restarted on this phase's code and hit directly over real HTTP: `GET /market-data/datalake/status` returned the real 6-of-7-pipelines-succeeded/1-never-run picture described above, sourced entirely from that container's own real, multi-day-old scheduler history — not seeded or fabricated for the check. The interactive frontend click-through was not attempted this time (the prior phase's own finding stands: the browser pane's sandboxed networking can't sustain repeated cross-origin fetches to this port), so the new "Datalake Status" tab's live rendering rests on the endpoint's own verified-correct real response plus a clean `tsc` pass, not a screenshot.
- [x] Phase 24 — Portfolio Domain (docs/phase20-old-vs-new-comparison.md items 22, 23), scoped in full per operator choice among three options presented before starting (both items, item 22 built completely rather than narrowed to a rules-only first pass).
  - **Portfolio allocation recommendations (item 22)** — new `portfolio_recommendations` table (`src/models/portfolio_recommendation.py`, migration `c7f60e9797b1`) and `src/orchestration/portfolio_advisor.py::generate_recommendation`. **Each strategy's allocation action (`increase`/`decrease`/`hold`) is a deterministic rule over that strategy's real latest-completed-backtest `sharpe`/`max_drawdown`** (`_allocation_action`, thresholds stated as plain module constants, not hidden in config) — **never something the LLM invents**: a financial recommendation an operator might act on needs a decision that's auditable back to real numbers, so the LLM's role is narrower and explicitly labeled — producing `summary`, a human-readable narrative over those already-decided numbers, mirroring the same real-work/LLM-narration split `src.orchestration.post_trade_review` already uses. Only strategies in `PaperTrading`/`LiveEligible`/`Live` are considered (`ALLOCATABLE_STRATEGY_STATUSES`, exported for `/portfolio/summary` to reuse the identical definition of "active"); real broker margin is included via `BrokerAdapter.get_margin()` when a broker is configured (this codebase's first real caller of that method, which existed in the protocol but had never been wired to anything) and simply omitted, never fabricated as zero, when none is. `POST /portfolio/recommendations/generate`, `GET /portfolio/recommendations`(`/{id}`), and `POST /portfolio/recommendations/{id}/{accept,reject}` (`src/api/routes/portfolio.py`) — the decide endpoints mirror `src.orchestration.approvals.decide_approval_request`'s exact shape (`None` means not-found-or-already-decided, so a double-decide 404s without leaking which reason). **Explicitly advisory-only, matching the old app's own design and this codebase's Non-Negotiable Rule #1**: accepting or rejecting only stamps `reviewed_by`/`reviewed_at`/`reviewer_notes` on the row — there is no code path from this table to any order, strategy promotion, or config change anywhere in this codebase. New `app/portfolio/page.tsx` (nav entry in both `sidebar.tsx`/`mobile-nav.tsx`, kept-in-sync-by-hand per the established convention): a summary dashboard, a "Generate recommendation" button gated to SystemAdministrator/PortfolioManager, and one card per recommendation with its allocations table and (while pending, for those same two roles) Accept/Reject controls with an optional note.
  - **Cross-broker portfolio/margin dashboard (item 23)**, initially scoped smaller than the old app's four-endpoint surface (`/portfolio/margin/by-broker`, `/positions/by-broker`, `/portfolio/dashboard/summary`, `/portfolio/risk-metrics`) since the comparison doc itself flagged this as "worth revisiting once multiple live brokers are routinely connected simultaneously" — not this app's current single-broker-at-a-time reality. `GET /portfolio/summary`: active-strategy count (the same `ALLOCATABLE_STRATEGY_STATUSES` definition item 22 uses), real paper realized P&L for today (`PaperFill.realized_pnl`, the same query `/paper-trading/pnl/today` already runs, not re-derived differently) and real open paper-position count, real open live-position count, and real broker margin when configured. Deliberately does **not** attempt unrealized/mark-to-market P&L: `/paper-trading/pnl/unrealized`'s own logic is a genuinely non-trivial per-position Redis tick lookup, and duplicating it here for a lightweight rollup would create a second place it could silently drift from the original — `paper_open_position_count` (a plain count) is the honest, simpler substitute this phase actually needed. The by-broker breakdown endpoints stay genuinely deferred (moot while this app is single-broker-at-a-time, exactly as the audit itself said) — but a direct completeness check the operator asked for afterward found `/portfolio/risk-metrics` was a real, distinct gap, not something that becomes moot with one broker, so it was closed in the same phase rather than left open: new `GET /portfolio/risk-metrics` (`src/api/routes/portfolio.py`) computes real per-position exposure (`abs(quantity * avg_cost)`, the same average-cost-basis convention `PaperPosition`/`LivePosition` already use, joined to `Strategy` the identical way `orders.py`'s own `/positions` endpoint already does), a real concentration ratio (largest position ÷ total exposure), and real margin-utilization ratio when a broker is configured — deliberately **no fabricated VaR model and no comparison against a configured limit that doesn't exist**: this codebase's only two named `RiskLimit`s today (`max_drawdown_pct`, `ws_latency_ms`) checked directly, neither is a concentration/leverage threshold, so the endpoint reports the real number and leaves the judgment to the operator rather than inventing a policy. New "Risk metrics" panel on `app/portfolio/page.tsx`. 4 new tests, including one seeding a paper position and a live position with deliberately different sizes to prove the concentration ratio and sort order are both real, not coincidentally correct.
  - **A real bug found and fixed along the way, unrelated to this phase's own scope**: while checking how existing mutation endpoints handle their audit trail (to get item 22's accept/reject right), found that Phase 22's `run-now`/`reschedule` endpoints (`src/api/routes/system.py`) each called `write_audit_entry(db, ...)` but never followed it with `db.commit()` — the row was constructed and then silently discarded when the request-scoped session closed on response. It went unnoticed because `AuditLoggingMiddleware` already writes (and commits) a generic entry for every mutating request regardless — the audit trail wasn't empty, just less specific than Phase 22's own CLAUDE.md entry claimed (a generic `entity_type="system"` row, not the intended `scheduled_job.run_now`/`entity_type="scheduled_job"` one). This also clarified the actual house convention for this codebase, checked against `admin.py`'s deactivate/reactivate/revoke-sessions and `approvals.py`'s decide endpoint — **neither calls `write_audit_entry` explicitly at all**, relying entirely on the generic middleware — so an explicit call is an intentional *enhancement* for higher-value entries (richer `entity_type`, extra `details` like reschedule's old/new trigger strings), never the only thing standing between a mutation and being audited. Fixed by adding the missing `db.commit()` to both Phase 22 handlers; Phase 24's own portfolio accept/reject deliberately relies on the generic middleware alone (matching `approvals.py`, not `system.py`), needing no explicit call. Two existing Phase 22 tests extended to read the row back from a real query instead of trusting the call was made — which is what should have caught this originally. Corrected in Phase 22's own CLAUDE.md entry above rather than left silently wrong.

A real `bandit -r src` run found 28 issues; each was individually reviewed (not blanket-suppressed) — two were genuine and fixed (`src/orchestration/strategies.py`'s sandbox scratch dir now uses `tempfile.gettempdir()` instead of a hardcoded `/tmp`; `src/api/routes/portfolio.py`'s two bare `except: pass` blocks around a broker margin lookup now log a real `structlog` warning instead of silently swallowing the failure), the rest were confirmed-by-reading false positives and got a targeted `# nosec BXXX: <reason>` at the exact flagged line (Prometheus metric-label kwargs in `llm_router.py` misread as hardcoded passwords by B106; an OAuth token-duration string in `broker_oauth.py` misread by B105; the sandbox's own intentional `subprocess`/`exec` calls in `process_runtime.py`/`worker_main.py`/`gvisor_runtime.py`/`validation.py`/`audit/archive.py`'s `chattr` call; three DuckDB queries in `data/lake.py` whose only interpolated identifier is always one of two hardcoded literals, never user input). New `[tool.bandit]` in `pyproject.toml` skips only B101 (assert-used — this codebase never strips asserts and never relies on one as sole security enforcement) and B404 (bare `import subprocess` — the real risk lives in B603/B607 at each call site, which stay active) project-wide, since those two fire on the shape of the code rather than anything a per-site review could judge; a real re-run after all fixes: **zero findings**, so the CI step is blocking, not advisory. `gitleaks` similarly: a real scan against 99 commits of actual history found zero leaks; against the raw working tree (including gitignored files) it also flagged one true local-only `.env` value (confirmed never committed, `git log --all -- .env` is empty) and one real false positive in a tracked file (`SECRETS_VOLUME = "tradingos-2.0_secrets"`, a Docker volume *name*, not a secret) — scoped out by fingerprint in a new `.gitleaks.toml`, not a broad path exclusion, so anything else stays caught. New `secrets-scan` CI job (blocking) using `gitleaks/gitleaks-action@v2` with that config. `mypy --strict` and `pip-audit` both wired as `continue-on-error` steps in the existing backend job per the operator's advisory-only decision — the real 428-error and real-CVE-list findings above are exactly what those steps will report on every future build. A coverage gate (`pytest-cov`, `--cov-fail-under=70`) was added to the same job's `pytest` step; the exact current baseline percentage couldn't be cleanly measured in this session — a full coverage-instrumented run of the ~1030-test suite very nearly triples normal runtime and, on its first attempt, ran long enough to exhaust this sandbox's shared Docker resources and crash the `ci-pg`/`ci-redis` containers mid-run (an infrastructure failure, confirmed by recreating them fresh and re-running the full suite normally afterward with a clean result — not a real regression) — so 70% was chosen as a deliberate, standard, defensible floor rather than a precisely-measured one; CI itself will report the real number on every real run going forward.

  - **Phase 26 follow-up — dependency CVEs actually fixed, not left advisory** (operator's later, explicit instruction: "Fix the dependency CVEs first, then mypy" — reversing the earlier "advisory only for now" choice above now that the operator wanted them closed, not just visible in CI). The initial `pip-audit` this phase's own audit-pass ran found **54 vulnerabilities across 9 packages**; every one with a real fix version was closed by bumping `pyproject.toml`'s pin, each verified compatible first via `pip-compile --dry-run` (a safe, no-install resolver check) before committing to it: `fastapi` 0.115→`>=0.141,<0.142` (its own pin resolves a `starlette` carrying 6 real CVEs, PYSEC-2026-1941/1942/161/2281/2280/249/248, only fully fixed ≥1.3.1 — `starlette` isn't pinned directly in this project, so the fix had to travel through FastAPI's own compatible range); `langgraph` 0.2.76→`>=1.2,<1.3` (2 real CVEs, PYSEC-2026-83 fixed only in ≥1.0.10, PYSEC-2026-2194 — pulling compatible `langgraph-checkpoint`/`langgraph-sdk`/`langchain-core` versions along with it, each of which had its own real CVEs at the old resolved versions); `cryptography` 43.0.3→`>=50,<51` (5 CVEs at the old pin — PYSEC-2026-1284/2141/35/3553/3554, GHSA-537c-gmf6-5ccf — plus a 6th, PYSEC-2026-3552, disclosed *after* this pass's own initial scan and only caught by re-running `pip-audit` post-upgrade, a live reminder that the CVE database itself is continuously updated, not a static snapshot to check once); `scikit-learn` 1.4.2→`>=1.5,<1.6` (PYSEC-2024-110). **One real finding was deliberately left, not fixed, and documented in `pyproject.toml` itself**: `ecdsa` (pulled in transitively by `python-jose[cryptography]`'s EC-algorithm support) carries CVE-2024-23342 (PYSEC-2026-1325, GHSA-wj6h-64fc-37mp), a Minerva timing side-channel attack against P-256 ECDSA signing/keygen/ECDH — explicitly *not* signature verification — where the `ecdsa` project has stated side-channel attacks are out of scope for the library and there is no planned fix; swapping JWT libraries to chase it was rejected as disproportionate once checked against this app's actual runtime behavior: `settings.jwt_algorithm` (`src/core/config.py`) defaults to `"HS256"`, pure HMAC, so the vulnerable EC signing code path inside `ecdsa` is never reached by anything this codebase does — a real finding in the dependency tree, genuinely unreachable through this app's own usage, accepted and cited by CVE ID rather than silently ignored. **[Update 2026-10-12: reversed -- python-jose was replaced by PyJWT after all;
