see the "JWT library swap" entry at the end of this section. A new, unfixable python-jose CVE tipped the calculation, and the swap turned out to be a one-module change.]**
  - **A real regression the FastAPI/Starlette major-version bump itself introduced, found and fixed, not just tolerated**: diagnosed via a temporary debug print in `require_role` showing a live route's `request.scope["route"].path` coming back as `/broker-credentials/zerodha/login-url` while `request.url.path` correctly held `/api/v1/broker-credentials/zerodha/login-url` — a real FastAPI 0.141/Starlette 1.7 internal change where nested `include_router` composition is now represented via `_IncludedRouter` wrapper objects that don't flatten sub-routes into the parent's `.routes` the way the pinned-before versions did, so a leaf `APIRoute.path` can silently drop an ancestor router's prefix. First suspected as narrow — specific to the one place two sibling routers share an identical inner prefix (`broker_oauth.py` and `broker_credentials.py` both declare `prefix="/broker-credentials"`) — but a full-suite run against a stale pre-fix code snapshot proved it was pervasive: that run showed 27 failures total, but 15 of those were the same already-diagnosed pre-existing environmental gaps every full-suite run in this project hits (Docker-mount-missing-sibling-files, `chattr` missing, real NSE egress), leaving **12 real failures actually caused by this bug**, not just the broker-credentials case — spanning `test_agents_api.py` (3), `test_audit_trace_and_summary.py` (5), `test_broker_credentials_api.py` (1), `test_canvas_api.py` (1), and `test_observability_audit_middleware.py` (2) — every one traced to the same root cause, confirming this wasn't one route's quirk but a property of the framework change itself. This mattered for two genuinely security-relevant reasons, not just cosmetics: `src/core/rbac.py::require_role` looks up `_POLICY_TABLE[(method, path)]` keyed by the full `/api/v1/...`-prefixed path, so a truncated `path` would miss every registered policy entry and silently fall through to this dependency's own default-deny (fail-closed, so the practical blast radius was "legitimate requests wrongly 403'd," not an RBAC bypass — but still a real correctness bug in the enforcement path); and `src/observability/audit_middleware.py`'s generic per-request logger builds its permanent `action` string the same way, so an uncaught truncation would have written `"POST /broker-credentials/zerodha"` into the audit trail instead of the real `"POST /api/v1/broker-credentials/{broker}"` — a wrong, harder-to-trace record in a log this codebase's own Rule 7 treats as the system of record for every mutation. Fixed identically in both places with a general reconstruction, not a route-specific patch: if the route's own `.path` doesn't already start with `/api/v1` but the real request path does, prepend it. `tests/test_rbac.py::_all_app_routes()` had its own, adjacent breakage from the same root cause — walking `app.routes` directly returned almost nothing (`_IncludedRouter` wrappers expose neither `.methods` nor `.path`), meaning this test's own RBAC-completeness sweep had been silently checking almost no real routes — fixed by switching to `app.openapi()`, FastAPI's own stable public API, which has to resolve every route's true absolute path correctly since it *is* the app's real OpenAPI contract, rather than continuing to hand-walk private internals (`_IncludedRouter`/`original_router`) that had already changed shape once and could again. New regression test `test_middleware_action_carries_the_full_path_for_a_shared_prefix_router` (`backend/tests/test_observability_audit_middleware.py`) asserts the real audit-log `action` value for a `POST /api/v1/broker-credentials/{broker}` request keeps its full path template.
  - **Verified**: a full backend suite run against freshly recreated `ci-pg`/`ci-redis` containers (a coverage-instrumented attempt at this had earlier exhausted this sandbox's shared Docker resources and crashed both containers mid-run — an infrastructure failure, not a code issue, recovered by recreating them and re-running non-instrumented) — **1033 passed, 15 failed, zero real regressions**, the 15 the same already-diagnosed environmental cases (Docker-mount-missing-sibling-files, `chattr` missing, real NSE egress) every phase since 22 has hit, one more pass than the prior 1032 thanks to this pass's own new regression test. After the final `cryptography>=50,<51` bump (closing the newly disclosed PYSEC-2026-3552), a targeted re-run of `tests/test_auth.py`+`tests/test_broker_credentials_api.py` against the rebuilt image: **21 passed**. A final `pip-audit` against that same rebuilt image: **14 findings, all either the one documented-accepted `ecdsa` CVE (listed twice, once for the direct pin and once for the resolved version) or `pip` itself** (25.0.1, carrying several of its own known CVEs fixed in later pip releases) — `pip` is the container's package installer, not an entry in this project's own `pyproject.toml` dependency list, so it's outside this pass's actual scope; `pip-audit` also correctly reported it couldn't audit `tradingos-backend` itself (not published to PyPI, expected for a private application package). Net result: **54 vulnerabilities across 9 real pinned dependencies closed to 1 accepted, cited, unreachable finding** — the dependency-CVE half of the operator's "fix the CVEs, then mypy" instruction is complete; the 428-error `mypy --strict` backlog (`pyproject.toml`'s `[tool.mypy]` section) is the next, not-yet-started piece of that same instruction.
  - **Phase 26 follow-up — the 428-error `mypy --strict` backlog closed to zero**, the second half of the operator's "fix the CVEs, then mypy" instruction. Real stub packages closed the `[import-untyped]` class first (`pandas-stubs`, `types-passlib`, `types-python-jose`, `types-psutil` added to the `dev` extra; **`types-python-jose` has since been removed along with python-jose itself -- PyJWT ships its own type hints**), plus a `[[tool.mypy.overrides]]` for `apscheduler` (checked: no `APScheduler-stubs`/`types-apscheduler` exists on PyPI, so `ignore_missing_imports` is the honest option there, not a stub gap this project can close). The largest single class, `[type-arg]` (missing generic parameters on bare `dict`/`list`/`tuple`/`Series`/`Callable`/etc.), was closed with a small scripted fixer reading mypy's own file:line output and parameterizing each bare generic with `Any` (or `Any, ...` for `tuple`) plus adding the needed `Any` import — run in two passes, since mypy reports only one bare-generic error per source line even when a line has two (e.g. `def f(x: dict) -> dict:`), so a second full run was needed to catch what the first pass's fix newly exposed on the same lines. A handful of low-count generics (`Task`, `Popen`, `Column`, `async_sessionmaker`, `ColumnElement`) were reviewed and parameterized by hand instead of blindly, since each needed a real, specific type (`Popen[str]` — every real spawn passes `text=True`; `Task[None]`/`Task[RunStatus]` per each loop's real return; `Column[Any]`→`InstrumentedAttribute[Any]` once real call sites showed every caller passes an ORM-mapped class attribute, never a raw `Table` column).
  - **Real bugs found and fixed along the way, not just annotations added to satisfy the checker** — the same discipline this session's CVE-fix pass already established, applied here too: `src/brokers/base.py`'s `BrokerAdapter` Protocol declared `broker_name`/`has_sandbox` as plain (settable) attributes, but `ResilientBrokerAdapter` (the circuit-breaker wrapper every broker call actually goes through) implements both as read-only `@property` — a real structural mismatch mypy caught once `ResilientBrokerAdapter` was type-checked against the Protocol it's supposed to satisfy; fixed by declaring both as `@property` on the Protocol itself, since nothing anywhere in this codebase ever reassigns either after construction, so requiring settability was stricter than the interface actually needs. `src/brokers/zerodha.py`/`upstox.py`/`src/observability/vitals.py` all typed their `transport` constructor param as `httpx.BaseTransport` (the *sync* transport base) despite every one of them only ever constructing an `httpx.AsyncClient` with it — a real, previously-invisible type-correctness gap, fixed to `httpx.AsyncBaseTransport`. `src/api/routes/auth.py`'s refresh-token-family revocation called `RefreshToken.__table__.update()` (`FromClause` has no `.update()` method — this only worked because `Table.update()` happens to exist and Python's own duck typing let it through untyped) instead of the idiomatic `update(RefreshToken)` every other UPDATE in this codebase already uses; fixed to match the established convention, not just to satisfy mypy. `src/engine/risk/circuit_breaker.py`'s `BrokerCircuitBreaker.call()` was typed `-> Any` (a generic passthrough that genuinely can't know its caller's return type without a TypeVar) — made properly generic (`Callable[[], Awaitable[_T]]) -> _T`), which fixed all 8 of `ResilientBrokerAdapter`'s own call sites at once instead of casting each individually, and gives every future caller its real return type too, not just these 8. Several `[union-attr]`/`[return-value]` findings were real `None`-safety gaps closed with an `assert`, each backed by an actual invariant check, not a blind `# type: ignore`: `src/orchestration/run_control.py`'s `create_run` re-fetches the `OrganizationRun` row it just created and committed a few lines earlier in the same function (nothing in this codebase hard-deletes one, confirmed by grep); `src/orchestration/strategy_suggestions.py`'s `review_suggestion`/`regenerate_from_suggestion` fetch `StrategyVersion`/`Strategy` by a non-nullable FK (checked each model's own `ForeignKey(..., nullable=False)`); `src/engine/risk/circuit_breaker.py`'s `call()` itself had one un-guarded read of `_opened_at` where every code path that sets `_state = OPEN` sets `_opened_at` in the same statement, an invariant `cooldown_remaining_seconds` already asserted a few lines above but `call()` hadn't.
  - **A real regression this session's own earlier mypy work introduced, caught only by actually running the test suite** — mypy and `ruff` both stayed clean throughout, which is exactly why this one slipped past both: `pandas` 2.2.3's real `Series` class has no `__class_getitem__` (confirmed directly: `pd.Series[int]` raises `TypeError: type 'Series' is not subscriptable` at the real Python REPL) — `Series[Any]` is only ever a valid *type-checking-time* construct, via `pandas-stubs`' `.pyi` file, never something the real class supports at runtime. The bulk `[type-arg]` fixer script above correctly satisfied mypy by turning every bare `Series` into `Series[Any]` across 13 files, but had no way to know pandas itself can't evaluate that subscript — so every one of those files now crashed on `import` with exactly that `TypeError`, the instant Python evaluated the (previously bare, now subscripted) annotation. Caught by a full `pytest --collect-only` sweep (the cheapest real way to exercise every module's import path at once) immediately after the mypy pass reported zero errors, rather than assuming a clean mypy run meant a working app. Fixed with `from __future__ import annotations` (PEP 563 — defers every annotation's evaluation to a string, never actually evaluated unless something calls `typing.get_type_hints()`, which nothing in these files does) added as the first import in all 13 affected files (`src/engine/indicators.py`, `src/engine/backtest/{comparison,engine,signals}.py`, `src/engine/optimization/{hyperparameter_sweep,walk_forward}.py`, `src/engine/paper_trading/daily_signal.py`, `src/engine/risk/correlation_constraint.py`, `src/orchestration/{backtests,risk_gate,strategies}.py`, `src/api/routes/{backtests,market_data}.py`). **PEP 563 doesn't cover a plain type-alias assignment** (`SignalFromParamsFn = Callable[[...], pd.Series[Any]]` is a real expression, not an annotation, so it's evaluated eagerly regardless of the `__future__` import) — the two real instances of this (`src/engine/optimization/hyperparameter_sweep.py`, `walk_forward.py`) needed the `Series[Any]` portion quoted as a string forward-reference instead (`Callable[[...], "pd.Series[Any]"]`), which `Callable`'s own subscripting accepts as a deferred reference without evaluating it. The same fixer script surfaced one more, adjacent bug while adding this: `src/engine/indicators.py` already had `from __future__ import annotations` from an earlier phase, but the fixer's own `from typing import Any` insertion (always placed right after the docstring) landed *before* it — a real `SyntaxError` in Python (`from __future__` imports must be the first statement in a file, strictly enforced by the interpreter, unlike mypy which parses it correctly regardless of position) — fixed by reordering. **A related false alarm, not a bug**: the first full-suite verification run after this pass showed 14 new `test_agents_api.py` errors (`FileNotFoundError: config/tradingos.config.json`) — traced to this session's own verification workflow, not the code: switching from the earlier `-v backend:/app:rw` named-volume pattern to a faster host bind-mount for rapid mypy iteration dropped the accompanying `-v config:/app/config:ro` mount (`config/` is a repo-root sibling of `backend/`, never copied into the image, always supplied via that second mount) — confirmed by restoring it and re-running, all 14 passing cleanly.
  - **Verified**: `mypy --strict src` — **0 errors** (down from the original 428), the real number now, not a target. `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` on the full `src` tree: clean (249 files) after this pass's own new imports/annotations pushed a handful of lines past the 100-column limit (fixed by wrapping, not by raising the limit). A full backend suite run against freshly recreated `ci-pg`/`ci-redis` containers, with the `config/` mount correctly restored: **1033 passed, 15 failed, zero real regressions** — the same 15 already-diagnosed environmental cases (Docker-mount-missing-sibling-files for the container/compose/seccomp tests, `chattr` missing ×2, real NSE egress ×1) every full-suite run this session has hit, an exact match to the dependency-CVE pass's own final clean number above. Both halves of the operator's "fix the CVEs, then mypy" instruction are now complete.
  - **Phase 26 follow-up — CI made green (first real GitHub runs, #9–#11).** The first real runs of this workflow failed in two jobs; GitHub withholds raw logs without a login, so each failure was reproduced locally against CI's own conditions instead of guessed at. **Backend job**: reproduced with the whole repo mounted and coverage on — coverage was 86% against the 70% gate, and exactly three tests failed, all of which depended on the machine running them rather than on the code: `test_bhavcopy_fallback_trigger_honestly_reports_synthetic_source` assumed NSE's archive is unreachable (any machine with real egress, a laptop or a CI runner, gets real data back and the assertion fails) — fixed by routing the fallback's httpx transport through an overridable `get_bhavcopy_transport` dependency (`None`/real network in production) that the test file overrides with a `MockTransport` returning 503; and the two `test_audit_archive.py` tamper tests called `chattr -a` via `subprocess.run(check=False)`, which still raises `FileNotFoundError` when the binary is absent — the helper now tolerates that. Run #11 confirmed it: every Backend step, including `pytest (with coverage)`, passed. **E2E job**: reproduced in an Ubuntu 24.04 container as a non-root user with Node 24, Python 3.12, Chromium and Postgres/Redis on `localhost` — 7 of 8 specs passed and one (`live-autonomy.spec.ts:105`) failed because the page had been sent back to `/login`. The backend log showed 28 `429`s, including on CORS preflights: the Next dev app polls dozens of endpoints per page from one IP, ~300 requests/minute, exactly `settings.api_rate_limit_per_minute`'s default ceiling (Phase 21's blanket limiter), so a 429 on the page's session check bounced the browser to login. `tests/conftest.py` already disables the limiter for pytest for this reason; the E2E backend start never did. Fix: `API_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE=100000` on the E2E backend step in `ci.yml` and in `e2e/README.md`'s documented setup (production default untouched). Re-running the same reproduction with it set: 0 rate-limit rejections, 8/8 specs passed. **Two real findings left for follow-up, not fixed here**: (1) `components/auth/auth-provider.tsx`'s `rehydrate()` treats *any* failed `getMe()` on page load (429, 5xx, a network blip) as "no valid session" and sends the operator to the login screen even though their stored tokens are fine — only a real 401/refresh rejection should mean that; (2) `backend/build/` (243 files of build output) is tracked in git because `.gitignore`'s `/build` only matches the repo root, so every `pip install .` dirties the working tree.
  - **Both Phase 26 CI follow-ups above now closed.** (1) The session-restore bug: `lib/api.ts`'s `refreshTokens()` now reports ok/rejected/transient and only clears stored tokens on a real rejection (a 429/5xx/dropped connection no longer signs anyone out); `auth-provider.tsx` retries a transient `getMe()` failure (0.5s/1.5s/3s) and only a real rejection means signed out — if the server stays unreachable it enters a new `isUnreachable` state, retrying every 10s, and `shell-layout.tsx` shows a "Can't reach the TradingOS server" screen with a Retry button instead of redirecting to `/login`. New `e2e/session-resilience.spec.ts` (4 specs: no response at all, 429/503, unreachable-then-recovery, and a genuinely rejected session still going to `/login`); confirmed it can fail — run against the pre-fix source, the 3 transient-failure specs fail and the control spec passes. Full Playwright suite 12/12 and `tsc` clean in the CI-faithful container; CI run #13 green. (2) `backend/build/`: the 243 tracked files were all a stale `build/lib/src` copy from a past `pip install .` (nothing referenced them); untracked with `git rm -r --cached` and `backend/build/` added to `.gitignore` — verified a fresh `pip install .` regenerates the directory and leaves `git status` clean.
  - **Phase 19 Finding #1 closed — the live per-step agent feed.** The one piece Phase 19 left open ("the Live Activity Feed is the general audit trail, not per-step agent reasoning detail"): each step of a LangGraph pipeline run is now recorded and pushed live. **Design**: `src/agents/graph.py` gained a `PipelineEventSink` Protocol (`build_graph`/`run_pipeline` take an optional `event_sink`) so the graph stays DB-independent as before; the per-node wrapper that already timed every node now emits `step.started`/`step.completed` (with `step_index`, `duration_ms` and `_summarize_output`'s bounded account of what the node set — scalar fields, string length plus a 300-char preview, never whole objects)/`step.failed`, disabled agents emit `step.skipped`, and `run_pipeline` brackets the run with `pipeline.started`/`completed`/`failed`. Every emit goes through `_safe_emit`, so a sink that raises costs a missing feed row and a warning, never a failed strategy run. `src/orchestration/pipeline_events.py`'s `PipelineEventRecorder` implements the sink: each event commits in its own short-lived session (a run holds an LLM call open for seconds; the feed must show a step when it starts, not when the request's transaction ends) and is published to Redis `agent-pipeline-events:{run_id}`; persist and publish fail independently. **Why a new table, not `organizational_events`**: that table's `run_id` is a foreign key to `organization_runs` (the Phase 2 task-graph engine), which the LangGraph pipeline has never been part of — reusing it would have put phantom runs on the Mission Control Kanban. New `agent_pipeline_events` (migration `a3c91d7e5b42`, additive; real upgrade→downgrade→upgrade cycle verified), `sequence` gap-free per run without a lock since one run has exactly one writer. **API**: `POST /agents/pipeline/run` now mints a `run_id` and returns it; new `GET /agents/pipeline/events` (replay one run, or the most recent N across runs; all roles) and `/ws/agent-pipeline-events` (live, optional `?run_id=` filter, same auth/shape as `/organization-events`). **Deliberately not built**: LLM token streaming — Phase 19's audit suggested switching the nodes to `stream_complete`, but that streams for real only for Anthropic; every other provider's response is a finished completion re-chopped into word chunks, so a "live reasoning" view on it would be an invented typing effect on most deployments. Each step instead reports what is actually known: that it started, whether the LLM or the deterministic fallback answered (and which provider), how long it took, and a bounded summary of its output. **Frontend**: new `components/mission-control/agent-pipeline-panel.tsx` above Mission Control's existing grid — backfills from the REST route, appends live events (deduped by run+sequence), folds a run's events into one row per step (matched on node + `step_index`, since a node can run several times in one run), and has a "Run pipeline" form for SystemAdministrator/PortfolioManager (the roles `POST /pipeline/run` already allows). Nothing in the UI could start this pipeline before — only the API could. **Verified**: 18 new backend tests (`backend/tests/test_agents_pipeline_events.py`: paired started/completed ordering, step payloads, skipped agents, a failing node, a sink that raises, gap-free persisted sequence, Redis publish, publish surviving a dead database, history ordering, RBAC across roles, limit validation); full backend suite 1058 passed, 12 failed — the container/compose/seccomp tests that read repo-root files, which this run's backend-only mount lacks (they pass in CI, where the whole repo is checked out); mypy `--strict` 0 errors; ruff clean. 3 new Playwright specs (`e2e/agent-pipeline-feed.spec.ts`): a run started from *outside* an already-open panel appears live (so the steps can only have arrived over the WebSocket, not the one-time history backfill), a run started from the panel shows its steps, and a role that can't run the pipeline sees the feed but no form — full suite 15/15 and `tsc` clean in the CI-faithful container.

- **Agent long-term vector memory (backlog item 18, `docs/phase20-old-vs-new-comparison.md`) — built.** `backend/src/memory/` was a one-line stub; it is now `embeddings.py` (thin clients for Ollama `/api/embed`, OpenAI, Gemini `batchEmbedContents` and any OpenAI-compatible `/v1/embeddings` server; credentials come from the encrypted LLM provider store first, then the existing `OPENAI_API_KEY`/`GEMINI_API_KEY`/`OLLAMA_BASE_URL` settings, so there is no second place to paste a key; every response is validated for count, shape and a consistent dimension before it can reach the store), `store.py` (`QdrantMemoryStore`: one cosine collection per kind — `strategy`, `news`, `organization`, the old app's split — created lazily on first write once the dimension is known, queried with `query_points`), and `service.py` (`MemoryService`, the one object the API and the pipeline share). **No fallback embedder, on purpose**: a hash or bag-of-words vector would "work" and return confident-looking nonsense, so with no `QDRANT_URL`, no `MEMORY_EMBEDDING_PROVIDER`/`MEMORY_EMBEDDING_MODEL` (deliberately no default — an embedding model is a real choice), or no credentials, memory is *unavailable* with a reason naming the exact setting. `status()` does not trust configuration: it sends one tiny embed request (cached 60 s) — found necessary live, because the host's Ollama had only `qwen2.5:0.5b`, a chat model that answers HTTP 501 to `/api/embed`, which a config-only check would have reported as online. A changed embedding model is a `DimensionMismatchError` (HTTP 409), never a silent re-create that would destroy what the agents learned. **Pipeline wiring** follows the `PipelineEventSink` pattern: `src.agents.graph.AgentMemory` is a Protocol (the graph stays infra-independent), `run_pipeline(memory=)` recalls the closest `strategy` lessons for the objective once up front into the new `TradingOSGraphState.memory_context` (appended *after* the task in `_with_active_prompt`, so task framing still leads), the `memory_ingest` node — which until now only bumped a counter — also writes each rejection, and the run's outcome is written at the end; `PipelineMemory` swallows every failure and the graph wraps calls again, so memory can only ever add context to a run, never fail one. **[Update 2026-10-12: the recording half of this was a bug and was removed -- `memory_ingest` no longer writes rejections, no outcome is written, `_outcome_lesson` and `_strategy_label` are gone, and the `AgentMemory` Protocol now lives in `src.memory.lessons`. See the "Memory lessons fix" entry at the end of this list.]** The run emits a `memory.recalled` pipeline event and the Mission Control feed shows the lesson count. **API** (`/api/v1/memory`): `GET /status` and `GET /query` (all roles; reads are GETs so the audit middleware doesn't log queries as mutations), `POST /{kind}` (SystemAdministrator/PortfolioManager, the roles that can already run the pipeline; stamps `source`/`author`), `DELETE /{kind}/{id}` (admin only); 503 carries the unavailable reason, 409 a dimension conflict, 422 bad input. **Infra**: `docker-compose.yml` gains a `qdrant` service (`qdrant/qdrant:v1.19.1`, named volume, no published port, healthcheck via bash `/dev/tcp` since the image has neither curl nor wget — verified against the real image) and backend env `QDRANT_URL`/`MEMORY_EMBEDDING_*`; the backend deliberately does not `depends_on` it. `Dockerfile.allinone` has no Qdrant, so memory reads as unavailable there. **Frontend**: `components/mission-control/agent-memory-panel.tsx` — honest unavailable state with the backend's reason, otherwise per-collection counts, search, add (write roles) and delete (admin). **Verified**: 51 new backend tests (`backend/tests/test_memory.py`; real search against an in-process `AsyncQdrantClient(":memory:")`, embedding clients against `httpx.MockTransport`, graph recall/rejection/outcome/exploding-memory paths, RBAC, 422/409/503 mappings; the only fake is a test-only word-hash embedder, asserted to have no production counterpart); store/service also exercised against a real Qdrant v1.19.1 server container; full backend suite 1124 passed (1049 in the main run + the 73 sandbox tests re-run, + the 2 probe tests added after) once the sandbox tests ran in a container permitting `unshare` (73 of them fail without `--cap-add SYS_ADMIN` + relaxed seccomp — an environment limit of the dev container, not of this change), mypy `--strict` 0 errors (255 files), ruff clean, `tsc` clean. **Live-verified with a real embedding model** (`ollama pull nomic-embed-text`, 768-dim, via the rebuilt docker stack's backend container against the compose `qdrant` service): status reports online after the embed probe; related texts score 0.81 vs 0.51 for an unrelated one; two real `run_pipeline` runs on similar NIFTY objectives — the first recalled nothing, the second recalled the first run's recorded outcome from Qdrant (an "outcome" built from stand-in results -- see the update above); test memories were deleted afterwards. That live run exposed a weak lesson (a run stopped by code validation was recorded as 'evaluated none', labelled 'llm'); `_outcome_lesson` now says why a run stopped before evaluation (compliance block / validation failed after N attempts) and `_strategy_label` no longer uses the answering source — tested. **Not verified**: the panel in a browser against the live stack (the Console needs a login and no session was available to the verifier), and `e2e/agent-memory.spec.ts`'s with-memory-online tests (CI's backend has no Qdrant, so only its unavailable-state test runs there). **[Update 2026-10-12: the panel has since been seen in a real browser (screenshots from the CI-faithful E2E container -- not your live stack) and the memory-online tests have run and pass; see the last entry of this list.]** To enable: choose the provider + model in Mission Control > Agent memory (persisted in the database — see the follow-up entry below); `MEMORY_EMBEDDING_*` env vars remain only as a fallback.

- **Agent memory settings persisted in the database, not `.env` (follow-up to the entry above).** The embedding provider + model were env-only, so a choice made for a running stack lived in a compose override or `.env` and was lost on a rebuild. They are now a singleton row in a new `memory_settings` table (model `src/models/memory_settings.py`, `CHECK (id = 1)`, no serial; migration `b8e24f6a1c97`, additive, upgrade→downgrade→upgrade and `alembic check` verified on a scratch database). `MemoryService.effective_choice()` resolves **saved row → `MEMORY_EMBEDDING_*` env → unset**, reports which one won (`source`), and falls back to env if the table is unreadable rather than taking memory down; the row holds no credentials (those stay in the encrypted LLM provider store, and a model name is not a secret, which is why it can be shown back in the UI). API: `GET /api/v1/memory/settings` (all roles), `PUT` and `DELETE` (SystemAdministrator only; mutating, so the audit middleware records them); provider is validated against ollama|openai|gemini|custom. The service stays a process singleton (one Qdrant client); `memory_service_dependency` re-binds its override source to the request app's own session factory each call, because that factory is per-app (per-test in the suite) — the pipeline-run endpoint uses the same dependency, so a run recalls with the saved choice. UI: an admin-only form on the Agent memory panel (also shown while memory is unavailable, since that is where you fix it) with save/reset and a "Saved by …" line. `docker-compose.yml` also gains a default `OLLAMA_BASE_URL=http://host.docker.internal:11434` for the backend (`extra_hosts: host-gateway` was already there for exactly this): the code default `localhost:11434` can never reach a host-side Ollama from inside the container, and a base URL saved in Settings > LLM providers still wins. **Verified**: 9 new backend tests (saved choice beats env, env applies when nothing is saved, broken table → env fallback, admin-only writes, validation, reset, persistence across requests; test_memory.py now 60 tests), full backend suite 1133 passed (sandbox-capable container), mypy `--strict` 0 errors (257 files), ruff clean, `tsc` clean; live — rebuilt both images, started the stack with **no override file and empty `MEMORY_EMBEDDING_*`**, saved `ollama` / `nomic-embed-text` into the live database (done once by calling the app's own `save_choice` from inside the backend container, actor `setup (operator-requested persistence)` — not through the UI, so that one write is not in the audit log), restarted the backend container, and confirmed the choice came back as `database`, status online, and a real embed→Qdrant→search round trip scored 0.74. **Not verified**: the settings form in a browser (login needed) and the two new Playwright tests (`e2e/agent-memory.spec.ts`, 5 tests total now) — they are written to be deterministic in CI (they need no Ollama) but have not been run. **[Update 2026-10-12: they have since run and pass -- in CI, and in the container with memory online; see the last entry of this list.]** A transient moment during this work had the host Ollama's HTTP port refusing connections while its CLI still answered; it recovered on its own, and memory's status line correctly read "embedding failed … connection failed" while it lasted.

- **Docker image build-and-publish to GHCR (backlog item 30, `docs/phase20-old-vs-new-comparison.md`) — built.** New `images` job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, a two-entry matrix that `needs` every other job (secrets-scan, backend, e2e, frontend). **It builds on every run** — pull requests included, which is itself new coverage: nothing in CI built a Dockerfile before, so a broken one would only have surfaced at someone's next `docker compose build` — and **pushes only on a `push` event** (main, or a new `v*` tag trigger) that already passed everything else; PRs build and discard. Tags via `docker/metadata-action`: `sha-<short>` always, `latest` only from the default branch, `<version>` / `<major>.<minor>` from `v*` tags; image names are lowercased explicitly (`${GITHUB_REPOSITORY,,}`) since GHCR rejects the repo's mixed case; BuildKit layer cache in the GitHub Actions cache, scoped per image; `packages: write` is granted on this job only, the workflow default stays `contents: read`. **What is published, and the one deliberate omission**: `ghcr.io/ashfaque386/tradingos-2.0-backend` (`backend/Dockerfile`) and `…-allinone` (`Dockerfile.allinone`, whose empty `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` makes it host-agnostic by design). The root frontend-only image is **not** published — Next.js inlines `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` at build time (default `http://localhost:8000`), so a registry copy would silently work only against a backend on localhost:8000; the README says to build that one locally with the real URL. README gains a "Published images (GHCR)" section with the `docker run` for the all-in-one image (seccomp profile, secrets volume, `SECRETS_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, `JWT_SECRET_KEY`). **Verified**: `actionlint` clean; YAML parses with the expected job graph; `Dockerfile.allinone` built locally from a clean context (97 s at the final step) and the README's exact `docker run` was started — `/health` 200, `/login` served, `/api/v1/memory/status` 401 unauthenticated (the app, nginx and frontend all up behind one port), container reported healthy, then removed. **Verified on GitHub (run #20, commit `b0388c1`)**: all six jobs green; both image jobs actually ran Log in → Build and push (not skipped); the registry holds `ghcr.io/ashfaque386/tradingos-2.0-backend` and `…-allinone`, each tagged `latest` and `sha-b0388c1`; the workflow token could write packages with no settings change. The packages are **public** (anonymous token + manifest + tag list all answered 200; they follow this public repo's visibility — an earlier version of this entry and the README wrongly said they start private, corrected) — and the published all-in-one image was pulled anonymously and started with the README's `docker run`: `/health` 200, login page served. **Not yet exercised**: the `v*` tag path (versioned tags), and the PR path (build-and-discard) — neither has had a triggering event.

- **Backlog item 21 (agent run lifecycle controls) — audited; mostly already built, one real gap left.** The comparison doc said it "wasn't exhaustively checked"; it is now. **Built (backend + UI)**: `POST /orchestration/runs/{id}/pause` (RUNNING→PAUSED), `/continue` (PAUSED→RUNNING, then drives the run), `/retry` (FAILED→RUNNING; a permanent failure is refused with 409), `/rerun` (a fresh run linked by `source_run_id`, never inheriting `run_type`) — all via race-safe `conditional_transition`s, RBAC-gated to SystemAdministrator/PortfolioManager/RiskManager, with Mission Control buttons (`app/mission-control/page.tsx`); and the analytics half (`GET /agents/analytics/summary` and `/trend`, tested). **Test gap found and closed**: only rerun had an HTTP-level test and `continue_run` had none — added 11 (`backend/tests/test_orchestration_run_control.py`: continue drains a paused run, continue is a no-op from any other status, pause→continue over HTTP, pause on a non-running run is a 200 no-op **[Update 2026-10-13: now a 409 -- see the "No-op controls" entry at the end of this list]**, retry transient vs. permanent (409, run left FAILED), all four controls 403 for a read-only auditor, 404 for an unknown run); file is 21 passing. **Real gap, not built**: there is **no cancel** — `RunStatus` has no cancelled state and no endpoint (old app: pause/resume/cancel/retry/rerun). **[Update 2026-10-12: built -- see the two cancel entries below.]** **Also not found**: the old app's per-agent "narration". Left as a decision rather than built: a cancel needs a new terminal status (an enum/transition change, plus deciding what happens to the run's in-flight tasks), which is a design choice, not a thin endpoint. A pause on a run that isn't running returns 200 with the unchanged run rather than an error — existing behavior, now pinned by a test.

- **Pipeline-run cancel (backlog item 21's companion, "design B") — built.** The LangGraph pipeline (`POST /agents/pipeline/run`) is where runs take long enough to cancel (real LLM calls, seconds to minutes) and had no controls at all; the organization-run engine's runs finish in milliseconds today (every capability is an instant stub), so cancel matters here first. **Cooperative, checked only between steps**: `src.agents.graph` gains a `CancelSignal` Protocol (`is_cancelled()`, same infra-independence pattern as `PipelineEventSink`/`AgentMemory`) and `PipelineCancelledError` (carries the state as of the last *completed* step, the node that would have run next, and its index). Every node boundary — real nodes and the skip stand-ins for disabled agents — asks the signal before it starts anything; a step already executing is never interrupted, so an in-flight LLM call finishes and its result is kept. `run_pipeline` emits a `pipeline.cancelled` event (`node`, `step_index`, `steps_completed`) and re-raises; a cancelled run is not a result, so **nothing is recorded to agent memory for it** (recall up front still happens). An unreadable signal (Redis down) counts as "not cancelled" — fail open, a flaky cancel channel must never kill a healthy run. **Transport**: the run executes inside the HTTP request that started it, so the cancel arrives on another request and reaches it through a Redis flag `agents:pipeline:cancel:{run_id}` (1 h TTL bounding an unconsumed flag; the run clears it on the way out), exactly the shape of the chat stream's abort flag; `PipelineEventRecorder.is_cancelled()` reads it fresh each time. **API**: `POST /agents/pipeline/runs/{run_id}/cancel` (SystemAdministrator/PortfolioManager — the roles that can start a run) → **202** `{status: "cancel_requested"}` because nothing has stopped yet; **404** for an id no event was ever recorded under; **409** for a run that already has a terminal event; **503** if Redis can't take the flag. Run state is derived from the durable event log (`pipeline_run_state`: unknown / running / finished). The 409 is deliberate beyond tidiness: the audit middleware writes a row for every 2xx and records only actor/path/ids, so a 200 no-op would leave a "cancel" row in the permanent audit trail for something that never happened — the 202 is audited, the refusals are not (tested). `POST /pipeline/run` returns `cancelled: true` for a stopped run, with `node_log`/verdicts describing only what finished. **Frontend**: the pipeline panel shows a "Cancel run" button (run roles only) while the latest run is running, then "Cancelling… — stops when the current step finishes" until the run's own `pipeline.cancelled` event flips the status to CANCELLED — it never claims a stop that hasn't happened. **Verified**: 18 new backend tests (`backend/tests/test_agents_pipeline_cancel.py`): stop before the first step; stop mid-run keeps finished work; the in-progress step finishes and the next never starts; a skipped step is a checkpoint; no memory write; a raising signal doesn't break a run; Redis flag fresh-read/TTL/isolation per run; run-state derivation; and HTTP tests that cancel a run genuinely held open mid-flight on a second request (202 → the run ends `cancelled`, events end in `pipeline.cancelled`, flag cleaned up), idempotent double cancel, role matrix (401/403/202), 404/409, audit row for the 202 only, 503 on a Redis outage with the run left going. A mutation check (disabling the boundary check) made 8 of them fail. Related suites 209 passed; mypy `--strict` 0 errors; ruff clean; `tsc` clean. **Known limits**: ~~granularity is one step — a long LLM call must finish first~~ **[Update 2026-10-13: fixed -- a cancel now abandons the step in progress; see the "Mid-step cancel" entry at the end of this list]**; a run whose process died leaves no terminal event, so it still reads "running" and a cancel on it is accepted but never consumed (the pre-existing feed has the same blind spot); **[Update 2026-10-13: fixed -- see the "Dead pipeline runs" entry at the end of this list.]** no Playwright test, because CI's stack has no LLM and finishes a run in milliseconds — there is no way to hold one open there without fabricating events.

- **Organization-run cancel (backlog item 21's one real gap, "design A") — built.** Pause, continue, retry and rerun already existed; cancel did not (`RunStatus` had no cancelled state, no endpoint). **Model**: `RunStatus.CANCELLED` and `TaskStatus.CANCELLED`, both terminal, deliberately *not* `FAILED`+permanent — a deliberate stop must not read as a failure in the UI/analytics, nor be retryable like one. `CANCELLABLE_STATUSES` = pending, planning, running **and paused** (a paused run is exactly the kind someone abandons; it is in neither the terminal nor the crash-recovery set). Migration `c4a7e1b95d03` (additive): `ALTER TYPE run_status / task_status ADD VALUE IF NOT EXISTS 'cancelled'` — verified on Postgres 16 inside Alembic's transaction (the new label just can't be *used* until commit, and the migration only adds it). Postgres cannot drop an enum label, so the downgrade leaves the two unused labels and instead remaps the data onto the old vocabulary (cancelled run → failed + permanent with an explanatory `error`, cancelled task → unsatisfiable); upgrade → seed cancelled rows → downgrade (remap checked) → upgrade (idempotent) → `alembic check` clean on a scratch database. **`POST /orchestration/runs/{id}/cancel`** (admin / portfolio manager / risk manager, same as pause): 200 + the run, 404 unknown, **409** if it already finished. 409 rather than pause's 200 no-op (**[Update 2026-10-13: pause, continue and retry are 409 now too]**) for the same reason as the pipeline cancel: the audit middleware writes a row for every 2xx and records only actor/path/ids, so a no-op 200 would put a "cancel" row in the permanent trail for something that never happened (tested: the 200 is audited, the 409 is not). No free-text reason in v1 (the audit trail keeps no request bodies and the UI would never show one); the actor and task counts ride on a `run.cancelled` event instead. **Atomicity**: `conditional_transition` gained an optional `from_statuses` set (exactly one of it / `from_status`), so cancel is one atomic UPDATE that decides every race (vs pause, continue, another cancel); `cancel_run` then locks the unfinished tasks with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`, reads each one's prior state and cancels them in the same transaction (an `UPDATE … RETURNING` reports the *new* status — my first version counted zero in-flight tasks because of exactly that, caught by a test). A crash between the run flip and the task sweep leaves a terminal run with a few unfinished tasks, which is harmless: nothing drives a terminal run and crash recovery only revives non-terminal ones. **What a cancel cannot do**: capabilities run in a thread pool and Python threads cannot be killed (spiked: after `asyncio.wait_for` times out the thread still runs to completion), so a task already executing keeps running — cancel stops *further* work and discards the late result, it does not undo anything. Finished tasks keep their outcome and artefacts. **The engine writes that would have undone a cancel were unconditional (`WHERE id = …` only) and are now guarded**: the task start (`AND status = CLAIMED` — a task cancelled between claim and start never runs its capability), the succeeded write and `_mark_task_failed` (`AND status = RUNNING` — a late result keeps the task CANCELLED, writes no `ResultArtefact`, wakes no dependents, cascades no UNSATISFIABLE; a `task.result_discarded` event records that the work happened and was thrown away), and the planner's `RUNNING` / `CANNOT_PLAN` flips (`AND status IN (pending, planning)` — a cancel landing mid-planning rolls the just-produced plan and tasks back via the new `RunNoLongerPlannableError` instead of being overwritten back to RUNNING and executed; `create_run`/`rerun_run` return the cancelled run without a spurious `run.cannot_plan` event). Already safe by construction (each now pinned by a test): the driver re-reads the run status before every batch, crash recovery only touches non-terminal runs, the stall sweep only looks at RUNNING tasks, dependency promotion only touches PENDING tasks, retry/continue refuse a cancelled run (rerun still works on one), and the agent analytics' success rate and average duration count only succeeded/failed tasks (a cancelled task with a huge duration does not skew them; `tasks_total` still counts it as assigned). **Frontend (Mission Control)**: the run inspector drawer gets a "Cancel run" button for planning/running/paused runs with an inline confirm that says what it can and cannot do ("cannot be resumed — use Rerun"; "a task already executing cannot be interrupted: it finishes, but its result is discarded") and a "Keep it" way out; Rerun is also offered for cancelled runs; a "Cancelled" label, slate accent and slate task dots; and — a pre-existing gap fixed on the way — run actions no longer swallow errors silently, so a cancel that lost a race shows the server's 409 message. **Verified**: 30 backend tests (`backend/tests/test_orchestration_run_cancel.py`: cancel from every non-terminal state with a task of every status; refusals for finished/unknown runs; one event with actor and counts; two cancels / cancel×pause / cancel×continue races; a task finishing or failing *after* the cancel while its thread was genuinely blocked mid-flight; start-race; cancel during a good and a failing plan; recovery / stall sweep / retry / continue / rerun; HTTP 200/409/404/403/401, audit row for the 200 only; analytics), existing orchestration suites untouched (200 passed with the guards in), full backend suite 1192 passed, and five **mutation checks** — removing each new guard (succeeded write, failed write, start write, planner flip) or reverting cancel to a single source status makes its test fail; mypy `--strict` 0 errors, ruff clean, `tsc` clean. **Real-browser E2E** in the CI-faithful container (real backend + Next.js + Chromium): new `e2e/run-cancel.spec.ts` (3 tests) passes — cancel a paused run through the confirm step and check each task server-side (finished kept, ready/blocked cancelled), "Keep it" leaves it paused, and a cancel racing someone else's is reported instead of ignored. Since a run cannot be left paused through the API (every capability is an instant stub), `backend/scripts/seed_e2e_paused_run.py` plants one directly, the way `seed_e2e_live_intent.py` plants a live-eligible strategy (documented in `e2e/README.md`). That same run was the first time `e2e/agent-memory.spec.ts`'s settings-form tests executed anywhere: full suite 21 passed, 2 skipped (the two memory-online tests, which need a Qdrant + embedding provider). **Known limits**: only the first part of "pause/resume/cancel/retry/rerun + per-agent narration" is now complete — the old app's per-agent narration was not found and is not built; capabilities are still instant stubs, so cancelling a *running* organization run is, in practice, still only reachable for the milliseconds a run is in flight (the machinery is exercised by tests that hold a capability open) — the pipeline cancel above is where it matters today.

- **Memory-online E2E run and doc sweep (2026-10-12).** **E2E**: ran the whole Playwright suite in the CI-faithful container with memory genuinely online -- a real Qdrant v1.19.1 server beside it, the backend given `QDRANT_URL` and `MEMORY_EMBEDDING_PROVIDER=ollama` / `nomic-embed-text` against the host's real Ollama -- the first time the memory-online tests (and the "online" branch of the availability test) had ever executed. First pass: 22 passed, 2 failed, both with the same cause, **a bug in my own spec that the tests' permanent skip had hidden**: `getByLabel('New memory')` also matches the "New memory kind" dropdown (Playwright label matching is substring-based), so the add/search/delete test died on a strict-mode violation. Fixed with `{ exact: true }`, and the test now waits for the add to finish before searching (the panel clears the input only once the memory is embedded and written) instead of racing the write. Re-run: all five agent-memory tests (three always-on, two memory-online), the three pipeline-feed specs and a throwaway screenshot spec pass (9/9). The pipeline-feed specs also ran against online memory, so real pipeline runs recorded real outcome lessons into Qdrant during a real browser session (the screenshots show them). **Looking at the real data turned up two weaknesses in what the pipeline writes (not changed, flagged)**: (1) each run records an outcome lesson, and the same objective produces *identical* text stored as separate points -- repeated runs accumulate duplicates, so recall's top 3 can be three copies of one past outcome instead of three different lessons; a deterministic point id (hash of kind + text) would make identical lessons collapse into one. (2) Success lessons ("evaluated pass ... deployed to paper") rank above a human note for a query like "what went wrong with momentum strategies" (similarity 0.70 vs 0.67 for the note that actually answers it) -- a property of the embedding model matching on shared words, but it argues for recording only notable outcomes (rejections, failures, blocks) rather than every pass. **[Update 2026-10-12: both are resolved by the "Memory lessons fix" entry at the end of this list -- ids are now a hash of kind + text, and a normal run records nothing at all. Looking harder at what "notable" meant also showed that the rejection and outcome lessons had been recording stand-in results as fact; that was the larger problem.]** `e2e/README.md` gains a "Memory-online tests" section with the environment to run them (use a throwaway Qdrant -- the pipeline specs write real lessons into it). **Doc sweep**: `e2e/README.md` (it listed four specs and said the sandbox has no Docker; now all eight specs and the accurate reason), `docs/phase20-old-vs-new-comparison.md` (every item in section B now ends with a dated Status line, the summary table gains a Status column, and two audit misses are recorded -- item 27's continuous chain check already existed since Phase 11, and item 25's regulatory data was checked and is a thin placeholder: an empty position-limit table and circuit bands for only NIFTY/BANKNIFTY, everything else defaulting to a flat 10% / 20%), `docs/phase19-audit.md` (a status pointer), and four stale claims in this file (the frontend row said "currently running on mock data"; Phase 19's "Finding #1 remains the next step"; Phase 26's "e2e job not verified on GitHub's runners"; the item-21 audit's "there is no cancel"). One thing the sweep also confirmed in `ci.yml` and recorded under item 28: `mypy --strict` and `pip-audit` are still `continue-on-error` -- they report on every build but do not block a merge, even though mypy is at 0 errors. **[Correction, same day: `pip-audit` is *not* clean. Reproduced against the project's dependencies it reports 17 findings in 4 packages -- `ecdsa` (the documented, unfixable one), `python-jose` CVE-2026-85394 (new, no fix version; an HS256 algorithm-confusion bug that needs an asymmetric public key used as the HMAC secret with `algorithms` unrestricted -- not this app, whose `jwt.decode` pins `algorithms=[settings.jwt_algorithm]` and verifies with a symmetric secret), dev-only `pytest` 8.4.2 (fix 9.0.3, blocked by the `<9.0` pin) and `pip` itself -- and run #24's backend job carries exactly one failure annotation ("Process completed with exit code 1"), i.e. that advisory step failed while being tolerated. The item-28 wording I wrote earlier in this sweep said the CVEs were fixed save one; that was taken from the Phase 26 record, not re-checked.]**

- **JWT library swap: python-jose → PyJWT (2026-10-12).** **Why**: re-running `pip-audit` (previous entry) showed `python-jose` 3.5.0 with a new CVE on top of its transitive `ecdsa` finding -- CVE-2026-85394 / GHSA-3qf3-8w2g-rqmx, an HS256 algorithm-confusion bug (HMAC initialisation accepts a DER-encoded public key as the secret) with **no fix version**, an incomplete fix of CVE-2024-33663. Neither finding was exploitable here (`decode_token` pins `algorithms=[settings.jwt_algorithm]` and the key is a symmetric secret), but two unfixable findings in the one library that decides who is logged in, from a poorly maintained project, made Phase 26's "swapping JWT libraries is disproportionate" call the wrong one to keep: only `src/core/security.py` imported `jose` (three call sites and one `except`). **What changed**: `security.py` (`import jwt`; `except jwt.PyJWTError`; a comment at the decode call saying `algorithms` is one configured value, never read from the token), `pyproject.toml` (`PyJWT>=2.15,<3.0` replaces `python-jose[cryptography]`; `types-python-jose` dropped because PyJWT ships `py.typed`; `cryptography` stays a direct dependency; a pytest `filterwarnings` entry silences only `jwt.warnings.InsecureKeyLengthWarning` for the suite). A clean-venv install of the new `pyproject.toml` no longer contains `python-jose`, `ecdsa`, `rsa` or `pyasn1`. **Behaviour tested empirically on PyJWT 2.15.1 before choosing the pin** (it is newer than anything I knew): it rejects `alg=none`, HS384/HS512 signed with the *right* secret, expired, payload-tampered, wrong-key and garbage tokens; claims must be JSON-serialisable and `sub` must be a string (the app already stringifies `sub`/`jti`); an HMAC key shorter than 32 bytes **warns, it does not fail**. **Compatibility, in both directions**: tokens minted by python-jose -- literal fixtures in the new tests, minted with the old library using the app's exact payload shapes -- verify under the new code, so nobody is signed out by the upgrade; and tokens minted by the new code verify under python-jose (one-off check), so a rollback would not sign anyone out either. **Verified**: 29 new tests (`backend/tests/test_security_jwt.py`: the two old-library fixtures, plus rotation still being a hard cutover for them; round trips with every claim; a minted token checked against an independent HMAC-SHA256 computation, i.e. plain standard HS256; rejection of other-secret / `alg=none` / HS384 / HS512 / expired / tampered-role / stripped-signature / wrong-type tokens; twelve malformed-input shapes asserting the only exception that ever escapes is `InvalidTokenError`, never a library exception that would be a 500 on every protected route; the algorithm pinned from settings rather than the token; a short secret still working but warning); three mutation checks -- unpinned algorithm, signature not verified, expiry not checked -- each make the suite fail; full backend suite **1221 passed** in a clean venv where python-jose is not installed; `mypy` 0 errors (257 files) without the stub package; ruff clean; the full Playwright suite in the CI-faithful container, built from the new `pyproject.toml`, 21 passed / 2 skipped (every spec logs in; the live feed authenticates its WebSocket); `pip-audit` in the clean venv went from **17 findings in 4 packages to 1** -- dev-only `pytest` 8.4.2, whose fix needs the `<9.0` pin lifted. **Not done**: `pip-audit` and `mypy` are still `continue-on-error` in `ci.yml` (and the pytest finding means `pip-audit` would still exit 1); the live stack has not been rebuilt, so it still runs python-jose until it is. **[Update 2026-10-12: it has since been rebuilt and runs PyJWT 2.15.1 -- checked inside the backend container.]** **A finding made on the way [Update 2026-10-12: addressed -- see the "Signing-key hardening" entry at the end of this list; the live key itself is still the placeholder until an administrator rotates it]**: the live stack's JWT signing key is the public placeholder `change-me-in-production` (`jwt_signing_keys`: `created_by = env-seed`, length 23, seeded 2026-09-24 from a `.env` copied from `.env.example`, which ships that line uncommented above a comment telling you to generate a real one). Since Phase 21 the key lives in the database and the **DB row wins, so editing `.env` now changes nothing** -- the fix is `POST /api/v1/system/jwt-signing-key/rotate` (SystemAdministrator; mints a 64-character key and, by design, signs everyone out). Impact: the role comes from the database user record, so a forged token can only impersonate an existing active user, but anyone who can reach the API and learns a user id could mint that user's token. Ports are loopback-bound by default, which limits the exposure. PyJWT's key-length warning will make this visible in the backend log once the stack is rebuilt. **[Update 2026-10-12: rebuilt, and the warning is not in the log yet -- PyJWT warns when a token is minted or verified, and nobody has logged in since the rebuild; I did not trigger it, so this is still unconfirmed.]**

- **CI run #25 failed on gitleaks (2026-10-12) -- and why it only failed in CI.** The PyJWT push (`ec93732`) turned `Secret scanning (gitleaks)` red; because the `images` job `needs` it, **both image jobs were skipped, so no image containing PyJWT was published** (the registry still held the previous build). Backend (pytest, mypy, bandit), tsc and E2E all passed. **Cause**: my test-fixture constant `KEY = "compat-fixture-key-..."` in `backend/tests/test_security_jwt.py` matched the `generic-api-key` rule (entropy 5.07). It is a public key that only signs the literal fixture tokens beside it, not a secret -- but a scanner cannot know that. **Why I could not reproduce it at first**: a local scan with `zricethezav/gitleaks:latest` (8.30.1) found nothing, on the exact range and arguments the action uses. The action does not run the latest gitleaks: **`gitleaks-action@v2` defaults to 8.24.3** (`src/index.js`: `GITLEAKS_VERSION || "8.24.3"`), and `.pre-commit-config.yaml` pinned a third version (8.21.2), so the local hook is no guarantee either. With `zricethezav/gitleaks:v8.24.3` the failure reproduced immediately as exactly one finding. **Fix**: an inline `# gitleaks:allow` on the fixture line with the reason above it; a new `.gitleaksignore` listing by fingerprint the already-pushed finding plus one older false positive that the full-history scan under 8.24.3 also reports (the Docker volume *name* quoted in `docs/CLAUDE.md` prose, which predates the allowlist -- Phase 26's "zero leaks in the full history" was true for the version used then); `GITLEAKS_VERSION: 8.24.3` made explicit in `ci.yml` with the reproduce command in a comment; the pre-commit hook pinned to the same version. **Verified** under 8.21.2, 8.24.3 and 8.30.1: the fixture file scans clean (the inline allow is honoured) and the full history scans clean, and a simulated fix commit scans clean over the action's exact push range; `actionlint` clean. **Lesson**: to reproduce a failing CI scan, use the action's pinned version, not `latest`.

- **CI runners pinned to `ubuntu-24.04` (2026-10-12).** All five jobs in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (`secrets-scan`, `backend`, `e2e`, `frontend`, `images`) said `runs-on: ubuntu-latest`. GitHub's changelog (2026-09-17, "Ubuntu 26 generally available and latest migration", github.blog/changelog/2026-09-17-ubuntu-26-generally-available-and-latest-migration) says it moves that label to Ubuntu 26.04 gradually between 2026-10-19 and 2026-11-19, with `ubuntu-26.04` available to opt into now and pinning `ubuntu-24.04` as the way to stay -- and this pipeline leans on exactly the things that differ between releases: the AppArmor unprivileged-user-namespace restriction the strategy sandbox's tests have to work around in the `backend` job, Playwright's system libraries, and the apt package set. All five now say `ubuntu-24.04`, with a comment at the top of the file giving the date and the way to try 26 (change one job's label to `ubuntu-26.04` on a branch and watch it run); the AppArmor comment now reads "ubuntu-24.04 (pinned)" instead of asserting that `ubuntu-latest` is 24.04. Today `ubuntu-latest` *is* 24.04, so the pin changes nothing yet -- its value is that a passing `main` stays passing on the date GitHub picks. **Verified**: `actionlint` clean; every job parses to `ubuntu-24.04`. **Verified on GitHub**: CI run #27 (commit `a08c747`, the first push after the pin) ran every job of the pinned workflow and passed -- gitleaks 10 s, backend 12 m 4 s, E2E 4 m 18 s, `tsc` 25 s, and both image builds (1 m 36 s and 2 m 19 s; they build and push to GHCR) -- 14 m 29 s in all. **Not seen**: the runner-image line in a job log (step logs need a GitHub login), so "ran on `ubuntu-24.04`" rests on the workflow naming it for every job and the jobs having run, not on reading the image name. **Not done**: trying 26 -- its AppArmor default, Playwright support and package names are unknown until a branch runs on it; that is the deliberate migration this pin makes room for.

- **Memory lessons fix (2026-10-12): the pipeline was recording stand-in results as lessons -- a bug in my own memory feature, found while listing what was still pending.** **The bug**: the LangGraph pipeline's `market_analysis`, `options_strategy`, `compliance_check`, `backtesting`, `evaluation`, `optimization`, `risk_assessment` and `deployment` nodes are deterministic stand-ins -- a hard-coded Sharpe of 1.0, a `deployed: True` that deploys nothing -- and the memory feature wrote what they said down as lessons (`strategy X evaluated pass (sharpe 1.0) ... deployed to paper`, plus a rejection lesson from the evaluation rule). Recall puts those lessons into later LLM prompts as "Lessons from earlier similar runs (use if relevant)", so every run would have taught the next ones a fabricated outcome with the authority of memory -- worse than recording nothing. The nodes were documented as stand-ins; I wired learning to them anyway. **Checked before fixing**: the live stack had no pipeline runs and its Qdrant collection held 0 points, so no false lesson was ever stored there (the throwaway Qdrants of the E2E runs did receive some). **The rule that replaced it** (`backend/src/memory/lessons.py`, whose docstring tells the story): *a lesson is recorded only if the system can vouch for the fact behind it.* **What changed**: (1) every stand-in node marks its output `simulated: true` (first key, so the feed's short summary shows it); the evaluation rule is simulated iff its inputs are (`is_simulated(None)` is true); the `pipeline.completed` event reports `deployed: false` and `simulated: true` for the stand-in deployment; the Mission Control feed renders `(simulated)` after such a step; the run API returns the marker too. (2) The LangGraph pipeline now records exactly one kind of lesson -- generated code that failed validation after the maximum attempts (the lint is real and independent of any data). `_rejection_lesson`, `_outcome_lesson`, `_strategy_label`, the `memory_ingest` wrapper and `build_graph(memory=)` are gone, so a normal run records nothing. (3) The *real* strategy pipeline (`orchestration/strategies.py`) was the one place with something truthful to teach and no memory at all: `generate_strategy_code(memory=)` appends recalled lessons to the code-generation prompt, and `remember_code_failure` records a version whose code failed static validation or its sandbox smoke run (judged by `sandbox_result['success']`, not `sandbox_passed`, which the naked-options scan also clears). Wired into `POST /strategies` and `POST /strategies/{id}/suggestions/{id}/regenerate`; deliberately not into the screener (system-initiated; no request to carry a memory service). Not lessons, on purpose: the naked-options scan blocking a strategy (the legs are fixed by `_default_option_legs`, not written by the model, and grounding them depends on chain data being present), and an infrastructure fault in the sandbox (those raise; they say nothing about the code). (4) **Nothing derived from market data is recorded** -- no Sharpe, no P&L, no review finding -- because nothing records whether a backtest or a paper fill ran on real or synthetic data (`BacktestRun`, `PaperFill`, `Trade` and `TradeReviewFinding` carry no provenance field; paper trading falls back to `MockTickSource`'s random walk when no broker is configured, and the only place that says which it was is the live unrealized-P&L endpoint's `price_source`, computed at request time and stored with nothing); a lesson built on synthetic data would teach the same falsehood by a quieter route. When results carry provenance, that is the point to add performance lessons. (5) Memory ids are now `uuid5(kind + normalised text)`, so remembering the same lesson again updates one point instead of adding a copy -- the duplicate weakness flagged in the memory-online E2E entry above; its other flagged weakness (success lessons outranking a human note) is gone because success is no longer recorded. (6) `AgentMemory` moved to `src.memory.lessons` beside `recall_lessons` / `record_lesson`, which never raise and now give memory at most 10 s (`MEMORY_CALL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`) -- the embedding client waits up to 60 s on a host that never answers, and `POST /strategies` previously never touched memory, so a hung provider must not be able to stall it. **Verified**: 40 new tests in `backend/tests/test_memory_lessons.py` (the helpers, including a hung memory costing a bounded delay rather than a stall; every stand-in marked and the evaluation simulated exactly when its inputs are; the feed and completed event; what the LangGraph pipeline does and does not record, including that a result *not* marked simulated still records nothing and that a cancelled run records nothing even when a completed one would have; `remember_code_failure` table-driven over seven situations; the strategy pipeline against a real sandbox error, a fake sandbox, a naked-options block and an infrastructure fault; the prompt byte-for-byte unchanged without lessons; regeneration; and the real stack end to end -- `POST /strategies` -> `PipelineMemory` -> `MemoryService` -> an in-process Qdrant: a failed strategy leaves exactly one lesson, a similar request's code-generation prompt contains it, failing the same way again is still one point, and a normal `POST /agents/pipeline/run` leaves the store exactly as it found it) plus two in `test_memory.py` (the id is derived from what the memory says, pinned; saying it twice is one memory with the newer metadata) and the three tests of the removed behaviour (rejection / outcome / "stopped before evaluation" lessons) replaced by one; **22 mutation checks (each breaks one rule in the source -- a marker removed, a lesson recorded for a passing run / for performance / for a cancelled run, a naked-options block read as a code failure, memory not passed by either route, random ids, recall or record unbounded or able to raise, the lessons block never produced -- and the dedicated test for each was confirmed to catch it), all caught**; full backend suite **1260 of 1261 passed -- the one failure, `test_engine_sandbox.py::test_sandbox_blocks_getattr_string_obfuscation_of_a_banned_attribute`, died with `OSError: [Errno 12] Cannot allocate memory` while the sandbox worker's interpreter was starting, because I had a second heavy job (an E2E container installing packages) running beside the suite and the Docker VM ran short of memory; that file passes 14/14 re-run alone, and after comment-only edits the tests of every touched module were re-run (195 passed)** in a freshly built clean venv; `ruff` clean (`ruff check` and `ruff format --check`, 395 files); `mypy --strict` 0 errors (258 files); `tsc` clean. **Checked against a real Qdrant server and real `nomic-embed-text` embeddings** (a throwaway container and database; only the LLM and the sandbox were canned): a strategy whose code had a banned import left exactly one lesson (`Objective 'Momentum breakout strategy for NIFTY futures': the generated code failed static validation: banned import: os.`); a similar request's code-generation prompt contained it; an *unrelated* objective scored 0.408 against 0.592 for the related one **and still received the lesson** (recall has no similarity floor -- see below); failing the same way again stayed at one point; a normal LangGraph run recalled the lesson, returned `deployment_result: {simulated: true, ...}` and left the point count unchanged. **E2E**: the whole Playwright suite in the CI-faithful container with memory genuinely online (a real Qdrant v1.19.1 and Ollama `nomic-embed-text`): **26 passed** (24 repo tests plus 2 throwaway screenshot tests), `tsc` clean -- including the new test (a real pipeline run left the `strategy` collection unchanged) and the feed spec's `(simulated)` assertion; a screenshot of the feed shows `(simulated)` on the placeholder steps and nothing on the LLM-backed ones; the plain CI-like run (memory unavailable, as on GitHub): **21 passed, 3 skipped** (the three memory-online tests), `tsc` clean. `e2e/agent-pipeline-feed.spec.ts` now asserts the backtest step shows `(simulated)`; `e2e/agent-memory.spec.ts` gains a memory-online test that runs a real pipeline and asserts the `strategy` collection's count is unchanged. **Not done / still true**: recall returns the top few memories by similarity *whatever their score* (the prompt hedges "use if relevant"); a similarity floor depends on the embedding model, so it would need a setting. The organization-run engine's `risk_assessment` capability (`orchestration/capabilities.py`) is also a hard-coded `{"within_limits": True}`, unmarked -- memory is not wired into org runs, so nothing learns from it, but it is the same kind of stand-in. The no-LLM fallback strategy code returns a hard-coded Sharpe of 1.0; it is never read as a lesson (a sandbox smoke run's `result` is ignored) but is visible in `sandbox_result`. **The live stack was rebuilt from these commits the same day** (backend and frontend images; every container healthy) and checked read-only inside the backend container against the real Qdrant and the embedding choice saved in the database: a normal pipeline run, with no LLM call, returned `deployment_result: {simulated: true, ...}` and left the `strategy` collection at 0 points before and after; the running backend contains `lessons.py` and the new markers, and the built frontend bundle contains `(simulated)`. **Not checked on the live stack**: the Mission Control feed in a browser (the console needs a login I do not have) and the `POST /strategies` path (it would create a strategy in the database); both are covered by the E2E run and the backend tests above. **Non-Negotiable Rules audit**: no rule violated. No new endpoint and no migration (rules 7 and 8): the two endpoints that now carry a memory service were already audited by the middleware, and the lesson write is a side effect inside that audited request. Rule 3's spirit holds -- only request-driven flows write to memory; the screener, a background job, does not. Rule 6: the sandbox is untouched (its result is only read).

- **Signing-key hardening (2026-10-12): a deployment can no longer start on the public placeholder key, and an administrator can see and replace a weak one.** **The problem** (found re-auditing `pip-audit`): `.env.example`, `docker-compose.yml` and `Settings.jwt_secret_key` all ship `change-me-in-production`, and the first request in a deployment's life seeded the `jwt_signing_keys` row from it -- after which the database row wins over `.env`, so editing `.env` later changed nothing and anyone who had read the repository could forge a login token for any user id. The live stack was in exactly that state (`created_by = env-seed`, 23 characters, seeded 2026-09-24). **What changed**: (1) `signing_key_weakness()` in `src/core/security.py` (the shipped placeholder, or under 32 bytes -- PyJWT's own threshold); bootstrap now generates `secrets.token_urlsafe(48)` (`created_by = generated-at-first-start`, logged) instead of seeding a weak environment key, and still honours a strong one so sessions survive the upgrade that moved the key into the database. (2) `GET /api/v1/system/jwt-signing-key` (SystemAdministrator only) returns `created_at`, `created_by`, `weak` and the reason -- never the key. (3) Startup logs `security.jwt_signing_key_weak` at warning level, naming the fix; it cannot rotate silently, because rotating signs everyone out, and it never stops the app starting. (4) Settings > Security (`components/settings/signing-key-card.tsx`): the status, and **Rotate signing key** behind an explicit confirm that says everyone is signed out, you included; after rotating it offers *Sign in again*. Non-administrators see the section read-only. (5) `.env.example` leaves the key unset, `docker-compose.yml` defaults it to empty, `deploy/README.md` says so; a stray `rejections, outcomes` comment in the memory panel (stale since the lessons fix) corrected. **Verified**: 11 new tests (`backend/tests/test_jwt_signing_key_status.py`: what counts as weak, bootstrap never seeding the placeholder, a strong environment key kept, the endpoint flagging a weak key and never returning it, rotation making it healthy, admin-only, the startup warning present at warning level and silent otherwise); 6 mutation checks (weakness ignored at bootstrap, placeholder no longer weak, a 31-byte key passing, endpoint open to another role, startup downgraded to info, status always healthy), all caught -- the downgrade one first slipped through and the test was tightened; full backend suite 1272 passed (0 failed) in a clean venv; `ruff`, `ruff format`, `mypy --strict` and `tsc` clean; Playwright in the CI-like container 24 passed / 3 skipped, including three new specs (`e2e/signing-key.spec.ts`: healthy key and the key value never on the page; a non-administrator sees no controls; cancel changes nothing, rotating signs out, a new login works and the key is now the administrator's -- sitting mid-suite with every later spec unaffected). **Not done**: the live key. It is still the placeholder (checked 2026-10-12) -- rotating needs an administrator's own login, so it happens in the UI once the stack is rebuilt. PyJWT's short-key warning is not yet seen in the live log for the same reason (it fires on first token use).

- **`mypy --strict` and `pip-audit` are now blocking in CI (2026-10-13).** Both steps in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` had been `continue-on-error`, so they reported on every build and gated nothing -- and one of them had been failing, tolerated, for a while. **What it took**: `mypy src` was already at 0 errors (258 files), so that step only lost its `continue-on-error`. `pip-audit` had two findings left: dev-only `pytest` 8.4.2 (fix 9.0.3, blocked by the `pytest<9.0` pin, which in turn held `pytest-asyncio` at 0.24) and the runner's own `pip`. The pins are now `pytest>=9.0.3,<10` and `pytest-asyncio>=1.3,<2` (resolved: pytest 9.1.1, pytest-asyncio 1.4.0, pytest-cov unchanged), and the install step runs `python -m pip install --upgrade pip` first. The stale rationale comments ("advisory", the langgraph bump) were rewritten to say why each step now blocks. **Verified**: a venv built from the new `pyproject.toml` -- `pip-audit` reports no known vulnerabilities once `pip` is current (it flagged only `pip` and `pytest` before) and exits 0, `mypy src` exits 0, and the full backend suite on pytest 9.1.1 + pytest-asyncio 1.4.0 is 1272 passed, 0 failed; `actionlint` clean; no `continue-on-error` remains in the workflow. **What blocking means** (so it is not a surprise): a CVE published against *any* dependency fails the next build -- including an unrelated change -- until the dependency is bumped or the finding is acknowledged with an explicit `pip-audit --ignore-vuln <ID>` next to a stated reason; and because the image jobs `need` the backend job, a red audit also stops images being published. That is the intent, but it is a standing cost. CI audits the runner's Python, which carries packages my clean venv does not (`setuptools`, `wheel`); a finding in one of those would show up as a red build, not here. The comparison doc's item 28 and its table row are updated; per-module coverage thresholds and mutation testing in CI remain open.

- **Per-module coverage floors and mutation testing in CI (2026-10-13).** The last two open pieces of backlog item 28. **Measured first** (full suite with coverage, 31 minutes, 1272 passed): the suite is at **87.1%**, so the old global floor of 70% was loose, and the deterministic safety layer is at or near 100% file by file. **Coverage gate**: `scripts/check_coverage.py` reads the `coverage.json` the CI pytest step now writes and enforces `[tool.tradingos.coverage]` in `backend/pyproject.toml` -- 15 critical files (every `engine/risk` module, `options_grounding`, the orchestration kill switch / risk gate / risk limits / approvals / transitions, `engine/validation`, `core/rbac`) at 95% each, and 14 top-level packages at floors set about three points under what was measured (e.g. `src/api` 77 against 80.1); a floor naming a file or package with no measured statements fails (a rename the config missed must not turn the gate into a no-op). The global `--cov-fail-under` rose from 70 to 85. Deliberately not gated: `engine/sandbox/restricted_exec.py` and `worker_main.py` read 0% only because they run in the sandbox's own subprocess, which coverage does not trace (their tests are real, adversarial escapes included), and `main.py` / `workers` / `execution` / `risk` do not measure what they do; gating a number wrong by construction would teach everyone to ignore the gate. **Mutation testing**: a `mutation` job in `ci.yml`, one matrix entry per module so they run in parallel (mutmut 2.x has no parallel mode and a module takes 4-9 minutes), each running `scripts/mutation_gate.py` -- mutmut over that module against its own tests, failing if more mutants survive than `max_survivors` in `[[tool.tradingos.mutation.module]]`, printing every survivor's diff. Scope is Phase 14's: `engine/risk/kill_switch`, `compliance`, `correlation_constraint` and `orchestration/kill_switch`; not a prerequisite of the image jobs (a slow gate should not delay publishing, though a red run still says the safety tests slipped). **What the first run found**: 138 mutants, 31 survivors. Reviewed one by one: most are the cosmetic kind Phase 14 left alone (dataclass `frozen`/`slots` flags, error and notification text, the `KillSwitchMode` alias, the fake benchmark's default seed) -- but five were real gaps in safety code and are now killed by tests: the default NIFTY/BANKNIFTY circuit bands in `compliance.py` could change (10.0 to 11.0, a key renamed) with everything green, and the orchestration kill switch could set `updated_at` to `None` on a trip or a reset, or leave `""` instead of `None` as the reason after a reset. Baselines now: kill_switch 4 of 23, compliance 10 of 49, correlation_constraint 6 of 29, orchestration kill_switch 6 of 37.
