A mutmut detail that matters for reading its output: **"suspicious" means the mutant *was killed* but slowly**, not a near-miss -- only "survived" is a hole, which is what the gate counts. **Verified**: 29 floors pass on the measured data and two deliberate degradations (a safety file at 53%, a file missing) fail it; 21 new tests (`test_check_coverage.py`, `test_mutation_gate.py` -- including one that keeps the CI matrix and the pyproject module list identical -- and the two "found by mutation testing" tests, which between them kill five mutants); the real gate script run end to end on compliance (10 survivors, baseline 10) and the orchestration kill switch (6, baseline 6) after the new tests, and on the engine kill switch with its baseline lowered by one -- exit 1, "NEW survivors", the diff printed; `actionlint`, `ruff`, `mypy --strict` clean. **Not done**: mutation testing beyond these four modules **[Update 2026-10-14: `risk_gate`, `risk_limits` and `go_live_gate` are now covered -- see the "Mutation testing extended" entry at the end of this list]** (`circuit_breaker`, `options_grounding` are at or near 100% line coverage and would be the next candidates; `live_trading.py::expire_stale_intents` remains hand-analysed), and subprocess coverage for the sandbox workers.

- **GitHub Actions moved off Node.js 20 (2026-10-13).** Every run's annotations carried "Node.js 20 is deprecated ... forced to run on Node.js 24" for `actions/checkout`, `actions/setup-python` and `gitleaks-action`: GitHub switched the runner default to Node 24 (2026-06-02), so those actions only kept working because the runner overrode their declared runtime -- one runner change from failing. All ten actions in `ci.yml` now pin a release that declares `node24` (read from each action's own `action.yml`, not assumed): `checkout` v4 to v5, `setup-python` v5 to v6, `setup-node` v4 to v5, `upload-artifact` v4 to v6, `pnpm/action-setup` v4 to v5, `gitleaks-action` v2 to v3, and the four Docker actions to `setup-buildx` v4, `metadata` v6, `login` v4, `build-push` v7. The lowest Node-24 major of each, deliberately: the smallest behavioural change that removes the problem; newer majors exist for most and are a separate, optional bump. **Checked**: for every step, each `with:` input still exists in the new version (scripted against the new `action.yml` files); `gitleaks-action` v3's README states no change to inputs, outputs or behaviour and its default scanner is still 8.24.3 (ours is pinned explicitly anyway); `actionlint` clean. To keep them current, `.github/dependabot.yml` (added the same day) opens one weekly pull request for GitHub Actions updates (all of a week's bumps grouped, commit prefix `ci`, at most three open), validated against Dependabot's published v2 JSON schema. It is Actions-only on purpose: pip, pnpm and the Docker base images are version decisions that have each needed their own reviewed change, and automatic bump PRs for them would be noise. CI runs on `pull_request`, so each PR is tested -- and the first one will be the first time the PR path of the workflow (including the `images` job's build-and-discard branch and the secret scan on a PR token) has ever run. The first PR will also move the actions from the lowest Node-24 major each is pinned to the newest; whether `gitleaks-action` works with a PR's token is unconfirmed until then.

- **Dead pipeline runs now end instead of reading "running" for ever (2026-10-13).** **The problem** (a limit written into the pipeline-cancel entry above): a LangGraph pipeline runs inside the HTTP request that started it, so when that process dies -- a crash, a redeploy, an OOM kill -- nothing is left to write an ending. The event log just stops, the Mission Control feed showed a spinner that never resolved, and a cancel on the run was *accepted* (202, an audit row) and never consumed. **What changed** (`src/orchestration/pipeline_events.py`): (1) **a heartbeat** -- while a run executes, its process keeps the Redis key `agents:pipeline:alive:{run_id}` fresh (set before the run's first event, refreshed every 10 s, 30 s TTL, deleted on the way out; if the process dies it simply expires); `PipelineEventRecorder.heartbeat()` wraps the run in `POST /agents/pipeline/run`. (2) **A derived state** -- `pipeline_run_state` can now answer `abandoned`: no terminal event, no live heartbeat, and no event for 60 s. With Redis unreadable or absent there is no liveness signal, so only a much longer silence counts (10 minutes -- an LLM call can legitimately take minutes). (3) **A reaper** -- `reap_abandoned_runs` gives each such run the ending it could not write, a persisted-and-published `pipeline.abandoned` event (continuing the run's sequence, naming the node it stopped at, the steps completed and the last event), run once at startup and every 60 s by `start_abandoned_run_reaper_loop` (cancelled on shutdown beside the stall sweep). It never touches a run that has a terminal event or a live heartbeat -- so a run alive on *another instance* is safe -- is idempotent, and does not report a run it failed to write. `pipeline.abandoned` is a terminal event type, so (4) **cancel** on such a run is now a 409 ("no longer running -- the process executing it ended") with no flag set and no audit row, and (5) **the panel** (`agent-pipeline-panel.tsx`) shows `INTERRUPTED`, offers no Cancel, and turns the step that was in flight from a spinner into a failed row reading "interrupted: the process ended before this step finished". No migration: `event_type` has no database constraint. **Semantics worth knowing**: a run is judged dead from missing evidence of life, so a process alive but unable to refresh the heartbeat for 60 s (a blocked event loop) could be marked abandoned while still running; if it then finishes, its later `pipeline.completed` follows the abandoned event and the feed shows the later status. Nothing is *resumed*: an abandoned run's work is lost and a new run is needed. A process that is alive but hung inside an LLM call keeps heartbeating and still reads running -- that is what the existing cancel (step-granular) is for. **Verified**: 17 new backend tests (`tests/test_agents_pipeline_abandoned.py`: the state with and without Redis and with Redis unreadable, the heartbeat set before / refreshed during / cleared after -- including when the run raises -- the reaper's event, sequence, payload and live publish, idempotence, leaving alive / young / finished runs alone, not claiming a run it could not end, cancel refused for an abandoned run and still accepted for a quiet-but-alive one, and the real endpoint holding the heartbeat while a node executes); 11 mutation checks (liveness ignored, abandoned not terminal, the reaper restarting the sequence or skipping the heartbeat check, the heartbeat left behind or never refreshed, cancel not given Redis or accepting an abandoned run, the endpoint skipping the heartbeat, the reaper claiming unwritten runs), all caught; full backend suite 1310 passed, 0 failed (87.17% coverage, all 29 per-module floors met); `ruff`, `mypy --strict`, `tsc` and `actionlint` clean; and Playwright in the CI-like container, 26 passed / 3 skipped (the memory-online ones), including `e2e/pipeline-abandoned.spec.ts`: a dead run seeded *before the backend starts* (a new step in the e2e job, `scripts/seed_e2e_pipeline_run.py`) was ended by the real startup sweep -- checked through the API, cancel refused with a 409 -- and a run already ended that way renders as INTERRUPTED with its stuck step marked and no Cancel. The first Playwright run passed the sweep test and failed the panel test on a strict-mode locator in my own spec (`getByText('INTERRUPTED')` also matched the objective text); the product behaved correctly and the locator was made exact. **Live stack**: not rebuilt, so it does not have this yet; it has no pipeline runs at all (checked read-only), so the first sweep after a rebuild would find nothing to end. **Not done**: mid-step cancel (an LLM call still has to finish) and resuming an abandoned run.

- **Mid-step cancel: a cancel now takes effect during a step, not only between steps (2026-10-13).** **The problem** (the known limit in the pipeline-cancel entry above): cancel was checked only at step boundaries, so cancelling during a long LLM call did nothing until that call returned -- seconds to minutes in which the operator saw "Cancelling…" and the run kept spending tokens. **What changed** (`src/agents/graph.py`): each real node now runs through `_run_step`, which runs it as its own task and polls the `CancelSignal` every `CANCEL_POLL_SECONDS` (0.25 s; a Redis GET) until it finishes. On a cancel it cancels the task -- the LLM router has no `CancelledError` handling, so the in-flight call is dropped -- waits for it to unwind, and raises; `timed_node` then emits **`step.cancelled`** (`step_index`, `duration_ms`) and raises `PipelineCancelledError(..., in_flight=True)`, and `run_pipeline` emits `pipeline.cancelled` with `{step_index, steps_completed, in_flight}`. **The aborted step is discarded**: its result is never applied, `steps_completed` and the `node_log` in the HTTP response exclude it, and nothing is recorded to memory (as before for any cancelled run). Boundary cancels are unchanged (`in_flight: false`; the check before a step and in the disabled-agent skip node stays). **Edge cases pinned**: (1) a step that finishes despite the abort (it swallows `CancelledError` and returns) is **kept** -- `task.cancelled()` distinguishes aborted from finished, so finished work is never thrown away; (2) a step that raises is a failure (`step.failed`), never masked as a cancel; (3) if the run's own task is cancelled from outside (shutdown, dropped client) the `finally` cancels the step, so no orphaned LLM call is left running; (4) with no `cancel_signal` the node is simply awaited (no polling); (5) an unreadable signal still counts as not cancelled (fail open). A step that is fast (finishes inside one poll interval) costs no extra Redis reads. **Assumption to keep true**: abandoning a step is only safe because today's pipeline nodes read state and call the LLM router -- they hold no side effect to leave half-done (the "deployment" node is a stand-in that deploys nothing). A node that ever gains a real side effect (placing an order, writing a file) needs its own answer to "what does cancel mean here" before it goes through `_run_step`. **Frontend**: the panel folds `step.cancelled` into the step's row as a new `cancelled` status (Ban icon, "cancelled: stopped before it finished", `data-step-status="cancelled"`), the pending text is now "Stopping the step in progress", and `lib/api.ts`/`schemas.py` wording no longer says the step finishes first. No migration (`event_type` has no constraint). **Verified**: backend tests in `tests/test_agents_pipeline_cancel.py` -- the old "in-progress step finishes" test rewritten to the kept-because-it-finished-first case, plus new ones: a hung node is interrupted and the event order is exactly `step.started → step.cancelled → pipeline.cancelled`, an aborted step is not recorded to memory, a step that ignores the abort keeps its result, a raising step is a failure, an outside cancellation does not leak the step, and the HTTP test now cancels a run **held on a gate that is never released** and gets `cancelled: true` back with `node_log == [ceo_kickoff]`; 8 mutation checks (polling disabled, finished-vs-aborted check removed, not waiting for the task to unwind, the leak-guard removed, `step.cancelled` not emitted, `in_flight` wrong or dropped, signal not passed to the step), all caught; `ruff`, `mypy --strict`, `tsc` clean; full backend suite 1315 passed, coverage 87.21%, all 29 per-module floors met; Playwright 27 passed / 3 skipped (the memory-online ones) -- including a new spec that plants a mid-step-cancelled run (`seed_e2e_pipeline_run.py --cancelled`) and checks the panel renders the aborted step as cancelled, the earlier step as done, nothing spinning and no Cancel button. **Not covered end to end**: a *real* mid-step abort through a real LLM call -- CI has no LLM and finishes a run in milliseconds, so the backend tests hold a node open instead; the E2E spec plants the events. **Live stack**: not rebuilt, so it does not have this (nor the dead-run fix) yet.

- **Pause / continue / retry that change nothing are refused, not answered 200 (2026-10-13).** **The problem**: the audit middleware writes a row for every 2xx and records only actor/path/ids, so `POST /orchestration/runs/{id}/pause|continue|retry` on a run they did not apply to (pause on a finished run, continue on a running one, retry on a run that had not failed -- a double click, or a run that finished a moment before) returned 200 with the run unchanged **and left a "paused"/"continued"/"retried" row in the permanent audit trail for something that never happened**. Cancel and the pipeline cancel had already been built to avoid this; these three had not been (the pause no-op was a tested, deliberate 200 from the earlier run-control audit). **What changed** (`src/api/routes/orchestration.py`, via one `_no_op_refusal` helper): when the run-control call reports it did not apply, the endpoint answers **404** if the run does not exist, otherwise **409** with the run's real state -- `This run is completed; only a running run can be paused` / `...a paused run can be continued` / `...a failed run can be retried`. The existing 409 for a *permanent* failure on retry is unchanged, and rerun is unaffected (it always creates a run). `run_control` already returned whether the transition applied (it is the race-safe conditional transition), so there is no engine change, and the audit middleware needed none: it already records only 2xx. **Frontend**: no change -- `handleRunAction` already shows the API's message and refreshes the run list after a failed action (written for cancel's 409), and the buttons are only offered in the states where the action applies, so this surfaces only on a double click or a run that changed under the operator's feet. **Verified**: `tests/test_orchestration_run_control.py` -- the old "pause on a finished run is a 200 no-op" test replaced by a parametrized one (pause on completed/paused, continue on running/completed, retry on completed/running: each a 409 with the exact message and the run untouched), a test that drives each action once where it applies and once where it no longer does and checks the audit trail contains **exactly the three that took effect**, and the 404 test now checks the message; 7 mutation checks (each endpoint answering 200 again, 404 turned into 409, the message not naming the real status, an applied pause refused), all caught; `ruff`, `mypy --strict` clean; the orchestration, audit and RBAC test files 368 passed. The full suite and Playwright were not re-run for this change (it touches three route handlers and one test file; nothing else calls these endpoints and no Playwright spec uses them). **Not done** **[Update 2026-10-14: the rest of the API has since been swept -- see the "No-op audit sweep" entry at the end of this list]**: the same 200-no-op shape may exist on other state-changing endpoints elsewhere in the API; I audited only the run controls.

- **Mutation testing extended to the order gate, the risk-limit approval workflow and the go-live gate (2026-10-14).** Three more modules joined the `mutation` job (`[[tool.tradingos.mutation.module]]` in `pyproject.toml` + the matrix in `ci.yml`, kept in step by `tests/test_mutation_gate.py`): `src/orchestration/risk_gate.py` (the per-order gate: kill switch, compliance, naked options, correlation), `src/orchestration/risk_limits.py` (the stage -> confirm-by-someone-else -> apply workflow that is the only way a risk limit changes) and `src/engine/risk/go_live_gate.py` (the four go-live readiness conditions). Each runs against its own test file only. **What it found** (first pass, before any new test): `risk_gate` 8 of 16 mutants survived, `risk_limits` **19 of 36**, `go_live_gate` 17 of 56. Line coverage on these modules was already at or near 100% -- the point of mutation testing: the lines *ran*, nothing checked what they did. The real ones: in `risk_limits`, applying onto a limit that already exists (the existing flow only ever applied a first change to an empty table, so the update path -- value, `updated_by` -- and the duplicate-row case were untested), picking the limit by name (`==` to `!=` survived, i.e. applying one limit could have touched another), and every audit field of the approval trail (`confirmed_at`, `applied_by`, `applied_at` could be silently `None`); in `risk_gate`, the `and` in "has both a returns series and a benchmark" turned `or` (half the inputs would crash or evaluate nothing) and the correlation recorded on an intent starting as `""` instead of `None`; in `go_live_gate`, the default divergence limit moved 20 to 21 points, the divergence scaled by 101 instead of 100, and `<=` turned `<` at exactly the limit. Every rejection reason shown to an operator could also be reworded with no test noticing. **What was added**: tests that apply a second change onto an existing limit and check one row, the new value and `updated_by`; apply one limit and check another is untouched; check who/when on all three steps of the approval trail; assert each error's attributes and exact text; the half-the-inputs and recorded-correlation cases; the exact 20-point default (20.5 blocked, 19.95 allowed) and the at-the-limit boundary; each blocking reason's exact wording; and that an approved `OrderIntent` and the go-live input/verdict are frozen (what the gate checked is what gets submitted). **Baselines now** (`max_survivors`, a ratchet like the others): `risk_gate` 2 of 16, `risk_limits` **0 of 36**, `go_live_gate` 2 of 56, each confirmed by a full `mutation_gate.py` run (`risk_gate` and `go_live_gate` after their last tests were added; `risk_limits`' tests did not change after its 0-survivor run). What is left is deliberately cosmetic: the `slots` flag on a dataclass (`risk_gate` 1, `go_live_gate` 2) and one type annotation (`|` to `&`, no runtime effect). **Cost**: these run longer than the first four (measured locally in a container: about 35 minutes for `risk_limits` and about an hour for `go_live_gate`, whose 56 mutants are the most of any module; `risk_gate` finished sooner); they run in parallel as separate matrix entries and, as before, are not a prerequisite of the image jobs. The legacy `[tool.mutmut]` block lists all seven modules. A side effect worth knowing: mutmut re-writes the files it mutates with CRLF line endings on this Windows checkout, so after a local run `git status` shows the mutated sources as modified (content identical; `git checkout -- <file>` restores them). **Verified**: the three test files pass, `ruff` clean, `actionlint` clean, `tests/test_mutation_gate.py` passes (config and matrix in step). **Not verified**: the new matrix entries have not run on GitHub's runners yet -- the first CI run is their first run there, and a slower runner could hit a mutant timeout and count it as a survivor; if a baseline is exceeded by one for that reason, that is the first thing to look at. **Not done**: `circuit_breaker` and `options_grounding`; the full backend suite was not re-run (only tests were added, and the three files plus the gate test were).

- **A broker that rejected the credentials is no longer polled every 3 seconds, and a repeating job failure no longer floods the log (2026-10-14).** **The problem** (seen on the live stack): the paper-trading tick-publish job (`run_tick_publish_job`, every 3 s, market hours only) polls the configured broker for a quote. The stored Zerodha access token had expired (they last one trading day) -- or the broker connect never completed -- so Kite answered every call with `403 TokenException: Incorrect api_key or access_token`. A 4xx deliberately never trips the circuit breaker (it is a caller mistake, not an outage), so nothing slowed the polling down: **1,892 identical failures and tracebacks in the ~1.5 hours since the backend started**, burying every other log line, plus that many calls to a broker that had already refused the token. Each failure also wrote a `ScheduledJobRun` row (the history keeps only the latest 50 per job, so the table was not growing). **What changed** -- two independent layers. (1) **Stop asking** (`src/brokers/base.py`, `tick_source.py`, `engine/paper_trading/tick_feed.py`): a 401/403 from either broker is now a `BrokerAuthError` (a subclass of `BrokerRequestError`, so every existing handler still matches; raised through one `broker_request_error()` helper used by the Zerodha and Upstox adapters, including Zerodha's instruments download). `BrokerQuoteTickSource` catches it, logs **once** `tick_source.broker_credentials_rejected` (broker, HTTP status, "reconnect the broker in Settings > Broker"), and from then on raises `TickSourceUnavailableError` *without calling the broker* until credentials change. A reconnect is picked up at once: `get_tick_source` already builds a fresh source when the stored credentials change, and a fresh source is not suspended. For a fix made on the broker's side with the stored credentials unchanged, it probes again after 10 minutes (`AUTH_REJECTED_RETRY_SECONDS`) -- 6 calls an hour instead of 1,200 -- logs `tick_source.broker_credentials_accepted_again` once if the probe succeeds, and waits another full interval if it is refused again. Any other failure (a 404/429, a 5xx, a timeout) does **not** suspend anything. (2) **Say it once** (`src/observability/log_throttle.py`, installed by `configure_logging`): a `logging.Filter` on APScheduler's executor logger lets the first failure of each kind (job, exception type, message -- with the changing "next run at" time stripped) through in full, drops identical repeats for 60 s, and appends "[N identical failures since the last report not logged]" to the next one that gets through. This protects every scheduled job, not only this one; records without an exception are untouched and the table of remembered kinds is capped at 256. **Deliberately not done: swallowing the error in the job.** The job still raises on every firing, so `scheduled_job_history` (the Phase 22 durable run history) keeps recording each firing as FAILED with the real message ("zerodha rejected the stored credentials (HTTP 403); not polling quotes until the credentials change (next probe in Ns)"); a job that quietly returned would have made that history report success for a feed that publishes nothing. **Verified**: 21 new tests (`tests/test_brokers_tick_source.py`: both adapters raise `BrokerAuthError` for 401 and 403 and a plain `BrokerRequestError` for 400/404/429; after a refusal 50 further firings send **no** request and the warning is logged exactly once; the probe after the interval, its recovery logged once, a refused probe waiting a full interval again; ordinary failures never suspend; reconnecting replaces a suspended source. `tests/test_log_throttle.py`: first failure passes and repeats are dropped, the window and the dropped count, different errors/jobs/exception types are separate kinds, plain records untouched, bounded memory, idempotent install, startup installs it, and a **real APScheduler job failing every 50 ms logs exactly one traceback**); 15 mutation checks (suspension never set / never expires / not cleared by a good probe, every 4xx or no 401 / no 403 / a 404 treated as auth, the rejection not logged, the throttle dropping nothing / letting nothing through / not ignoring the next-run time / ignoring the exception type / not reporting the count / throttling plain records / not installed at startup), all caught after two tests were tightened -- the first pass missed two of them and caught one for the wrong reason; `ruff`, `mypy --strict` clean; related suites 164 passed; full backend suite 1357 passed, coverage 87.30%, all 29 per-module floors met. **Live stack**: not rebuilt, so it still floods until it is. The flood also depends on a stored Zerodha token that is no longer valid -- fixing *that* is the broker connect, a separate problem (see the redirect-URI notes: this fix makes the failure quiet, it does not make Zerodha connect). **Not done / worth knowing** **[Update 2026-10-14: item (a) is fixed -- see the "Per-symbol isolation" entry at the end of this list; the back-off, the Settings signal and the symbol format below are now done too -- see "Open engineering items closed"]**: (a) `publish_ticks_once` publishes symbols in a loop with no per-symbol isolation, so one symbol the broker rejects (an unknown symbol, say -- the live database has an active `DEMOSTOCK` subscription) stops every symbol after it from getting a tick; a persistent per-symbol 4xx other than 401/403 is therefore still a job that fails every firing (now logged once a minute, not every 3 s, but still starving later symbols); (b) the Settings page does not surface "the broker rejected the token" -- the signal is a single warning in the log and the failing job in the scheduled-jobs history.

- **One symbol the broker cannot quote no longer stops every other symbol's tick -- and a missing quote is no longer a price of 0.0 (2026-10-14).** **The problem, part 1**: `publish_ticks_once` (the body of the 3-second paper-trading tick-publish job) looped over the active symbols and let the first exception escape, so one symbol that failed stopped every symbol after it from getting a tick -- on every firing, for as long as that subscription existed. The live log showed the job polling `DEMOSTOCK`, a symbol Zerodha does not list. **The problem, part 2 (found while fixing part 1, and worse)**: for a symbol the broker had no data for, both adapters' `get_quote` answered **a quote priced 0.0** (`float(row.get("last_price", 0.0))` on an empty row) instead of failing. Per Kite's own docs `/quote` keys its response `exchange:tradingsymbol` and a missing key means no data; the adapters request and look up the bare symbol, so this is not a corner case for a symbol that does not match. Every consumer took that 0.0 at face value: the paper tick feed published it as a real tick, and `process_tick` feeds `tick_price` straight into `check_universal_stop_loss` (`tick_price <= avg_cost * (1 - stop%)`), so **a 0.0 tick stops out any open long paper position**; `live_trading._submit_intent_to_broker` built the live order intent with `proposed_price = reference_price = 0.0` and a position value of 0, which the risk gate's position limits pass trivially. **What changed.** (1) `publish_ticks_once` now tries every symbol independently, and after the round raises one `TickPublishError` (`failures`: symbol to exception, `published`: the ticks that did go out; `__cause__` is the first failure; the message names up to 5 symbols with a 120-character reason each and "; and N more") -- so the job still *fails* (the Phase 22 run history shows which symbols and why, and the log throttle from the previous entry keeps it to one line a minute) but nothing is starved. `TickSourceUnavailableError` (the whole source is down) is still re-raised at once, unwrapped, ending the round; cancellation (`BaseException`) is never swallowed; a Redis failure for one symbol is isolated like any other. (2) New `BrokerQuoteUnavailableError` (a `BrokerRequestError`, status 404, so it never trips the circuit breaker and is not an auth error) raised by both adapters when the response has no entry for the symbol or its `last_price` is missing or <= 0. Because it is raised at the adapter, all three `get_quote` callers fail closed: the tick source raises it per symbol (not published, not suspended -- the other symbols keep polling), `live_trading` already turns any quote failure into a `failed` intent with a `live_order_intent.submission_failed` audit row (now reading "quote lookup failed: ... no usable quote for DEMOSTOCK"), and the option-chain spot lookup is best-effort and logs. **Verified**: new tests -- `test_engine_tick_feed.py` (a failing symbol does not stop its neighbours and the good ones are in Redis; the message and its 5-symbol cap and reason cut; all-success still returns the ticks; a source that is down stops the round unwrapped with earlier ticks kept; a Redis failure for one symbol is isolated; cancellation propagates), `test_brokers_zerodha.py`/`test_brokers_upstox.py` (six no-usable-quote bodies each: no entry, another symbol's entry, zero, null, empty entry, empty body), `test_brokers_tick_source.py` (a symbol with no quote neither yields a tick nor suspends polling), `test_orchestration_paper_trading_scheduler.py` (the real job, two subscriptions, one unquotable: raises naming it, the other symbol still gets its tick) and `test_orchestration_live_trading.py` (a missing quote fails the intent, nothing but the quote lookup reaches the broker, audit row says why); 14 mutation checks on the code plus the live-trading and scheduler tests each checked against their own mutation (the abort-on-first-failure, the unreported failures, the unwrapped source-down, the swallowed cancel, the unchained cause, the summary pieces, a zero or missing quote accepted in either adapter, the wrong error type or status), all caught; `ruff`, `mypy --strict` clean; full backend suite 1379 passed, coverage 87.35%, all 29 per-module floors met. **Not verified / worth knowing**: I could not check what the real Kite or Upstox API returns for the symbols *this* deployment stores (it only reaches a broker with a valid token, and the live token is expired). If those symbols are bare (`INFY`) where Kite expects `NSE:INFY`, then quotes were already always 0.0 and now fail with "no usable quote" instead -- which is the honest outcome, but it means live and paper quoting through Zerodha may show this error for every symbol until the symbol format is sorted out; that is a separate, still-open question about the adapters' request format. A persistent per-symbol failure still costs one broker call per firing for that symbol (it did before too, at the first symbol); there is no per-symbol back-off. The live stack is not rebuilt.

- **No-op audit sweep: 11 more endpoints no longer leave a row in the audit trail for something that did not happen (2026-10-14).** **Scope**: the audit middleware writes a row for every POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE that answers 2xx. Following the run-controls fix, I listed all 92 mutating routes (`@router.post/put/patch/delete` across `src/api/routes`) and read the handler -- and the domain function it calls -- for every one that is a state *transition* (a verb: reset, deactivate, activate, accept, delete, set...). The creates, compute-only endpoints, ingestion triggers and PUT-style saves were classified by what they do, not read line by line. A route was a defect only if it could answer 2xx while changing nothing, or while changing something it should not have. **Two remedies, chosen by what each endpoint already promises.** (1) *No documented idempotent behaviour -> refuse* (409, or 404 for deleting what is not there; nothing changes, and a refusal is never audited): **kill-switch reset** on a switch that is not tripped (this one also *destroyed evidence*: it overwrote `reset_by`/`reset_at`, the record of who last genuinely reset the switch and when, with a reset that cleared nothing -- the domain function `reset_kill_switch` now raises `KillSwitchNotTrippedError` before touching anything); **admin deactivate** of an inactive user, **reactivate** of an active one, and a **role change** to the role the user already has; **prompt-version activate** of the version that is already active (it re-stamped `activated_at`, *wiped* `diff_from_previous` -- the diff against the version it replaced -- and wrote a second `prompt_version.activated` row; new `PromptVersionAlreadyActiveError`); and **DELETE of broker credentials / LLM provider credentials / a notification channel that is not stored** (the stores already returned whether anything was deleted and the handlers ignored it; the LLM and notification handlers also wrote their *own* "credentials deleted" audit row unconditionally, so the trail said credentials had been deleted that never existed) -- now a 404, as `memory forget` and `chat delete` already were. (2) *Idempotent by design -> keep the 200, suppress the row*: three endpoints whose existing tests or docstrings document that a repeat is a 200 with the current state -- **autonomy set to the value it already has** (the domain function already wrote no audit row for a repeat, but the generic one still fired), **operator-guidance deactivate** of already-inactive guidance, and **memory-settings reset** with no saved choice (a test pins "Idempotent"). They call the new `skip_audit_entry(request, because=...)` (`src/observability/audit_middleware.py`), which sets a flag on `request.state` that the middleware reads after the handler; it is per request and never hides a failure. Changing those three to 409 would have broken documented, tested behaviour for no gain. **Frontend**: the Overview kill-switch console now shows the API's message on a refused reset and refreshes the switches (`app/page.tsx`; previously a generic "Try again" and a stale "tripped" display if someone else had already reset it). The other call sites already display `ApiError` messages, and each button is only offered in the state where the action applies (Activate only on non-active versions, Reset only when the memory source is `database`, delete only when configured), so these refusals surface only on a double click or a state that changed underneath the operator. **Reviewed and deliberately left alone**: *revoke-sessions* with nothing to revoke (it answers `revoked_count: 0` honestly, and revoking is an incident-response step that did run); *chat abort* with nothing streaming (a fire-and-forget request flag, like the pipeline cancel's 202; `stream_assistant_reply` clears a stray flag at the start of the next message); *PUT-style saves* of an unchanged value (the save happened); *evaluation endpoints* that report an outcome in the body (paper tick, live `generate-intent`, the daily-signal runs, `kill-switch/check`: a tick or signal *was* processed, whether it produced a fill or an intent is the response); *compute-only POSTs* (`/audit/verify`, backtest compare / walk-forward / monte-carlo, gateway validate, go-live readiness, the LLM/notification connection tests): the request ran, no state transition is claimed; the inbound webhooks. Subscription enrolment and the other creates do create a row each call. **Verified**: `tests/test_noop_audit_sweep.py` (10 tests, each checking the *audit trail itself*, not only the status: kill switch refused and unaudited, and a second reset cannot overwrite who genuinely reset it; admin no-ops refused with the user's state exactly as the one real change left it and the trail holding only the real change, plus a test that real role changes and reactivations are still audited; prompt version refused with `activated_at` and the diff unchanged and exactly one `prompt_version.activated` row; the three deletes 404 with no audit row of either kind; guidance deactivated twice -> 200 both times, one row; the skip does not leak into the next request; autonomy repeated in both directions) and one added to `tests/test_memory.py` (a no-op reset is a 200 with no row, a real one is audited); 16 mutation checks (the middleware ignoring the flag, each refusal removed or turned into a 500, each skip removed, a repeat never recognised or always assumed), all caught; `ruff`, `mypy --strict` clean; 199 tests across every suite touching these endpoints passed; full backend suite 1390 passed, coverage 87.25%, all 29 per-module floors met. **Not done / worth knowing**: the sweep is by reading, not by a mechanical check -- a *new* endpoint can reintroduce the shape (a transition that answers 200 when nothing changed), and nothing fails if it does; the rule for reviewers is "a verb endpoint refuses (409/404) when its target state already holds, or calls `skip_audit_entry` if repeats are documented as 200". Cancel on a *pipeline run* and the orchestration run controls were fixed earlier; gateway rollback to the already-active version was not examined in depth (it re-applies the config through `apply_config_text`). The live stack is not rebuilt. **[Follow-up 2026-10-14: CI's mutation gate for `src/orchestration/kill_switch.py` failed on this change -- the new `KillSwitchNotTrippedError`'s `mode` and message were unchecked by that module's own test file (the gate runs only that file, not the route tests). Two orchestration-level tests were added and the gate is back at its baseline of 6. I had mutation-checked the new endpoint tests but not re-run this module's gate; after editing a module listed under `[[tool.tradingos.mutation.module]]`, run its gate.]**

- **Open engineering items closed: the nine follow-ups from the broker/audit work (2026-10-15/16).** Ten numbered notes below (memory has three parts). Verification, stated plainly: every change's own suites passed and each was mutation-checked; the full backend suite was started but stopped before it finished, so it was NOT run in full locally (CI runs it); Playwright was not run locally for these changes (CI runs it; two new specs were added but not yet executed).
  1. **Notification channel errors.** The Settings card's Disconnect and alert-level toggle had no error handling, so the 404 the audit sweep added (or any failure) was an unhandled rejection, and a failed toggle stayed ticked. Both now show the API's message, refresh the card, and revert the toggle. Playwright (`e2e/notification-channels.spec.ts`) forces the failures by intercepting the request.
  2. **The unidentified `ConnectTimeout`s -- probably found, and a real gap closed.** Not confirmed (the old logs are gone), but the only thing making ~1,900 outbound calls in that window was the Zerodha poll, and 4 timeouts in that many is a normal network-blip rate. Reading the adapters showed the real problem: a connect/read timeout, DNS failure or connection reset escaped as a bare `httpx` exception, which is not a `BrokerServerError`, so **the circuit breaker (which counts only that family) never saw a broker outage** -- an unreachable broker was called on every firing for ever, and the log line named neither broker nor call. Both adapters (and Zerodha's instruments download) now raise `BrokerServerError(503, "<broker> <METHOD> <path>: unreachable (<ExceptionType>: ...)")` via `broker_unreachable()`; tested end to end (three timeouts open the circuit and the fourth call never reaches the network).
  3. **Per-symbol back-off.** `publish_ticks_once` takes a `SymbolBackoff`: a symbol that fails is skipped for 30 s, then 60, up to 10 min; a success clears it; a *new tick source* (credentials changed) clears everything. Skipped symbols are still reported in `TickPublishError` (as `SymbolBackedOffError`, with the last reason) so the job keeps failing visibly in the run history, but the broker is no longer asked about a symbol that will not start working by itself (it was 1,200 calls an hour per such symbol). Failure and recovery are logged once, on the transition.
  4. **The broker's rejection is visible in Settings.** `token_status` is derived from the clock alone (the "expires around 06:00 IST" estimate), so the card said "Connected" while Zerodha refused every quote. New `src/brokers/feed_health` records a 401/403 the moment the tick source sees it and clears it when a call succeeds, when credentials are saved, deleted or re-authenticated (including the OAuth callback), and when the tick source is rebuilt. `GET /broker-credentials` reports `feed_rejection_status/detail`, `feed_rejected_at`; the card and the status strip show "Rejected by the broker -- reconnect". Process-local on purpose: a live signal the next market-hours poll re-establishes (a restart showing nothing means "not known yet", not "accepted"). Playwright (`e2e/broker-feed-rejection.spec.ts`) checks the rendering against a stubbed status response, since a real rejection needs a live broker.
  5. **Quote symbol format.** Per Kite's own docs `/quote` takes and returns `exchange:tradingsymbol` (`NSE:INFY`); the adapter sent and looked up the bare symbol, so a plain symbol could only ever find nothing. A bare symbol is now requested as `NSE:<symbol>`, a qualified one passes through, and the response is read by that key (the bare symbol is a tolerant fallback). Upstox's quotes endpoint takes `instrument_key` (the adapter sent `symbol`); its response object's own key format is not documented in what I could read, so a row is matched on the `instrument_token` it carries, with the key as given and the `|` to `:` form also tried. **Not verified against the live APIs** (only a valid token reaches them): this follows the documented formats. Index spot lookups (`NSE:NIFTY 50`) are still best-effort and may find nothing.
  6. **A mechanical guard for the no-op audit pattern.** `tests/test_audit_route_classification.py` discovers every mutating route (from the app's OpenAPI schema, cross-checked against each route module's router) and requires it to be classified: `NOT_A_TRANSITION`, `SET_VALUE`, `REQUEST_ACCEPTED`, `REFUSES_NOOP` (must name a test that exists) or `IDEMPOTENT_SKIPS_AUDIT` (its handler must call `skip_audit_entry`, and only such handlers may). A new endpoint fails until classified -- i.e. until someone decides what it does on a repeat. Shown to fail on each of: an unclassified route, a stale entry, a refusal naming a missing test, an idempotent handler that does not skip, and an undeclared handler that does. Writing the table exposed five refusals the sweep had called already correct that **had no test** (approval decided twice, strategy promoted twice, live eligibility from the wrong state, risk-limit confirm/apply twice, chat session deleted twice); `tests/test_noop_refusals.py` pins them. It guards the *process* (every route has an answer), not the answer's quality.
  7. **Mutation testing: `circuit_breaker`, `options_grounding`, and `expire_stale_intents`.** `circuit_breaker.py` 39 survivors to 6 (the real ones: the default threshold and cooldown, the exact cooldown boundary, the accessors the status API reads, the error types' attributes, the test double inside the module); the 6 left are a `TypeVar` name, an annotation, a `slots` flag and two mutants that crash the module at import (a `StrEnum` member set to `None`), which mutmut calls "suspicious". `options_grounding.py` 37 to 5, and the real finding was `continue` turned into `break` in both loops: an ungrounded leg, or a bought leg, would end the naked-options scan and **a naked sold leg after it would go unreported**; also the strictness of "further out of the money" and the exact reasons the risk gate shows. The 5 left cannot change behaviour (negating the loop variable over a symmetric range, rounding prices that are multiples of 0.1 to 3 places, two `slots` flags). `expire_stale_intents` was inside the 800-line `live_trading.py` and only hand-analysed; it is extracted to `src/orchestration/live_intent_expiry.py` (re-exported, so every import still works) with its own DB-light tests, and gates at **0** survivors (the `<=` boundary is killed by freezing the clock). All three are in the CI matrix (10 modules). Lesson re-learned: a gate run overlapped with other DB-backed tests failed its clean baseline ("no mutants generated"); never run them concurrently.
  8. **Memory.** (a) *Similarity floor*: recall returned the nearest few lessons however unrelated and the prompt called them "lessons from earlier similar runs". `MEMORY_RECALL_MIN_SCORE` (default **0.5**, `-1` disables) is applied in the Qdrant query itself and exposed as an optional `min_score` on `GET /memory/query`. The default is measured with nomic-embed-text on the host: a lesson about the same kind of strategy scored 0.60-0.69, a loosely related one 0.44-0.52, an unrelated one 0.31-0.49. Other models score on other scales (OpenAI's are lower): re-tune when `MEMORY_EMBEDDING_MODEL` changes. (b) *`sentence-transformers` provider*: a model run in the backend process (no server, no key, nothing leaves the machine). It needs the optional `local-embeddings` extra (PyTorch, several GB -- not in the default image or `dev`); without it the provider reports a reason naming the extra. Models are loaded once per process, encoding runs in a worker thread, `trust_remote_code` is False. Tested against a stub with the real package's surface; **a real model download and encode was not tested**. (c) *Provenance*: see below.
  9. **Data provenance (the part of 8c that needed a design).** `BacktestRun`, `PaperFill`, `Trade` and `TradeReviewFinding` carried no record of whether their figures rested on real or generated data -- and the review's commentary is folded into an LLM prompt (`strategy_suggestions.review_suggestion`), where "12 fills, win rate 58%" computed on a random-walk feed read exactly like a measured result. New `data_source` (`real` | `synthetic` | `supplied` | `mixed` | `unknown`, CHECK-constrained, migration `d1f5a8c3b7e2`; existing rows `unknown` because nobody can say). Ticks carry their source through the Redis stream (the mock walk is `synthetic`, a broker quote `real`); **a paper fill combines its tick's source with its order book's, and the book is always synthetic, so a fill on real ticks is `mixed`, not `real`**; one input nobody can vouch for makes the whole `unknown`; the manual tick endpoint and the backtest endpoint label caller input `supplied` (this system cannot vouch for bars a caller posts); a live trade is `real` (placed through a broker adapter, priced from its own quote). The review records the combined source and appends a plain caveat to its commentary unless the data is real. Exposed on the backtest, paper-fill and trade responses; the backtests page shows it. **Not done:** performance lessons for memory (which this makes possible but was not asked for), and tagging the data lake's own rows -- ingestion defaults to a fake provider, so a bars-from-the-lake backtest cannot yet be called real. Migration verified up, down and up again on a scratch database.
  10. **The `ubuntu-26.04` runner trial.** The label is generally available (GitHub's changelog) and `ubuntu-latest` starts moving to it on 2026-10-19. A throwaway branch off `origin/main` with every job on `ubuntu-26.04` (enabled by a `trial/**` push trigger in that branch's own workflow file) **passed all jobs**, including Playwright and the strategy-sandbox tests that depend on the AppArmor sysctl; the backend job took 19m 51s against 10-14 minutes on 24.04 (one sample; runs vary). Branch deleted. CI stays pinned to `ubuntu-24.04`: moving is now a tested, deliberate one-line-per-job change rather than a surprise, and 24.04 is not going away soon. I could not read job logs (they need a GitHub login), so the image version is inferred from the label resolving, not read from the run.
  **Verification**: new tests for every item (adapters and breaker end to end, tick feed back-off, feed health through the API, the classification guard, the refusals, the provenance vocabulary/streams/fills/review/endpoints/live trade, the floor at store/service/pipeline/endpoint level, the provider against a stub, the three mutation targets); mutation checks on each (all caught after tightening the tests that missed -- a few misses were test gaps, e.g. an unlabelled tick being indistinguishable from the mutation, or `real` and `supplied` both combining to `mixed` through a synthetic book). **Live stack: not rebuilt.**

- **CI runs only what a change can affect (2026-10-16).** Every push used to run the whole pipeline: the backend suite (10-15 min), Playwright, both image builds and ten mutation jobs (4-9 min each) -- about 20 jobs and well over an hour of runner time for a docs-only commit. A new `changes` job runs `backend/scripts/ci_plan.py` (stdlib, a pure `plan(changed_files, modules)` function plus a thin git/env wrapper) and the other jobs gate on its outputs. Rules: docs (`docs/`, `*.md`, `LICENSE`, `.gitignore`) run nothing but gitleaks; `backend/`, `config/` or `deploy/` runs the backend job; anything outside those (app, components, lib, `e2e/`, package manifests, Dockerfiles, root scripts) runs `tsc`; either runs Playwright; images build unless only `backend/tests/`, `e2e/` or `.github/` changed; the mutation matrix is built from `pyproject.toml` and holds only the modules whose source or own test file changed (all of them when `pyproject.toml` or `mutation_gate.py` changed). `images` runs when its needs passed **or were skipped** (`!cancelled()` plus explicit result checks), never when one failed. **When in doubt, everything runs**: a tag, a manual dispatch, the new weekly `schedule` (Mondays 03:17 UTC, which also catches drift nothing in the repo caused -- a fresh dependency advisory, a base-image change), a change to `ci.yml` or the planner, and any diff that cannot be read (new branch, rewritten base, no git). The step summary shows what ran and why. Two further changes: the hard-coded mutation module list in `ci.yml` is gone (it was a second copy of `pyproject.toml`'s; `test_mutation_gate.py` now asserts the workflow builds its matrix from the plan and has no list of its own), and a push to `main` is **no longer cancelled** by a newer push (only a PR's superseded run is) -- the plan diffs against the previous commit, so cancelling a run would let the next docs-only push skip jobs the cancelled one never finished. **Trade-off, stated plainly:** a skipped job does not re-check an old failure -- if the backend job failed on commit A, a docs-only commit B goes green; the weekly full run and any later code change are what bring it back. Tests: `tests/test_ci_plan.py` (27: every rule, the event handling, and a real `git diff` between two commits). Verified: the planner against this repo's real history and `actionlint` on the workflow; the first run on `main` (#45, a `ci.yml` change, so everything by design) passed all 17 jobs (5 plain, 10 mutation, 2 image) -- the dynamic mutation matrix expanded to the 10 modules and `images` ran after gated needs. A docs-only push (#46, 13 s total) ran gitleaks + `changes`, and backend, Playwright, `tsc`, mutation and images were all skipped.

- **Building images behind a TLS-intercepting antivirus/proxy (2026-10-16).** A rebuild of the live stack failed with `unable to verify the first certificate` / `CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED` from `pnpm build` and `pip install .`: Norton's Web/Mail Shield re-signs HTTPS with its own root, which the Windows store trusts but a build container does not (it had worked until then; the cause was not established -- Docker Desktop had just been restarted). Fixed without turning the shield off: `docker-compose.yml` declares an optional build secret `extra_ca` read from the `EXTRA_CA_PEM` environment variable, and the two `Dockerfile`s mount it (`RUN --mount=type=secret,id=extra_ca,required=false`) only for the steps that reach npm/PyPI -- `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` for pnpm, `PIP_CERT` (pip's own bundle plus the extra root) for pip. Unset, the Dockerfiles behave exactly as before (CI never sets it). A secret mount is not stored in a layer: checked that the built backend image's trust store has no Norton entry and its history no certificate. Use: export the root from the Windows store (`Cert:\CurrentUser\Root`, subject `Norton Web/Mail Shield Root`; public part only) to a PEM, then `EXTRA_CA_PEM="$(cat that.pem)" API_HOST_PORT=8001 node scripts/docker-up.js -d`. Not covered: the backend image's `apt`/`curl` step (it fetches the Postgres apt key over HTTPS) and the frontend's `apk add` do not use the secret -- they are cached, but a build that has to redo them behind the same shield would still fail. Verified on the live stack: rebuilt, both containers healthy, `alembic` at head `d1f5a8c3b7e2`, the frontend answers 200.
